A move arms the same focus return as a pointer Reload when focus was in the frame and names the new frame once in the live region; a second link before the new view shows keeps the first move's focus return and announcement.
  - Main judges it again by its own state, which is the authority because a canvas can forge the message: `parseGotoRequest` (`requests.ts`) takes exactly `{gen, goto}`, and `goto` accepts it only while the window is open, the phase is ready, the generation is current, the value is in the current run's listed frames and the canvas still resolves; anything else is refused silently, since the renderer has already judged and there is no reply path. The playing frame reloads (`reload`, no history entry, no refit). Another frame starts as a play does, with a new generation and iframe, a fit to its mark and a Back/Forward entry, but without `activate`: activation shows a hidden app and focuses the window, so a canvas calling `click()` on a timer could take focus from a person working in another app.
- **Timeout and kill.** The host page starts `PROTOTYPE_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` when it sets the iframe's `src`; a run with no result by then is a start failure. Nothing a run waits on goes through CDP, because a hung canvas process blocks child-session commands. Leaving a run that is still loading (another play, Reload, Back/Forward, its canvas gone, close) and a timeout kill that run's canvas frame process (`WebFrameMain.osProcessId`), never the host's or the main window's renderer: otherwise the next iframe reuses the hung process and times out too. The window's canvas iframes share one renderer process, so a kill also stops an earlier run still on screen; the view says so (`previousStopped`), and the host drops those iframes for main's latest still image. Main captures the iframe area whenever the state leaves a ready phase, before that step's effects, and sends it before the view it belongs to. The area is `prototypeCaptureRect` (header rows from `prototypeSideLayout` as 'Fit' gives them, the side and bottom margins, the visible box for the run's mark and the mode, less the open conversation panel and its 8 px gap at the stored width cut to the window's current range: the content panel is then `left − 16` wide, so the panel's text never lands in the still). The panel-relative box goes with the still (`PrototypeSnapshot.box`), and the renderer shows the still there unchanged; resending the latest still after a kill reuses the box it was captured with, so a later window resize or mode switch never moves it.
- **Isolation.** The window uses its own memory-only partition with the same permission, device and download denials as the default session. The partition exists for the kill: in the default session, killing a canvas frame's process also killed its host page, while in the partition the prototype's process is never the main window's canvas or the host page, and the main canvas stays responsive while a prototype hangs. The app-wide gates (`web-contents-created`: window navigation, the board's folder-key frame gate, new windows and `<webview>`) apply to this window too, and its own `will-frame-navigate` adds a stricter gate (`prototypeFrameNavigationAllowed`): the direct child frame may navigate only when the host's top frame started it and the URL is the current run's. The board's gate lets a canvas move to another document of its folder, which a prototype must not, so links, `location` changes, `location.reload()`, native form submits and download links are refused; same-document moves (hash, `pushState`) never reach the event. A nested iframe's own navigation keeps the board's rule, and the sandbox stops it from moving its parent. `allow-forms` is there because without it no `submit` event fires and a script's `preventDefault` handler never runs; the gate still refuses the native submission. Requests such as `fetch` keep the server's rules (CORS above).
- **Sender role.** The window's main frame is the sender role `prototype` (`senderKind` in `index.ts`, `senderAllowed.ts`): it calls its own requests, the conversation channels scoped to the playing canvas and a short list of composer channels (preset reads, attachments without capture, `shell:openLink`, the Agent usage read for the context ring's drawer). The main window's guard is not widened (`mainOnly` gains only the main window's play, its two drawing-relay requests and the playing-frame read `prototype:playing`; other handlers that check `fromWindow` themselves stay main-only). Each request parser checks the role again. Canvas iframes have no preload, so a canvas that escaped into the window's page still reaches only the playing canvas's conversations: no project conversation, other canvas's conversation, canvas deletion, account identity or setting besides Last used. Channels: §7 'Prototype window IPC'. The scope (`prototype/conversationScope.ts`, Electron-free and ajv-free because the renderer imports its types):
  - Main judges each conversation request against the target and the conversation index as they are when it arrives, so one sent before another play is judged by the new target. A conversation id must be of the playing root and resolve to the playing canvas's listed row (exactly one row, the service's own admission rule); `models` and `skills` need the playing root, `list` names the canvas the window shows and is answered only when that is the playing canvas, `resumeTarget` and `start` (`new` and `resume` only) the playing canvas, `delete`, like every by-id method, only a conversation of the playing canvas, and `newDesign` is refused. While the target's row is being read, gone or shares its ID, everything is refused rather than guessed; the run's phase is not looked at, so a lost frame or a failed load leaves the panel usable. `list` answers only the playing canvas's rows; for another canvas it answers `not_playing`, which the window does not show as a failure and reads again on its next view. The view can trail main's play behind a still-image capture, and a list of the new canvas applied to the canvas still on screen would read that canvas's conversations as deleted and drop their drafts; the window also ignores a read started before its last canvas switch, so A → B → A cannot apply the first A read. Root and canvas are compared together because worktrees share canvas IDs and paths (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01).
  - Conversation broadcasts go to the prototype window only when they concern the playing canvas, matched by root and canvas key rather than the listed row, so an event is not lost while the root's list is read again. Events stream many times a second, so the filter keeps the index in memory until a list change or a bind, and reads it again for an id it does not know.
  - The remembered conversation (`prototype:rememberedConversation`, `prototypeRememberedConversation` in `prototype/conversationScope.ts`): the window asks, with no argument, for the one id ui-state's `conversationByCanvas` holds for the canvas main plays now (§7 'Navigation persistence and IPC'), taken from the main window's last accepted `ui-state:save` of this run, else from `ui-state.json` as the previous run left it (no root-spelling migration on that raw read, so a root renamed since reads as nothing remembered until the main window saves). It is null while nothing plays, the canvas or root is not listed, or the canvas's ID is listed twice. Only that id leaves ui-state, and the window never writes ui-state, because main replaces the whole file on each save and two writers would overwrite each other. An answer that arrives after main moved to another canvas can name that canvas's conversation; the pick takes only an id listed on the window's own canvas.
  - Attachments (`attachmentHandlers.ts`): an id belongs to the draft of the window holding it (the one it was issued to, or the one that took a returned queue back), and once a start, send or queue carrying it is accepted it is the conversation's. The prototype role may use only ids it holds now, so it can neither break the main window's draft nor take a sent id back. A closed window removes only the drafts it still holds, because a queued message rechecks its ids when it goes out. Owners are keyed by the window's webContents id, so a late reply to a closed window is never the next window's. The Attach dialog opens over the window that asked.
  - Narrow reads stand in for the main window's: provider readiness with every account's identity emptied, delivered under the main preload's `onAgentAuth` name; the terminal work of the session the playing canvas would resume, in any project, since that session holds the choice wherever it works; the New conversations default, Last used and buddy without Home's last project; the playing root's `FolderEntry` (canvas rows, no conversations); and Agent usage (`agent-usage:read` and `agent-usage:changed`), the same `UsageEntry[]` the main window gets, which carries no connection ID, profile or account (§7 'Agent usage'), so no account identity crosses. A composer pick goes to main, which hands it to the main window to remember as its own: main writing ui-state itself would be lost to the main window's next whole-file save.
  - Toward the main window only a checked reveal goes (`prototypeRevealRequest`: a chip or answer chip of a playing-canvas conversation, Settings, a conversation or a listed canvas of the playing root). Main shows and focuses the main window, which decides what it can show now (`renderer/revealDecision.ts`) and cancels nothing.
  - Id-only relays: the drawing project conversation is computed by the main window's renderer, which reads project conversations the prototype role cannot, and reported with the target's canvas generation, so a report made for an earlier play of the same canvas (A → B → A) is refused; a promotion is main's own `conversation.bound` for the playing canvas (`drawing.ts`, `promoted.ts`, `targetRelay.ts`). Each holds only until the playing canvas changes, even back to the same one, or the window closes. The prototype window gets ids, never a project conversation's content, and opens them only through a reveal.
  - Notifications use the conversation the open panel reports (`showing.ts`) only when it is in scope when reported, only within the same canvas generation, and only while the window is focused, shown and its panel open.
- **Reload focus guard** (`renderer/prototype/focusGuard.ts`): a reloaded canvas's own `focus()` or `autofocus` pulls focus into its iframe, and the next keys, IME composition included, go to the frame. Neither `inert` on the iframe nor `allow="focus-without-user-activation 'none'"` stops a frame's own `focus()`, and the host sees focus leaving for a cross-origin iframe only as a `focusout` with no `relatedTarget`; it then checks one task later whether that iframe holds focus and gives it back to the column element. The host cannot tell the frame's `focus()` from the person's click into the frame, so it does this only for a run whose iframe was created while focus was in the column, and only until `FOCUS_GUARD_MS` (1 s) after that run is ready and shown; without the limit a click into the frame went back to the composer.
- **Read state.** The window's debugger only injects. It never feeds `DocumentBindings` or `seenProof.ts`, which take the main window's debugger and its main frame's direct child only, and the server's response records prove nothing without a main-window loader, so the server needs no change. A canvas played only in this window, or reloaded there after a change, is never recorded as seen (§7 'Seen canvases').
- **Fit** (`prototype/fit.ts`): the window's content is the mark plus the window chrome, at least `PROTOTYPE_WINDOW_MIN_WIDTH` wide and `PROTOTYPE_WINDOW_MIN_HEIGHT` high, and inside the work area unless the work area is smaller than those minimums. A new window is centred on the main window's display; the opening run's refit keeps the window's top-left on its own display and moves only to stay inside. Nothing else fits the window: a later frame keeps its bounds, header rows follow the width the person left, and capture reads the content size as it is. The chrome is the side and bottom margins and the header (`PROTOTYPE_CHROME`), plus `PROTOTYPE_NAME_ROW_HEIGHT` when `prototypeHeaderRows` puts the name on a second row because fewer than `PROTOTYPE_NAME_MIN_WIDTH` px remain for the name after reserving both header drag gaps. `prototypeSideLayout` gives the rows from two given widths: with the conversation panel closed, the window's width W and the right group `PROTOTYPE_HEADER_RIGHT`; with it open, the area left of the panel, L = W − P − 8, and `PROTOTYPE_HEADER_RIGHT_OPEN`, because the header's conversation button leaves the group. Nothing measured feeds them, so the fit (from the fitted, work-area-clipped width and panel), the capture and the renderer's layout agree. The two R values are mirrored by `--pw-right` in `styles.css`, on `:root` and on the open layout (`.pw[data-panel="open"]`), pinned by `WindowShell.test.tsx`; a feature that adds a header control changes both constants and both mirrors. The header's conversation button is `hidden` (`.pw-conv[hidden] { display: none; }`) while the panel is open, an exception to keeping a focus-return target's box (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-03): it must take no Tab stop and no box while R is `PROTOTYPE_HEADER_RIGHT_OPEN`, so the renderer gives focus back to it on close only after the view has removed `hidden`. The minimum height is the window's content, not the content panel's, so it stays one constant whatever the header's rows and the conversation panel: the host passes it as `minHeight` and with every `setMinimumSize`. A shorter frame sits centred in the taller content panel by `prototypeViewportBox` in Fit and fills it in Responsive.
  - The conversation panel's widths and fits are `prototypePanelBounds`, `openPanelResize` and `prototypePanelFit` (the rules and widths themselves are PRODUCT §5 item 2 'Prototype window'; the constants are in `fit.ts`). `prototypePanelFit` takes the name's row from the fitted L. Below a `PROTOTYPE_PANEL_WINDOW_MIN_WIDTH` window the minimum and maximum widths cross and the panel keeps its minimum with no range, which is why the shipped `panelBounds` (where the maximum wins) is not used. A widened window is moved left to stay inside, because macOS does not move a widened window back into the work area, and the open minimum is clamped to the work area's width, because macOS keeps a window as wide as its minimum even past the work area. Main cuts every width the renderer sends to the window as it is then, since the request may predate a shrink.
  - Resize order (`prototypeWindow.ts` `resize`): a rising minimum is set after the bounds, because a minimum alone does not widen the window; a falling minimum is set before them, or narrower bounds are held at the old minimum. Closing the panel only lowers the minimum and keeps the window's size.
- **Export** (`createPrototypeExport` in `canvasExport.ts`, `controller.exportTarget`): the view carries the target's root and canvas row path (the last listed path while the canvas is gone), so the window's renderer holds the project's absolute root; the canvas iframe is cross-origin and never reaches it. Export sends the target the view showed at the click, and main exports only when it is still the one playing, compared through main's own list (a canvas ID two rows now share resolves to nothing): otherwise it refuses `changed`, or `notListed` when nothing plays that main can name, so a stale click never exports another frame. Main then opens the save panel as a sheet of the prototype window, with the default name `<canvas file stem>-<frame id>.html` (`/`, `:` and control characters become `-`), and runs `doan export <path> --frame=<id> --prototype -o <file> --force --repo <root> --json`; the frame goes in the flag's own argument, because an ID such as `--` or `-o` would otherwise read as a separator or a flag. A success without `prototype: true` counts as failed. The file holds the playing frame's link group as the CLI judges it from the file, not the window's frame list ('Exported canvas and viewer' 'Prototype page'), and the success toast counts its other frames from `linkedFrames` (`interpretExport`; a value that is not a count reads as 0, since the file is written). The instance's lock is one per window, not per canvas (key `window`), held from the click through the save panel and the CLI run; nothing waits on the window, so a window closed during a run still gets its file written, and a reopened window's Export is refused as `exporting` until that run ends. Its toasts pause while the window is not shown natively: main pushes the window's visibility on the window's `show`, `hide`, `minimize` and `restore` and on AppKit's hide and unhide, because `document.hidden` misses a minimized window and a hidden app.
- **Menu.** The window reports focus and blur, and main rebuilds the app menu with `menuState`'s `prototypeFocused`, which turns off every item acting on the main window's canvas, conversation, view or navigation (§7 'App menu'). File > Close Window closes the focused prototype window instead of the main one.

### Exported canvas and viewer

Product rules: PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Export and viewer'. The code is `cli/internal/export/` (pipeline), `cli/internal/export/viewer/` (the pages, embedded with `go:embed`), `app/scripts/viewerInspect.mjs` (the viewer's generated scripts) and `cli/internal/cli/export.go` (the command, §12). A prototype export (`--frame <id> --prototype`) runs the same pipeline and differs only in the page around the canvas ('Prototype page' below).

- **The serving scope is the export boundary.** Export resolves every local reference in the app server's order (real path, inside the repo, inside `repo.Scope`, regular file; §13 'Paths inside the repo') through one `repo.Resolver` per export and `repo.Scope`, so a file is included exactly when the app could serve it, and `.doan/config.json` and other `.doan/` metadata are refused as out of scope. Files are read by `export.ReadFile`: each segment is opened descriptor-relative with `O_NOFOLLOW`, the counterpart of the server's `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY`, and the size check uses `fstat` on the same descriptor. Never widen this with a path of its own.
- **Custom property `url()`s resolve from their user** (`cli/internal/canvas/custom.go`, `cli/internal/export/customurl.go`). Chromium (Electron 45) resolves a relative `url()` in an unregistered custom property against the stylesheet whose `var()` puts it into a real property, through any chain of custom properties, not where it is declared; the canvas's `<style>` and `style` attributes count as the canvas's folder. Users in different folders each show their own file, which one data URL per declaration cannot reproduce. `canvas.CustomUsers` records which folders use each property. A rule counts only when the rightmost compound of one of its selectors asks for class, id and type names the canvas's elements have (`canvas.Elements`: lowercased, elements inside `<template>` included), because the app never applies any other rule; nothing else about selectors or the cascade is checked, so a rule whose element never inherits the property still counts. Whatever this cannot judge statically counts, so export never embeds one file where the app shows another: every rule in a canvas with a `<script>`, an event handler attribute, a `javascript:` URL, a `contenteditable` attribute (typing Enter inserts a `<div>`) or an SVG `set`/`animate` of `class` or `id`, or whose style text declares `-webkit-user-modify` or any property with an escaped name; a selector with an escape, a namespace or a comment; rules inside at-rules other than `@media`, `@supports`, `@layer`, `@container`, `@scope` and `@starting-style`; and style attributes. Pseudo-classes, pseudo-elements, attribute selectors and `&` add no requirement. Export builds it from the canvas and every stylesheet it reaches before rewriting, and `doan check` from its own reach, which includes export's, so a canvas the check leaves quiet is never refused for this. When those folders resolve the URL to two or more different repo paths (`Ambiguous`), export refuses with `ReasonAmbiguous` whether or not the files exist, and check warns `ambiguous_custom_url`. Otherwise the one path goes through the serving-scope resolution above: an existing served file is inlined, a refused one refuses with its reason, and a missing one refuses as a missing direct reference when the canvas declares it. A property nothing uses resolves where it is declared and a missing file there blocks nothing. A property registered by `@property` with a syntax accepting `<url>`, `inherits` and `initial-value` also resolves where it is declared; a rule missing any of them is dropped by the browser and does not count. The descriptors' values are not checked, so a rule the browser drops for a bad value still counts as registered. Properties registered from JavaScript keep the declaring file as base.
- **`<base href>` is refused, not followed** (`cli/internal/export/base.go`). The app resolves the canvas's relative references from its first `<base href>` outside `<template>`; export, like `doan check`, resolves from the canvas's folder. So when that base moves the folder (`baseMoves`: after dropping ASCII tab, LF and CR, trimming, reading `%2E` as `.` and cutting query and fragment, anything but empty, `.`, `./` or one same-folder file name), every canvas-level local reference export would include (`<img>`, `<link rel=stylesheet>`, `<script src>`, `url()` and `@import` in `<style>` and `style` attributes, and custom property `url()`s the canvas itself uses) is refused with `ReasonBase` before it is resolved, and no stylesheet is followed from it. Local frames are still `ReasonFrame`, since classification comes first; a custom property `url()` nothing uses stays as written. Every `<base>` outside `<template>` loses its `href` in the result, and the element goes when no attribute is left: every reference is a data URI, so the base has no use (a leftover base did not break fragment references in Chromium, WebKit or Electron 45). A canvas whose base does not move exports byte for byte as without the base element. `<base>` inside inline `<svg>` or `<math>` also counts (the tokenizer does not track namespaces), which can refuse more than needed.
- **`<template>` depth.** Every tokenizer walker (`firstBaseHref`, and `rewriteHTML`, which `collectUses` runs through) counts `<template>` through `templateTag`: a start or self-closing tag opens one at any depth, as the HTML parser does, and an end tag closes one. A walker that leaves a template early reads inert content as document content and can resolve an active `url()` from the wrong file (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-09).
- **Rewriting** changes only what it must. `rewriteHTML` re-serializes the start tags it changed and copies every other byte; CSS references come from `canvas.CSSRefs`, a lexer that returns spans and shares `ScanCSS`'s rules (`cli/internal/canvas/css_refs_test.go` `TestCSSRefsMatchesScanCSS` pins that they agree, and `css_golden_test.go` pins all three scanners' results). The lexer hands out one token at a time and keeps only the tokens since the last `{`, `}` or `;` outside parentheses, so memory follows the longest statement, not the stylesheet; text with no such boundary is still held whole. `@import` is followed with an ancestor stack (cycles) and a cache keyed by which stylesheets the result reached (diamonds). A data URI is percent-encoded so it is safe in an HTML attribute, `srcset`, a CSS `url("…")` and a `<style>` element alike, and `<` in rewritten CSS is written `\3c ` so inlined text cannot close the `<style>` it sits in.
- **Memory.** Large outputs are allocated once at their final size: a rewritten stylesheet's replacements are gathered before its builder grows, a data URI's percent-encoded size is counted first, rewritten tags are written straight into the output buffer, which grows by the tag and the rest of the source when it must, and `Assemble` sizes the page before escaping the canvas into it. The output bytes are pinned by `TestExportOutputIsPinned` and `TestAssembleOutputIsPinned`, and `TestExportAllocationIsBounded` caps the allocation of a 4 MiB stylesheet export at 10.5 times its size; undoing any one of these copy cuts exceeds it.
- **Size limit.** The 50 MiB cap (`MaxBytes`) stops the rewrite early: a file's `fstat` size is checked before it is read and the length of what is built is counted while rewriting, because `@import` diamonds and cycles can multiply a small input. The assembled page is checked again, since the viewer and the `srcdoc` escape add bytes. The board's play and measurement, the player and the prototype export data add about 148 KB to every general Export (measured on the same near-empty canvas, 207,191 → 355,648 bytes), plus about 100 bytes per exportable frame and link group; a prototype file is always smaller than the general Export it comes from, so the player refuses nothing for size.
- **SVG `<use>` is refused unless it points into the canvas** (`refuseUses` in `cli/internal/export/inline.go`). The app draws no `<use>` whose reference is another file, an external URL or `data:`: the canvas's opaque origin blocks the load before any request is made, and an exported file behaves the same in Chromium and WebKit. Copying the sprite into the file would show icons the app leaves blank, and the sprite's `<style>` would then style the whole canvas (scoping it with a `:where()` prefix or `@scope` reached the `<use>` clone only in WebKit). So when the source contains `<use`, export takes the references from `canvas.Parse` (`UseRefs`: SVG `<use>` outside `<template>`, `href` before xlink `href`, the list `svg_use_not_inline` reads) and refuses each one that is not a fragment with `ReasonUseExternal` before resolving anything, `<base>` included; the tokenizer and the parser can disagree on malformed markup, so the parser's list decides. Fragment references stay as written. `<image>` and `feImage` keep data: URLs.
- **Page.** `Assemble` replaces the placeholders in `shell.html` in one pass (`fillShell`, pinned equal to a `strings.Replacer` by `TestFillShellMatchesAReplacer`), so a canvas or a name that contains one is never expanded. The canvas goes into `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts" srcdoc>`, so it runs in an opaque origin as in the app (§3). **There is no CSP meta on purpose:** a `srcdoc` document inherits its parent's CSP, so a restrictive one would block the canvas's own CDN scripts and fonts; the shell's code is inline and makes no request.
  - **Project crumb.** `assemble` fills the project name, its initial and its hue class into static HTML, escaped, so they show without JavaScript. The name comes from `exportProjectName` (`cli/internal/cli/export.go`): the folder of `repo.ReadGit`'s `Main`, else the export root's folder. Go `projectHue` and `projectInitial` (`cli/internal/export/project_thumb.go`) must equal the renderer's (`shellNavigation.ts`); both languages' tests read `schema/fixtures/project-thumb/cases.json` (§11). The viewer copies the app’s breadcrumb layout and shrink rules. The named container measures the content box, so each breakpoint subtracts the header’s two 16px insets. The viewer shares the 688, 568, 488 and 408px queries and the app adds 628px for the worktree switch. The viewer applies the hide rules under `.js` so its static preview keeps both text labels. `exportViewer.test.ts` checks the shared declarations and the rendered header at the boundaries.
  - The measure script (`lib.js` + `measure.js`) is injected inside a function after `<head>`, else after `<html>`, else after the doctype, and a leading byte order mark is dropped: a script before the doctype, or a BOM left as text, puts a standards-mode canvas into quirks mode. The function wrapper keeps `lib.js`'s top-level names from becoming globals a canvas could overwrite or be shadowed by.
  - **Navigation.** The canvas is a `srcdoc` document here, unlike in the app, so `measure.js` takes the prototype page's History retry and its `#` link handling ('Prototype page' 'Navigation' below): a History call with a URL is made again without it, and a click on a link written `#…` that the canvas did not cancel sets `location.hash`. Without them WebKit loads the viewer page into the canvas frame, Chromium drops the click, and both throw `SecurityError` on `pushState` (observed in exported files). Links to other documents are left as they are and reported under `links`. The copied text is pinned to `isolate.js` by `exportViewer.test.ts`.
  - Parts are copies of the app's canvas view (height and scroll functions, zoom and Fit values, message limits, tool keys, panning, the toolbar's Select and Hand items, panel and toolbar CSS values, the MIME table), pinned pair by pair by `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`, which fails when the app side changes and the copy does not. The frame list and zoom keep the app's class names so a `/design-sync` measurement maps one to one. The `layout` message adds `total` (all frames) for the note on frames past the first 1,000.
  - **Panel corners.** The panel clips square and an `::after` cover paints `--bg-app` outside its 24px corners, because a four-equal-radius clip over the iframe cuts large canvases (§3 'Canvas view'). The cover assumes the page behind the panel is plain `--bg-app`, and with `pointer-events: none` a press in a corner outside the curve reaches the board or header under it.
  - **Tools.** The toolbar sits in the panel outside the scrolling `#board`, which the panel's `overflow: clip` keeps from scrolling it away (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-02). The drag surface is `#board` itself, not a separate layer: while `layerShown` (Hand, a held Space or a drag in progress, and only once the canvas is drawn) `#board` gets `data-pan` and CSS gives the iframe `pointer-events: none`, so pointer and wheel reach `#board` and the native scroll (with inertia) and the existing ⌘/Ctrl-wheel listener apply unchanged. A drag moves `scrollLeft/Top` by the pointer's travel within the scroll range (`panScroll`), carrying the fraction Safari rounds away, and ends on `pointerup`, `pointercancel` or `lostpointercapture`; V, H, Esc and a Space release during a drag apply after it. For the cursor under a resting pointer (§3 'Canvas view' Input), `syncSurface` posts `{type: "pan", pan}` to the canvas on each change of `data-pan`, and again after the iframe's `load`; `measure.js` adopts the same `grab` sheet, and the end of a pan flips the iframe's `cursor` two frames later as the app does.
  - **Key forwarding.** The page decides tool keys with the copied `toolShortcut` on document keydown/keyup (bubble), so a menu that `preventDefault`s a key keeps it, and an open frame drawer or zoom menu also keeps V and H (`popupKey`). With focus in the canvas, `measure.js` forwards only Space down/up, V, H and an Escape not taken by the canvas or a text field, as `key` messages that `parseMessage` validates; `acceptMessage` stays the one listener. ⇧1 and ⇧0 are not forwarded. Space never sticks: every Space keyup sends a Space-up, the page accepts a forwarded Space-down only while `document.activeElement` is the iframe (the canvas's own scripts can post anything), `measure.js` releases on its blur and pagehide, and the page releases on window blur when `document.hasFocus()` is false and on the iframe's `load`. Esc returns Hand to Select only when nothing took it (`escapeSelects`); a page Esc that closes the tooltip or a menu is spent, as with Radix in the app, while a forwarded Esc goes straight to Hand → Select. The app differs: it replays a forwarded Esc on its document, so an open app layer takes it first (§3 'Actions and focus').
  - **Inspector** (PRODUCT §5 item 4 'Inspector'). The canvas side is `inspect.js` (generated) and `inspectSelection.js`, placed between `lib.js` and `measure.js` inside the measure script's function; the shell side is `inspectPanel.js` (generated), `inspectMessage.js` and `inspectView.js`, between `lib.js` and `shell.js` inside the shell's function (`assemble.go`). The shell owns inspector state. Select selects whether the column is open, folded or yielded (PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Export and viewer'), so `inspect-mode` always opens selection and its `styles` flag says only whether rows and the element tree are read, and an empty sandboxed sibling iframe exists in every inspector state, removed only while the board pans. The canvas can read its named WindowProxy across origins as soon as the mode changes, so an immediate click follows Select while Option-click still reaches the original control. A press keeps that window's identity. Folding, opening, yielding, panning, navigating or clearing the board invalidates it. The shell accepts replies only for its current epoch and acknowledges the first inspection, a selection-only reply with no rows included, before the canvas applies clicks queued for that opening (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-20). Selection, hover and text-drag handlers are written for the viewer, because the app's are tied to its seq and anchor protocol; the selection steps, readers and rows are generated.
    - **Generated from the app's sources.** `app/scripts/viewerInspect.mjs` bundles, with the `rolldown` already installed, `inspect.js` from `app/src/main/cssomMatched.ts` (`inspectElement`) and `canvasScript.ts` (`frameStep`, `escapeTarget`, `elementSelector`, `visibleRect`, the element tree and text-drag helpers, and DOM readers it lifts out of `canvasScript`'s injected function by AST), and `inspectPanel.js` from `inspectorRows.ts` (`rowDisplay`, `rowCopyText`, `rowTooltip`), `treeScrollLeft.ts` and the inspector bounds, clamping and key steps in `rightPanelSize.ts`. It fails when a lifted declaration is missing or refers to a closure name it did not lift, and `--check` (a verify step) fails when the committed files differ from the sources. `cssomMatched.ts` is imported by no app code; it is the generator's input, not dead code. The hand-copied `lib.js` stays as it is.
    - **Width.** The shell measures the content panel inside the window margins. One CSS width sizes the inspector grid track and centres the toolbar over the remaining board. During a drag the separator captures its pointer and a cover keeps the iframe from taking it. Only a completed width change writes the preference to browser-local storage. If storage is unavailable, the shell keeps the width in memory until the file closes. A resize of the window only changes the displayed width.
    - **Values from the CSSOM.** A browser has no CDP, so `cssomMatched.ts` rebuilds CDP-shaped matched styles from the CSSOM (cascade order, specificity with `:is`/`:where`/`:has`, `@scope` boundaries and proximity, `@layer`, `@media`/`@container`/`@supports`, nesting, inline and presentational styles, inherited properties) and feeds the app's own `inspectRows`. WebKit refuses `cssRules` of an `@import`ed `data:` sheet, so the collector decodes its text and parses it again with `replaceSync`. Where the CSSOM cannot give the app's source (a longhand after a `var()` shorthand in the same rule, which the CSSOM leaves empty, and any value an unreadable cross-origin sheet changes), that row falls back to the applied value with no token. To find the latter, each inspection disables the unreadable sheets for a moment and compares computed values, with a `transition-duration`/`-delay: 0s` guard and every CSS animation's time, play state and rate restored afterwards; without constructable stylesheets (Safari before 16.4) every row is the applied value. Known limits: while the sheets are off, a transition in flight whose declaration or end value comes from an unreadable sheet is cut (Chromium jumps to the end, WebKit restarts it), and each inspection fires `animationcancel` (WebKit also `animationstart`) and replaces the canvas's `Animation` objects although the motion continues.
    - **Inspector messages are untrusted too.** The canvas's own scripts share the frame and can post any inspector message. `inspectMessage.js` validates shape, row count and every string length against caps that a test reads from the producers' sources (`VALUE_LIMIT`, the element tree's and text copy's limits) and checks seq and epoch; the view renders only through `textContent`. A press or a drag in progress counts only while `document.activeElement` is the canvas iframe (as the forwarded Space-down), and the shell ends a drag itself on its own `pointerup`, `blur` or focus elsewhere, so a forged message can neither close the person's menu nor hold V, H and Space. Copy uses `navigator.clipboard.writeText` with an `execCommand('copy')` fallback and reports in the panel's `aria-live` line.
    - **Size.** The original inspector added about 83 KB per viewer page, measured on the same near-empty canvas. The pinned output tests make every byte change explicit; the prototype page does not include it.
  - **Play, size bubble and measurement on the board** (PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Export and viewer'). The `layout` message's frames carry the app's `prototype`, `parent` and `cut`, and the message its `gotos`, under `canvasMessage.ts`'s caps (`measure.js` reports, `lib.js` `parseLayout` validates, `exportViewer.test.ts` compares both with the app's script on the same document). Play is judged only from that live layout (the first 1,000 frames), never from the file's `meta.prototype`, which only the player's export reads. The size bubble sits outside the `aria-hidden` `#inspect-marks`, so its Play can take focus; the measurement lines are `#measure`, hidden from assistive technology.
  - **Generated app judgments** (`viewerApp.js`, global `viewerApp`). `viewerInspect.mjs`'s third entry bundles the app's own Play, drawer, size bubble, measurement, history, fit, run script, run report, view and toast rules (the module list is the entry in the generator), so the browser and the app cannot drift; it is never edited by hand, `--check` catches drift, and the generator fails when a bundle still imports a module, because a leftover import (a `node:` built-in kept external, since `requests.ts` shares a file with Node-only code) would be an undefined global that stops the whole page script. It treeshakes with `moduleSideEffects: false`, which leaves `inspect.js` and `inspectPanel.js` byte for byte as before.
  - **Prototype export data** (`meta.prototype` in `doan-meta`, `frameExports` in `assemble.go`). Go judges and pre-renders what a prototype file of each frame needs, so the browser never re-implements its escaping or judgment: `frames` (`id`, `name` as escaped NAME, `start` as META's JSON value, `group`, and `mark`, the raw mark the player sizes its run by until the run reports), `refused` (`unmarked` or `shared`, once per id; an id in neither list is a frame a script made, and `shared` is refused with the script-made sentence, as the app reads the CLI's `frame_not_found`), `groups` (each link group's frames JSON, never cut), `page` (prototype.html cut at NAME, the META values and SRCDOC), `isolate`, `measure` (the measure script's offset and length in the UTF-16 string the browser reads, counting WHATWG UTF-8 replacement and CR LF normalization) and `external` (the CLI's internet URL count, for the toast). The browser only joins `page[0] + name + page[1] + start + page[2] + group + page[3] + escape(canvas) + page[4]`, the canvas being the board's `srcdoc` with the measure range replaced by `isolate`; its one escape (`&` and `"`) is held to Go's by the shared table `cli/internal/export/testdata/srcdoc-escape.json`. It refuses as broken data that does not hold together (wrong shapes, a group without its frame, a range that is no longer the measure script) rather than write a file that would not play, and looks ids up in `Map`s, since `__proto__` or `constructor` as object keys would find `Object.prototype`. For an LF-only, valid UTF-8 canvas the result is byte for byte the CLI's file; otherwise it parses the same. The data adds nothing to the prototype file.
  - **Player window** (`player.js`; PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Export and viewer'). The player is the same file opened again at `#doan-play=<encodeURIComponent(id)>`: it alone kept history entries, reload, a closed board and DOM separation working in Chromium and WebKit (`about:blank` with a written document shares the board's origin and records no entries, a `blob:` URL loses its hash moves in Chromium, `data:` does not load, and `noopener` loses reuse and blocked-window detection). Boundaries someone could otherwise undo:
    - **The guard before the board iframe.** An inline script just before `#canvas` in `shell.html` marks a `#doan-play=` page and, with a `MutationObserver`, takes the board iframe's `srcdoc` and removes the iframe as the parser inserts it, before it loads; the shell script then runs `startPlayer()` (`lib.js`, `viewerApp.js` and `player.js` share one function with the board's files, which run only when it returns false). Removing the iframe later let the board canvas run inside a popup player in Chromium (2 of 20 opens).
    - **The run iframe** is `sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms"` with no `allow-same-origin`, a new iframe for every run and Retry, with `name` the frame id; its `srcdoc` is the board's with the measure range replaced by `isolate.js` first and then the app's run script (`prototypeScriptSource`, generated), which reports the mark, size, frame list and `gotos` the app's window uses. Link moves follow only `isolate.js`'s `{type: "doan-prototype", goto}`, and messages count only from the current run's `contentWindow` with origin `"null"`.
    - **Strings only between board and player**, checked by `event.source` (`file:` origins are all `"null"`): the board sends `play:<id>` and `mode:<fit|responsive>` to the window it opened, the player sends `playing:<id>` and `mode:<…>` to `window.opener` (`boardMessage`, `playerMessage`). The board never judges whether the frame is the one playing, since it does not see the player's link moves; the player keeps or restarts. No function or object crosses, and the player's code runs in its own realm.
    - **`window.open` only from the viewer's own button handlers.** `playPrototype` is the one caller, reached only from the toolbar's, bubble's and Play drawer's `click` and `keydown` handlers, never from a canvas message: a click inside the board's canvas iframe lends the board user activation (and `MessageEvent.isTrusted`), so a message handler could open windows the person never asked for. `null` is a blocked window.
    - **A unique window name per board page** (`doan-play-` and 32 random hex digits), so a reloaded board, a board opened again and another file each open their own player. There are no window features: the browser chooses tab or window. The board polls `closed` every 250 ms and, with no blur 250 ms after `focus()`, assumes the window did not come forward.
    - **History.** Each frame change sets `location.hash`, one entry, and every entry's `history.state.doanPlayer` holds the whole history and mode, so a reload restores both. In WebKit the player, linked to its opener, runs in the board's WebContent process, and that process loses file:// read access when the board's tab closes, so a reload after that fails (Safari shows “Cannot open file”); clearing `window.opener`, opening `about:blank` and then navigating, and replacing the URL all fail the same way, only `noopener` avoids it (at the cost above), and a ⌘R key handler cannot help because Safari reloads before the page sees the key. Chromium is not affected. Back and Forward at the end are `aria-disabled` with a click guard, never native `disabled`, so focus stays.
  - Left out: picking, anchors, Reload and conversation. Geist is not embedded (about 93 KB of base64 per export); the system font stack in the tokens applies. The frame drawer is built when it opens and is not updated while open. A Safari pinch gesture is not handled, only ⌘/Ctrl-wheel.
- **Prototype page** (`AssemblePrototype`, `viewer/prototype.html`, `prototype.css`, `prototype.js`, `isolate.js`). It shares `Assemble`'s one-pass placeholder filling (`fillPage`), `srcdoc` escaping, script placement and byte order mark removal; only the shell differs. The canvas export's output is pinned by its own tests (`TestExportOutputIsPinned`, `TestAssembleOutputIsPinned`), so the prototype path cannot change it. The start frame was judged from the file before (§4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6'), and the CLI adds the other frames of its link group (`canvas.LinkGroups`, convention 15) in document order: a frame with no links makes a group of one, and a group is never cut. The page does not read the mark again, and its size plays no part in the file.
  - **The whole canvas goes in once**, as for the viewer, and only the screen hides the rest: cutting the frame out loses listeners and ancestor selectors, as in the prototype window ('Prototype window' 'Rendering in place'). The rewritten canvas, with `isolate.js` first in its head, is the `data-srcdoc` attribute of a hidden holder, escaped as the viewer's `srcdoc` (`&` and `"` only), so it costs the same bytes and the 50 MiB limit applies to the page as before; `prototype.js` reads it once and gives every iframe that string. A JSON string would grow `<`, `>` and `&` to six bytes, and `<template>` would drop the doctype and the `html`, `head` and `body` attributes, so neither holds it. The group is `<script type="application/json" id="doan-prototype">` with `{"start", "frames": [{"id", "title"}]}`, `title` being the shown name (title, else id) so the fallback stays in Go, and `<`, `>`, `&` escaped.
  - **Layout.** Each frame plays in an `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts allow-forms" srcdoc>` that fills the browser viewport by CSS, and the outer page does not scroll, so `innerWidth`, `innerHeight`, media queries and `100vh` follow the browser window (Responsive; there is no Fit). `allow-forms` is there for the reason the prototype window gives; the origin stays opaque (no `allow-same-origin`). The static tab title is the start frame's `<title or id> — Prototype`, HTML-escaped; `prototype.js` sets the tab title and the iframe's `title` to each frame it plays. `prototype.css` copies the values it needs from `shell.css`, because the file cannot link the app's tokens. The page adds no header or button.
  - **Frame switching** (`prototype.js`, the page's last script). A new iframe for every frame shown, never a new `srcdoc` on the same one: the same iframe keeps its `WindowProxy`, so `event.source` could not tell an old document's messages apart, and it adds an entry to the iframe's own history. The iframe gets `sandbox`, then `name` (the frame's id, which `isolate.js` reads as `window.name`, readable in the opaque-origin document in Chromium and WebKit), then `srcdoc`, all before it is inserted, because the sandbox flags apply from the first document on. The frame on screen stays, `inert`, until the new one reports `ready` or `missing` or its own 10 s timer runs out; a frame replaced while still loading is dropped at once. Messages count only from the newest iframe's window with origin `"null"`.
  - **Hash contract.** The frame shown is the page's hash, `#<encodeURIComponent(id)>`, a public contract a recipient can bookmark. Opening and every `hashchange` resolve it the same way (`resolveHash`): empty, not decodable, or naming no frame of the group is the start frame. Opening corrects the address with `location.replace`, adding no entry. A followed link from the frame playing (only once it is `ready`, only its first link, only a value of the group) sets `location.hash`, one history entry, and the `hashchange` plays it; a link to the frame playing restarts it with no entry. So Back and Forward step one frame each and restart it, Back from the first frame leaves the file, and a reload restarts the frame on screen. The tab title changes right after the new entry so the browser's history menu names the frame. When focus was in the frame (or a frame dropped while loading had claimed it), the new iframe takes focus once it is ready.
  - **Hidden until isolated.** Before isolation an iframe would draw the whole canvas, other frames and notes included, so each starts `visibility: hidden`. `prototype.js` registers its listener before it creates the first iframe, shows a frame on `ready`; on `missing`, or when nothing came within 10 s of that frame's start, it keeps it hidden and shows the line instead; a `ready` after the timeout removes the line and shows the frame. The timer means a document that never answers still leaves a message on screen (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-22). It takes only messages from the iframe's own window with origin `"null"`. Without JavaScript no iframe is made, so only the no-JavaScript line shows.
  - **`ready` is a display rule, not a security proof.** A canvas script can post `ready` itself and show the unisolated canvas; the file holds the whole canvas anyway, so the sender's own scripts revealing it is not guarded, and no `allow-same-origin` is added to check the sender.
  - **Isolation** (`isolate.js`): the first script in the `srcdoc` head, inside a function (`measure.js`'s place; no `lib.js`, which it does not use), reading its frame from `window.name` before any canvas script runs. It finds the frame by ID at DOMContentLoaded, else after the `load` handlers, and then waits for it as the app's prototype window does (up to 2 s, the same `LATE_FRAME_MS` literal, pinned by `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`), so a link in a frame the run script finds late is still followed; it isolates exactly one with the prototype window's markers and adopted sheet; none or two report `missing`. It does not read the mark, so the player's verdict is the run script's. It reports once. The sheet and the `KEEP` list are copies of `prototypeScript.ts`'s, pinned as text by `app/src/main/exportViewer.test.ts`, so the exported page defines `--doan-viewport-height` as the window does ('Prototype window' 'Rendering in place'); `100dvh` is there for a phone browser's visible height, not yet checked on a real phone (BACKLOG).
  - **Links** (`isolate.js`), with the prototype window's click rules and table ('Prototype window' 'Links', `clicks.json`, which the export's test runs in a real DOM): a window capture listener registered before canvas scripts, `closest("[data-doan-goto]")` from the target as the window sees it, the link inside the playing frame, and a decision one task later or, if a navigation starts after the click's dispatch first, at its `beforeunload` (a navigation that commits before the timer would take the timer with it, and a message the old document posted reaches the viewer with no source, which it refuses); it posts `{goto}` (`null` over 200 UTF-16 units). It decides by the final `defaultPrevented`, as the window does, and never cancels a link click itself, so every way the canvas cancels counts and nothing else does. On a link with an `<a href>` or `<area href>` around it, the browser would follow the href after the dispatch, so the capture listener adds a one-time window listener for that click: it runs in the window's bubble phase after every canvas listener registered before the click (each phase invokes a fresh copy of the list) and takes the hrefs a browser follows (`href` and the XLink `href`, as attribute nodes) off the nearest `<a>` or `<area>` then, leaving the activation nothing to follow; after the decision the timer puts each back unless the canvas set one meanwhile or moved the node to another element. An `href` in any other namespace is the canvas's and stays. The navigation listener below skips such a click. Stopping the navigation instead (`window.stop()` from the timer) lost the race in Chromium over http, and the Navigation API's `navigate` does not fire in this opaque-origin iframe.
  - **Navigation.** Bubble-phase `window` listeners, so the canvas's own handlers run first and a router link that checks `defaultPrevented` still sees its event (capture would break it): every `submit` is `preventDefault`ed; a click on `<a href>` or `<area href>` the canvas has not already prevented is prevented, and a written `href` starting with `#` is set on `location.hash` instead, because a `srcdoc` document resolves `#id` against the outer file's URL (WebKit then loaded the outer page into the frame and Chromium dropped the click). `history.pushState` and `replaceState` are wrapped before any canvas script: a call whose URL the browser refuses with `SecurityError` (a fragment or path resolves against the file URL while the document is `about:srcdoc`, in Chromium and WebKit) is made again without the URL, so the state and the entry stay and the script goes on, but `location` does not change; a call the browser takes (an `about:srcdoc` URL) keeps its own result, and any other error reaches the canvas. A `<base href="about:srcdoc">` would fix fragments but break `//host/x` URLs, so there is none.
  - **Limits** (Chromium and WebKit): a frame a script creates cannot be exported (the CLI refuses it as `frame_not_found`, and so does the prototype window's Export when it plays one); a script's `location` change and `form.submit()` are not blocked, and a click or submit handler that stops propagation keeps its default (WebKit loads the target into the frame; Chromium stops a `file:` target itself but loads a web URL); requests such as `fetch` are not blocked; a router that reads `location.pathname` or `hash` back after `pushState` may not follow. A browser reload brings the frame back. On a link with an href, a listener the canvas adds during the click that runs after `isolate.js`'s sees the link without its href, and if it sets the href again the browser follows that (the frame navigates while the link is still followed), a `MutationObserver` on it sees the href removed and put back, and a canvas that stops the click's propagation before the window keeps the href, so the browser navigates the frame (as above) while the link is still followed. Entries the canvas adds inside an iframe (a `#` link, `pushState`) stay in the joint session history after the iframe is replaced: observed, each cost one Back that changed nothing, and when the frame had answered at once the browser later pruned them and Back went to an unexpected frame; this is accepted as browser behavior. The file's link groups are static: a link a script creates can move to a frame already in the exported group, but cannot expand that group.
  - **Pinned** by `TestAssemblePrototypeOutputIsPinned` and `TestAssemblePrototypePageShape` (`assemble_prototype_test.go`), the prototype tests of `exportViewer.test.ts` and `export_test.go`. `TestPlayerJoinsTheCLIsPrototypeFile` and `TestPlayerRefusesWhatTheCLIRefuses` (`assemble_export_test.go`) run the real `viewer/player.js` under `node` against `AssemblePrototype`, so `go test ./...` needs `node` on `PATH` and fails without it. A browser check of a written file is a `.scratch/` probe, not a suite.

## 4. CLI ↔ app

Only `active`, `onboarding …`, `open`, `work`, `handoff` and `remove` need the app, and `work` succeeds without it; every other command is file-backed. Shapes: `schema/runtime.v1.schema.json`, `schema/app-socket.v1.schema.json`. App side `app/src/main/runtime.ts`, CLI side `cli/internal/cli/active.go`, each with its tests. (Reference: Orca's `orca-runtime.json` + authToken over a unix socket, local bundle 1.4.230.)

- **Runtime file.** `<userData>/runtime.json` and `<userData>/conversation-runtime.json` are each written as a fresh 0600 temp file and renamed; both name the same socket, each with its own token ('App-socket credentials'). The socket sits in an `mkdtemp` directory chmod 0700 (the directory, not the socket file, is the access boundary), outside userData because of macOS's 104-byte socket-path limit. `requestSingleInstanceLock` keeps two launches from overwriting one runtime; closing the window only hides it, so `active` keeps working.
- **Connection contract** (both languages must agree): one request line, one reply line, close; 64 KiB line cap; a 2 s deadline from connect that stays armed after the reply. The app checks schema, then token (lengths before `timingSafeEqual`), then project membership, so a bad token never learns which roots are open. The token is not pattern-checked, so any wrong token reaches `bad_token`. Each socket version dispatches on its own, so a new version adds a handler and changes no other version's answers.
- **App-socket credentials** (`answerLine`, `CONVERSATION_RUNTIME` in `runtime.ts`): `runtime.json` holds the full token, for the person's terminal agents and every version. `conversation-runtime.json` holds the conversation token, which the app accepts for v1 and v3–v6 only; v2 (onboarding) and v7 (`doan remove`) answer it `bad_token`. Doan's app conversations get only the conversation file: their `DOAN_RUNTIME` names it. For Doan-connection conversations the fence and Codex permission profiles reopen it and not `runtime.json` (§7 'Fence'), so their agents cannot clone, set up or remove projects through the socket. A legacy-external conversation (a resumed terminal session) runs with no fence (§7 'Fence'), so its agent can still read `runtime.json`; for it the credential is no boundary. Before either is dialled, the CLI refuses `doan onboarding …` and `doan remove` in any app conversation (`conversation_refused`, 'Removing a project over app-socket v7' 'Before the app').
- **Selection ownership.** No selection, or one owned by another root, answers `result: {}`. Roots compare literally with Go's discovered real root; main never rediscovers it.
- **`DOAN_RUNTIME`** overrides the runtime path (tests and dev point it at an app run with a temporary `--user-data-dir`). For `active`, empty means default. A nonempty relative value is `usage_error` for every app command, `work` included, checked in `run.go` before the command body. For `onboarding …` and `open`, set-but-empty is explicit and never launches ('Bootstrap'). Shell `check` uses only its parent directory, for session recording, and an invalid value never breaks a file-only command.
- **CLI reader.** Command bodies get the runtime path and dialer from `Run`, never from the environment. Runtime and reply are size-bounded and schema-validated; readers normalize numeric `v`. Before dialing, the CLI requires the socket and its directory to be this user's, with no group or other write on the directory (`ownSocket` in `socket.go`): the runtime file can outlive the app's socket directory, so the file alone does not show whose socket it names. Unusable runtime, refused socket, dial failure, timeout or `bad_token` → `app_not_running`; a reply with another `v`, including an older app's v1 `bad_request` → `app_protocol_mismatch`; malformed reply or app `bad_request` → `internal_error`. For v4–v7, `busy` keeps the app's message through `withoutSecrets`, and for every other code the CLI writes its own message and `nextSteps`, never the app's text. No port guessing. A runtime swapped for a FIFO after stat can still block (accepted).
- **Cleanup.** `will-quit` synchronously removes the socket directory and removes each runtime file only if its bytes are still this instance's. Crashes and startup-time exits can leave files; the next start replaces the runtime and the CLI treats a stale socket as unavailable. Other instances' old temp directories are not swept (accepted). A socket that fails to start is logged and the app continues; `active` then reports the app unavailable.

### Onboarding over app-socket v2

`doan onboarding …` reaches the onboarding service (§7 'First-run onboarding') over version 2 of the same socket; requests, replies and error codes are in `schema/app-socket.v2.schema.json`. v1 and `active` are unchanged.

- **Dispatch.** Main reads numeric `v` first: `v: 2` goes schema → full token (§4 'App-socket credentials') → service (a thrown error is a generic v2 `bad_request`); `v` 3–7 have their own handlers (sections below); every other line takes the v1 path byte for byte, so an older client gets the refusal it understands. The v1 connection contract holds; a late reply is dropped but the work continues.
- **No secrets on the wire.** Replies never carry account names, secrets, callback codes, the socket token or credential paths. The CLI (`cli/internal/cli/onboarding.go`) also filters every reply: a `message` naming the token or runtime path is replaced, any other such string makes the reply `internal_error`. Its own failure messages omit the runtime path.
- A reply with `v` other than 2 is `app_protocol_mismatch`. Request refusals create no operation; operation failures are reported on the operation. Onboarding requests stay accepted after first run completes.
- **Storage in onboarding.** `project_inspect`, `project_select` and `project_clone` take an optional `storage` (`repo` | `local`, absent = `repo`); what each request and reply carries, `inspection_stale` included, is in the `$comment`s of `schema/app-socket.v2.schema.json`. A reply to a request without `storage` has no `storage` key and no `.git/info/exclude` label, so an older CLI still reads it. The CLI sends `storage` only for `--storage`, so an explicit mode against an older app is `bad_request` → `internal_error`, as for v3 `update` ('update stays in v3'). A local plan for a folder in no Git repository (below HOME) also lists `.git/`, and select then runs `doan init --storage local --git-init`, so the person's `initialize` consent covers the new repository. A refused local setup fails the operation with `local_not_git` or `local_exclude_failed`; a failed `git init` is `init_failed`, a missing git `git_missing` and an unremovable new `.git` `cleanup_failed` (§12 'Storage'). Fixtures `schema/fixtures/app-socket-v2/` pin the shapes, and the `*-storageless.json` replies are tested against the pre-storage shape on both sides.
- **Bootstrap** (`onboarding start` and `open`, sharing `bootstrap` in `onboarding.go`). If the first attempt finds no app and `DOAN_RUNTIME` is unset, the CLI opens the `.app` containing its own real path once (`/usr/bin/open`) and polls for a bounded time (`app_launch_failed`). `onboarding start` launches in the background (`open -g <bundle> --args --doan-launch=background`, `launchArgs`), `open` in the foreground. Both launches run with only a fixed `PATH` and the caller's `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME` and `TMPDIR` (`launchEnv`), because `/usr/bin/open` hands the caller's whole environment to a newly launched app: launched from an agent terminal, the app would otherwise carry that terminal's profile, session id and hook variables. `--args` reaches only a fresh launch; `open -g` to an app that is running but not yet answering sends a reopen, which arrives as an `activate` that does not activate the app, so the window stays as it was (§7 'Background reveal'). A `not_ready` answer (v4, v6) means an app that is running but still starting: it is asked again within the same cap with no launch, and one that is still not ready at the cap ends as `busy` for `open` (`--prototype` too); among "not running" answers, only a vanished runtime or token ends that wait early. An app this invocation launched that is still not ready at the cap ends as `app_launch_failed`. The bundle is trusted by membership, not code-signature authenticity: canonical bundle of the CLI's real path, `CFBundleIdentifier` `design.doan.app`, executable resolving inside it to a regular file, no linked MacOS directory or executable. A permission-denied runtime or socket never launches (likely a sandbox around a running app). Tests: `onboarding_test.go`.

### Work signals over app-socket v3

`doan work start|update|done` (`cli/internal/cli/work.go`, path, session, argument, request and reply rules in `work_rules.go`) tells the running app which canvases an agent works on and, with `update`, the frame it starts editing and a one-line note, over version 3 of the same socket; shapes in `schema/app-socket.v3.schema.json`. A terminal session sends all three; an app conversation sends only `update`. v1, v2 and `active` are unchanged. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 items 1–3 and 10.

- **`update` stays in v3.** A new socket command takes its own version; `update` is not a new command but a new action of the same `work` command and keeps `v: 3`: a newer app accepts every older request, and an older app refuses `update` in its schema as `bad_request`, which the CLI reports as `delivered: false` ('Best effort'), so no CLI/app pairing blocks an agent. A new version would only have turned the same refusal into another code.

- **Best effort.** The command exists so agents signal without being blocked: everything after argument and repo checks ends `ok: true`, exit 0, with `delivered: false` unless the app accepted a v3 request (no runtime, an unusable or other-version runtime, dial failure, permission denial, the 2 s deadline, `bad_token`, `project_not_open` (including an auto-registration that misses main's deadline and still completes), an older app's v1 refusal, a malformed or oversized reply, a missing HOME). It never launches the app (no bootstrap). An older CLI reading a newer app's reply with `others` fails its older schema and reports `delivered: false` although the app took the signal (accepted: it blocks nothing); a newer CLI reading an older app's reply sees no `others`, which agents read as no information.
- **Suppression.** With `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION=1` (set by the app for its own conversations, §7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC'), `start` and `done` send nothing and answer `delivered: false` after the usual argument checks, since the app already follows the turn. `update` is sent with `conversation` set to `DOAN_CONVERSATION_ID` in place of `session` and `provider`, and only when that value meets the opaque-ID contract; otherwise it sends nothing. The app keeps such an update apart from terminal work ('Conversation state'), so an app conversation never counts as a terminal: no sidebar terminal spinner, terminal-session continue block, sibling notice or Move to Trash block.
- **Update arguments** (`workUpdateArgs`): one path, at most one `--frame` with v4's frame bounds, and a `--note` of one line without control characters (C0, DEL, C1, U+2028/2029) and at most 120 code points after trimming; a blank note is no note. Anything else is `usage_error`, never cut, so the agent can resend it and a cut cannot change its meaning. The v3 schema carries the same limits and the app validates against it, so `workNoteMax` and the excluded characters must stay in step with it. Omitting `--frame` or `--note` clears that part.
- **Paths.** `workTarget` accepts only `.doan/canvases/*.html` or `.doan/ds/*.html` (lower-case `.html`, as the listing, §1 convention 1) inside the real root, existing or not yet created, resolved through symlinks (a missing file through its nearest existing ancestor) and sent as the on-disk repo-relative spelling with `/`; a link leaving the root, a directory or a dangling link is refused (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-31). Root aliases (a symlinked ancestor, or the root spelled in another letter case or Unicode form, as a shell's logical `$PWD` keeps it) resolve before the lexical check: the nearest ancestor that is the same file as the root is replaced by the root. A link is accepted only when its resolved path is still directly in `.doan/canvases/` or `.doan/ds/`; one into another directory, the repo's included, is refused.
- **Session.** `workSession` picks `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID` (provider `claude`), else `CODEX_THREAD_ID` or `CODEX_SESSION_ID` (`codex`), with the same opaque-ID contract as shell check associations; both providers, two different Codex IDs or an empty or invalid value is anonymous. It is a separate function from `sessionSettings` because that one also needs profile and runtime paths.
- **Reply line.** The 64 KiB reply cap counts the newline: `active.go` `readLine`, which every app command shares (`active` and `work` directly, `onboarding`, `open`, `handoff` and `remove` through `exchange`), refuses a longer line even when its newline arrives as the next byte.
- **Dispatch** (`answerLine` in `app/src/main/runtime.ts`): `v: 3` goes schema → token → path pattern (`start`, `update`) → a conversation's `update` to the conversation state, else registered root or auto-registration; every other `v` keeps its path, so the order still hides which roots are open from a bad token. The pattern check sits before the root check so an unregistered root never starts a `doan ls`; the schema enforces the same pattern, so no reply changes.
- **Auto-registration** (`app/src/main/autoFolders.ts`, `runtime.ts`, `shell.ts` `adopt`): a `start` or terminal `update` from an unregistered root runs `doan ls --json --repo <root>` (never Git in TS, §13) and registers the root only when the result is `list`, its `root` equals the requested root as a string (the app never recomputes a root; POSTMORTEM 2026-10-22), and a registered root is in its `git.worktrees`. That one condition means same repository, same doan root path, another worktree, so another doan root of the same checkout is refused; it needs no in-memory `common`, so it works right after launch, and a list cut at `gitWorktreeLimit` may refuse. The comparison is redone against the registered roots when `ls` ends, so a Remove from Doan during it registers nothing. Concurrent `start`s and `update`s for one root share one registration, and a `done` for a root with a pending registration applies after it; otherwise `done` never registers. A conversation's `update` never registers: its root is registered already. Main answers within `AUTO_REGISTER_WAIT_MS` (1.5 s, inside the CLI's 2 s that starts before dialing) or replies `project_not_open`, but never kills the `ls` (only `runLs`'s default 10 s timeout applies) and applies the request once registered. `adopt` adds the root, its mark and its `common` in memory and publishes the root as `list` from that same `ls`, so it never appears as a separate `loading` project; it sends the list only, never `FolderFocus` (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-03), and the root joins `workState.roots` before the work applies.
- **State** (`app/src/main/workState.ts`, wiring in `index.ts`): memory only, never `sessions.jsonl` or ui-state, and never used to pick a worktree on re-entry. An identified session is one set across roots, so its `done` clears every root; anonymous work is one set per root. Display identity is root + path (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01). Expiry is per session, `WORK_EXPIRY_MS` after the later of its last `start` or `update` and the last folder-refresh `rev` change of one of its paths, with one unref'ed timeout armed for the earliest deadline (no polling). `MAX_WORK_SESSIONS` counts identified and per-root anonymous sessions; `MAX_WORK_PATHS` counts (root, path) pairs, and a capped `start` or `update` still renews its session. An `update` adds its (root, path) as `start` does and replaces that entry's frame and note; a `start` keeps them and only renews the entry's `touched`. Each session gets a work turn id (`turn`, provider included) when main creates it, so the session after a `done` or expiry is a new turn; the renderer keys its follow state by it rather than by when it first saw the work, which would differ with when a canvas was opened or subscribed. Roots still unregistered after any auto-registration are dropped; removing a project or an auto-registered worktree drops its paths; a path not yet listed waits in the set.
- **Conversation state** (`app/src/main/conversationWork.ts`, wiring in `index.ts`): one entry per conversation, separate from `workState.ts`. Main accepts an `update` only while that conversation's turn on the same root runs (`ConversationService.liveTurns()`: accepted, not ended, closed or failed in persistence, and the app not quitting) and records that turn's id; any other is answered `ok` and dropped, since the agent could do nothing with an error. `ConversationService` clears the entry when the turn can take no more updates: its terminal event, deletion, a history that stopped taking writes, and quit, before the quit writes. Memory only; conversation events are unchanged, and a renderer reload reads the entry again. The request names the conversation but no turn, and a Codex runner's environment is fixed for its process, so an update sent in one turn and arriving after the next turn was accepted counts for the new turn (accepted: it takes a Stop and a new send inside the CLI request's milliseconds, and leaves one marker until the next update or the end of the turn); one arriving between turns is dropped.
- **Other sessions** (`app/src/main/workOthers.ts` `othersOnCanvas`, wiring in `runtime.ts` `workApplied` and `index.ts`): after a `start` or `update` is applied (directly, as a conversation's `update`, or after auto-registration), main answers `{v: 3, ok: true, others?}` with the other sessions on the request's root and path, compared as strings (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01). Terminals come from `workItems` and drop out per file, `WORK_EXPIRY_MS` after that path's own `touched`, without changing the session expiry above. Conversations come from `liveTurns()`: a running turn whose current-turn `ConversationWork` names this root and path (with its frame and note), or, when the current turn has none, a turn bound to this canvas (no frame or note, listed after the rest); a `ConversationWork` of another turn counts as none (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27). The caller is left out by `session` and `provider` (an anonymous caller is its root's anonymous session; PRODUCT §5 item 10) or by conversation id. Elements never carry an id (v3 schema); newest `touched` first, cut to `WORK_OTHERS_MAX` (the schema's `maxItems`, so the reply stays far under the 64 KiB line). An empty list is omitted; `done`, errors and `project_not_open` carry none, and a throwing lookup answers `ok` without it. The answer path reads no disk inside the CLI's 2 s: `ConversationService.liveTurns()` reads only memory, recording each turn's root, provider (a legacy-external conversation's from `session.runtime`) and bound canvas when the turn is accepted and again on every save of a new canvas (`promoteTo`, `refreshCanvas`, including a busy send's refresh), and dropping it on delete. A canvas moved during a turn with no send keeps the old path in both the index and memory until the next send.
- **IPC.** Main-window-only `work:get`/`work:changed` carry `WorkItem` (`workState.ts`; null session and provider for anonymous work) and `conversation-work:get`/`conversation-work:changed` carry `ConversationWork` (`conversationWork.ts`). The renderer subscribes before reading; main publishes only when the items change, comparing every field (`workItemsChanged`), because an update that changes only a frame or note must still reach the renderer.

### Opening a canvas over app-socket v4

`doan open <canvas path> [--frame <id>]` (`cli/internal/cli/open.go`, argument, request and reply rules in `open_rules.go`) asks the running app to open a canvas as a `doan://` link does (§7 'Deep links'), over version 4 of the same socket; shapes in `schema/app-socket.v4.schema.json`. v1–v3 are unchanged. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 items 3 and 10.

- **Before the app.** Argument errors are `usage_error`. The path goes through `workTarget` as for `work` ('Work signals over app-socket v3' 'Paths'), but a file that does not exist is `canvas_not_found` without asking the app. `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION` changes nothing: an app conversation has an explicit `DOAN_RUNTIME` (§7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC'), so it reaches only its own app and never launches one.
- **Dispatch** (`answerLine` in `app/src/main/runtime.ts`): `v: 4` goes schema → token → registered root, then readiness and the list check, then the renderer (`openRequests` in `deepLinks.ts`, §7 'Deep links'); a bad token still learns nothing about open roots.
- **Answers.** `not_ready` is the one retryable code: launch restore or the first project reads are unfinished (`openLinksReady()` over all roots), or the target root is still loading. The app answers it at once and holds no request, so a CLI that gave up is never followed by a late move. `busy` carries the app's message as the reason (a locked page, a dialog that refuses to close, first run, or no renderer answer in time); messages and the other codes as in 'CLI reader'. A frame result that does not fit the request (`none` exactly when no frame was asked) is `internal_error`.
- **Deadline.** The app's 2 s deadline from connect and the CLI's 2 s per attempt (taken before it dials) both hold; the renderer's answer timeout sits well inside them (§7 'Deep links'). Tests inject the clock and never wait.

### Handoff over app-socket v5

`doan handoff <canvas path> --message <text|->` (`cli/internal/cli/handoff.go`, text, key, request and reply rules in `handoff_rules.go`) asks the running app to start a new conversation on a new canvas once the asking conversation's turn ends (§7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'Handoff'), over version 5 of the same socket; shapes in `schema/app-socket.v5.schema.json`. v1–v4 are unchanged. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 items 2 'Handoff' and 10.

- **Before the app**, in this order: arguments and a missing `--message` (`usage_error`); no valid `DOAN_HANDOFF` (`not_app_conversation`, checked before standard input is read, so a terminal agent is refused at once); `--message -` reads standard input up to the 64 KiB line limit; the text must be UTF-8, non-empty after trimming, free of C0 control characters other than `\n` and `\t` and of DEL, and at most 10,000 code points, and is refused, never cut, so the agent can resend it; then the repo, the path through `workTarget` ('Work signals over app-socket v3' 'Paths') narrowed to `.doan/canvases/*.html`, and a file that does not exist is `canvas_not_found`. It never launches the app (no bootstrap): only a running app conversation can ask.
- **Key.** `run.go` alone reads `DOAN_HANDOFF` and passes it as `ctx.Handoff` (a value outside the schema's 64-hex pattern counts as none), as it does `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION`; the command body reads no environment. The key only names the asking conversation; the socket token stays the boundary (§7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'Handoff').
- **Line limit.** Both validators count `text`'s `maxLength` in code points, while Go `json.Marshal` writes `<`, `>`, `&`, U+2028 and U+2029 as six-byte escapes. The limit is set so that 10,000 such characters (60,000 bytes) still fit the 64 KiB line with root, path and token, and the CLI also checks the marshalled request against that line minus a token headroom (`handoffLineMax`), refusing a longer one as `usage_error` instead of sending a line the app would answer with `bad_request`. The path pattern `^\.doan/canvases/[^/]+\.html$` is narrower than v4's, because a `.doan/ds/` page is not a canvas a conversation starts on.
- **Dispatch** (`answerLine` in `app/src/main/runtime.ts`): `v: 5` goes schema → token → registered root (`project_not_open`) → `ConversationService.requestHandoff`: the key (`not_app_conversation`), then `handoffEligibility` (`conversation/handoff.ts`) in the order `root_mismatch` (the conversation's root, compared as a string), `not_running`, `not_canvas_conversation`, `plan_mode`, `handoff_turn`, `already_requested` (this turn already took a request for the same path; a request for another path is taken, so a turn can ask once per new canvas), `same_canvas`. A bad token learns nothing about roots or conversations, and a thrown error is a plain `bad_request`.
