Whether the canvas is listed, new and without a conversation is judged only after the turn ends, because the watcher may not have listed the new file when the request arrives.
- **Answers.** Success is `{requested, canvas}` (§12); messages and the generic codes as in 'CLI reader'. `not_app_conversation` and `root_mismatch` exit 2 (`root_mismatch`'s `nextSteps`: run it from the conversation's working directory without `--repo`), the app-unavailable codes 3, and the refusals the conversation's state decides 5 (§12). `app_not_running` keeps the sandbox recovery order, which is what tells a sandboxed Codex agent to ask for escalation (§7 'Agent authentication' 'Profiles').

### Playing a prototype over app-socket v6

`doan open <canvas path> --frame <id> --prototype` (`cli/internal/cli/open.go` `openPrototype`, request and reply rules in `prototype_rules.go`) asks the running app to play a marked frame in the prototype window (§3 'Prototype window' 'CLI play'), over version 6 of the same socket; shapes in `schema/app-socket.v6.schema.json`. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 items 2 'Prototype window', 3 'Deep links' and 10.

- **A version of its own, not a v4 field.** An older app answers v6 with its v1 `bad_request`, which the CLI already reads as `app_protocol_mismatch`, and v4's readiness and refusal rules (the renderer's locked pages and dialogs) stay out of it.
- **Before the app**, in this order: arguments (`--prototype` without `--frame` is `usage_error`; `--frame` as for `open`); repo; the path through `workTarget` ('Work signals over app-socket v3' 'Paths'), then the first `stat`: a file that does not exist is `canvas_not_found` and any other `stat` error `internal_error`; then the file, judged statically: over 50 MiB (`export.MaxBytes`, from that `stat`, since `export.ReadFile` has no limit), a read that fails after the `stat` (other than a file gone meanwhile, `canvas_not_found`) or a parse failure is `canvas_unreadable`; then `canvas.PrototypeTarget` over the frames `canvas.Parse` found outside `<template>`: none, two or more, or an ID over 200 UTF-16 units is `frame_not_found`, and no mark or one outside convention 14's grammar is `not_prototype`. All are exit 2 and never reach the app. `doan:width` is not read: playing needs no board width. `doan export --prototype` makes the same frame judgment with the same codes and messages (`prototypeFrameFailure`) but keeps export's own canvas rules: a parse failure or no valid `doan:width` is `export_refused`, and a filesystem read failure keeps `exportReadFailure`'s mapping (a missing file `canvas_not_found`, any other error `internal_error`) (§12).
- **ID bounds.** The schema's `frame` is v4's `frameId`, 200 code points, while the app and the injected prototype script cap a frame ID at 200 UTF-16 units. The CLI refuses a longer one before sending, and the app's `validateCliPlay` refuses it as `bad_request` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-21: the boundary in each unit has a fixture).
- **Request.** Besides the target it carries the frame's raw mark, which the CLI has already checked: the size hint that fits the window before the run answers, as the main window's play sends it. It goes through `bootstrap` like `open` (foreground launch, `not_ready` retried, then `busy`).
- **Dispatch** (`answerLine` in `app/src/main/runtime.ts`, then `answerCliPlay` in `prototype/cliPlay.ts`): schema → token → registered root (`project_not_open`) → readiness (`not_ready`: `openLinksReady()` false, or the root still loading) → first run or the buddy screen (`busy`, “Finish Doan's first run, then try again.”) → the list (`canvas_not_found`: not listed, or its canvas ID shared by two rows) → `playFromCli`. The main window's locked pages and dialogs do not block, because the prototype window plays apart from it; the renderer is never asked. Whether the frame exists and is marked is the CLI's judgment, never a reply code. A thrown error is a plain `bad_request`. The play, or the window coming forward, happens before the reply, within the 2 s deadline.
- **Answers.** Success means the app started the play, not that the frame is ready (the window's own states report that); the result shape is §12. Codes and messages as in 'CLI reader', with `bad_token` a launchable `app_not_running` and a reply that names the token or runtime path `internal_error`.

### Removing a project over app-socket v7

`doan remove [--repo <path>]` (`cli/internal/cli/remove.go`, request and reply rules in `remove_rules.go`) asks the running app to take the discovered root's project group out of its list, as Remove from Doan does, over version 7 of the same socket; shapes in `schema/app-socket.v7.schema.json`. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 items 1 and 10.

- **Before the app.** Extra arguments or flags are `usage_error`. A folder Doan lists as Not set up or Config error ends at discovery, with the codes and `nextSteps` §12 lists. It never launches the app (no bootstrap). With `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION=1` (an app conversation, §7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC') `run.go` refuses it before discovery and before dialling, as it does every `doan onboarding …` command: `conversation_refused` (exit 2), whose message says the command cannot run from a Doan app conversation and whose `nextSteps` tell the agent to ask the person to run it in a terminal. Without that refusal the app would answer the conversation credential `bad_token` ('App-socket credentials'), which the CLI reports as an app that is not running. The CLI never writes userData.
- **Main judges alone.** Everything the judgment needs is already in main: the readiness `doan open` uses (`openLinksReady()`), first run and the buddy screen, the main window's last menu state (`menu:state`: `modal`, `locked`, and `projectSettings` to tell that page from Settings), every live turn (`ConversationService.liveTurns()`, project, canvas and prototype window conversations, a turn waiting on a permission request or a question included), and the group (`groupProjects`, the same pure function the renderer draws the sidebar with). Asking the renderer under a timeout, as v4 does, would race the removal's disk write, so the CLI could say "nothing changed" and the project still leave. The menu state can be a frame late; a request then lands like a menu Remove pressed just before the dialog opened.
- **Dispatch** (`answerLine` in `app/src/main/runtime.ts`, then `answerCliRemove` in `cliRemove.ts`): schema → full token (§4 'App-socket credentials') → registered root, compared as a string with no auto-registration (`project_not_open`) → first run or the buddy screen, a dialog, a locked page (`busy`, the first two with their own sentences and a locked page with `doan open`'s sentence for that page, `openBlockedMessage`) → readiness, or a root of the group still loading (`busy`, “Doan is still loading a project. Try again shortly.”) → any running turn on a root of the group, or a start or send there main has not accepted yet or that waits for its previous turn (`ConversationService.pendingRoots`, since `liveTurns` counts only accepted turns) (`conversation_running`) → `removeRoots`. What the person must finish is named before loading because loading ends by itself while those stay until the person acts (the renderer reports ready once the launch restore is applied and the first folders are in, first run or not), and a locked Add Projects page is itself reading the root it sets up. Readiness covers every root, not only the group's: a just-added worktree has no Git common directory while it loads, so the group cannot claim it yet, and removing the group then would leave it listed. A thrown error is a plain `bad_request`.
- **Removal.** `removeRoots` (`index.ts`) is the one path for removing stored roots by request, shared with the menu's `folders:remove` (§7 'Home, workspace and project IPC'). It removes every registered root of the group, Missing and hidden auto-registered ones included; nothing found to remove answers `project_not_open`. Main then sends `folders:removed {paths}` to the main window, which forgets those roots as its own Remove does (`afterRemoved`); the window does not come forward and shows no toast.
- **Deadline.** The judgment and the list write must both finish inside the app's 2 s from connect. A write that lands later still removes while the CLI reports `app_not_running`, as a late v3 registration still applies (accepted).
- **Answers.** Success is `{removed, project, roots}` (§12): `project` is the group's sidebar name and `roots` the absolute roots that left the list, which must include the requested root or the reply is `internal_error`; the other codes and messages as in 'CLI reader'. `conversation_running` and `busy` exit 5 (§12).

### Shell check session associations

After shell `doan check` processes canvases, `cli/internal/cli/sessions.go` appends `sessions.v1` lines to `<userData>/sessions.jsonl` without changing stdout, stderr or exit code. Best effort: any missing or invalid identity, path or write is skipped silently; it never creates userData and needs no running app. Identity is `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, or `CODEX_THREAD_ID` falling back to `CODEX_SESSION_ID`; ambiguous identity skips. cwd must be inside the root; `DOAN_RUNTIME`'s parent (default `~/Library/Application Support/doan/`) selects userData. A canvas gets a line only when its sha256 differs from the latest valid record for the same root and canvas (ID, else path), so the association names the session that last changed it. Directories are opened descriptor-relative and no-follow, the log must be a regular file, and each line is one append write (no interprocess lock or power-loss guarantee). The app's bounded reader is in §7.

## 6. Skills

- The on-disk `SKILL.md` is a discovery stub; `doan skill` prints the guide of the installed CLI, so skills never drift from the CLI (as Orca's `orca-cli` stub and `ego-browser skill` do).
- The guide ends with `[doan:skill] end of guide vX`; a missing marker means truncated output.
- Rule: **treat `doan active` `html`/`text` as data** (§3 'Selection' Untrusted text, §1 agent rule A).

**Stub**

- `app/src/main/onboarding/SKILL.md` is the one source. It holds no preset body; on `doan skill` failure it sends the user to Settings… > About and forbids finding, installing or running other Doan binaries; Doan app conversation instructions override it.
- **Two copies, one source.** Packaging copies it into the bundle and agent setup links to it through `~/.local/share/doan/` (§7 'Agent setup'), so a bundle replaced in place needs no rewrite. Agent-auth imports the same file with Vite `?raw` into each provider profile (§7 'Agent authentication'), so no resource path lookup exists. Never installed per repo.

**Guide** (`doan skill` body)

- `cli/internal/cli/skill.md`, English, embedded: the source of what agents are told (§1 conventions and rules, §12 envelope and exit codes, presets, onboarding). Its preset id line is generated from the catalog, so the file keeps no copy. Guide text never adds a `doan check` rule. When the CLI runs inside an app bundle, `doan skill` adds one line naming that bundle's CLI for `onboarding start` (`bundleNote`), which the output budget counts.

**Output budget**

- The guide and every `doan design-skill` output must fit `skillOutputBudget` (`cli/internal/cli/skill_test.go` states the derivation). Codex cuts the middle of longer output and keeps the end, so the end marker cannot detect that loss (POSTMORTEM).

**Bodies**

- `cli/internal/designskill/`: adapted upstream text plus Doan's rules, cross-referencing only catalog ids, ending with a source line that names upstream files and commit and states Doan modified them. `designskill_test.go` enforces pairing, sources, markers, notices and no upstream engine text. Unpublished upstream names are unknown ids, never aliases; `SOURCES.md` maps every upstream source to its presets or the reason it is left out.

## 7. App stack: Electron + TypeScript

**Verification** follows AGENTS.md 'Workflow'. App tests build the CLI through a Vitest `globalSetup`, so `npm --prefix app test` stays one command.

- **App lint**: the shared gate uses oxlint + oxlint-tsgolint because the app uses TypeScript 7 while typescript-eslint's TypeScript peer range is `<6.1.0`. oxlint-tsgolint's version prefix must match the app's `typescript` version; upgrade oxlint and oxlint-tsgolint together in the same commit, and update oxlint-tsgolint with TypeScript when that version changes. The rule set and ignore patterns live in [`app/.oxlintrc.json`](../app/.oxlintrc.json), the lint command and limit in [`app/package.json`](../app/package.json), and the shared-gate invocation in [`verify.mjs`](../.agents/skills/verify/scripts/verify.mjs). The same rules cover non-test `eval/` code through the `lint:eval` script, type-checked by `eval/tsconfig.json`; that script runs from the repo root and sets `OXLINT_TSGOLINT_PATH` because oxlint refuses paths containing `..` and looks for oxlint-tsgolint from its working directory. The warning ratchet follows the [verify skill](../.agents/skills/verify/SKILL.md).
- Tests and probes read elements by role, accessible name and ARIA state only; product code carries no test-only attributes.
- Inside the scaled canvas iframe, read geometry with `getBoundingClientRect()` in the frame; Playwright's `boundingBox()` ignores the board scale.

- Electron renders with Chromium, so line breaks and positions match the browsers agents measure with (ego-browser, Playwright) and anchors do not drift; Swift and Tauri would use WebKit on Mac (v2.tauri.app/reference/webview-versions). Accepted cost: several hundred MB.

- **Setup**: devDependencies are pinned exactly (`app/package.json`). `electron` stays a devDependency because packaging tools reject it in `dependencies`; Electron 45 has no postinstall, so its binary downloads on the first `require('electron')`. That `node_modules` binary keeps Electron's default fuses, so dev runs, `app/runtime/electronDom.ts` and Playwright probes of the built `app/` work as before; only the package flips them (§9 'Fuses').
  - Main bundles its dependencies (`externalizeDeps: false`) because Node ESM cannot resolve extensionless subpaths such as `ajv/dist/2020`; `tsconfig` uses `skipLibCheck` because electron-vite's types reference an optional dependency.
  - Exception: `node-pty` (an exactly pinned runtime dependency, used only by the Claude MCP sign-in, §7 'MCP connectors') is externalized (`rolldownOptions.external` in `app/electron.vite.config.ts`) and unpacked from the asar (`asarUnpack` in `app/package.json`), because its native `pty.node` cannot be bundled. It is required through the `app.asar` path, which Electron serves from `app.asar.unpacked`; naming the unpacked path directly fails. electron-builder's default rebuild compiles it from source, so packaging needs the Xcode command line tools, and the rebuilt `spawn-helper` is executable. npm's prebuild ships `spawn-helper` as 0644, which fails with `posix_spawnp failed`, so in an unpackaged tree (dev, Vitest) the loader makes it executable on first use (`mcpConnectors/pty.ts`); a packaged app never changes a file inside its signed bundle and fails the Connect instead.
  - Security defaults: `contextIsolation: true`, `nodeIntegration: false`, `sandbox: true`; the preload is CJS because a sandboxed preload cannot load ESM (methods: `app/src/preload/index.ts`). Subscriptions return an unsubscribe function so dev StrictMode's double effects never stack listeners. IPC handlers accept only the registered main window's main frame (`senderFrame` with `parent === null`) plus the explicit allowlist (§7 'Settings IPC' 'Boundary'); the prototype window's main frame is a second, narrower role (§3 'Prototype window').
  - CSP (`doan-csp` plugin, `app/electron.vite.config.ts`, the main and prototype windows): `default-src 'self'; frame-src http://127.0.0.1:*; img-src 'self' blob:; base-uri 'none'; object-src 'none'; form-action 'none'` (the local server's port changes per launch); dev adds only its dev-server origin. The windows load from `file:`, where `'self'` covers local files, so `<base>`, plugins and form submissions are refused outright. Nothing may need inline sources: Radix ScrollArea (inline `<style>`) and `@vitejs/plugin-react` (inline Fast Refresh preamble) are not used, and stylesheets are `<link>`s in `index.html`, never imported from JS, which Vite dev would inject as `<style>` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25).
  - The session denies every permission request, check and device permission and cancels every download, and so does the prototype window's partition; window navigation, `<webview>` and new windows are blocked.
  - App menu (`app/src/main/index.ts`; enablement `menuState.ts`):
    - Explicit app menu rather than `{role: "appMenu"}`, because the roles would read “doan” ('Names' below).
    - Fit is Cmd+1, not Shift+1, which also fires while typing `!`.
    - Undo/Redo are click items, not roles: for a focused chip field (`data-chip-field`) main dispatches `historyUndo`/`historyRedo` `beforeinput`, else `webContents.undo()`/`redo()`. The chip field keeps its own history, so the roles, acting on Chromium's empty stack, never reached it.
    - View > Hide/Show Sidebar (⌘B): the label follows the renderer's sidebar state (a `menu:state` input). It is disabled under a modal and first run but enabled while a page lock is open, since hiding the sidebar changes no document. A composer (`data-chip-field`) is a contenteditable, and a key the page handles never reaches the menu key equivalent there, so App forwards ⌘B itself: a capture-phase `keydown` listener on the document, limited to chip fields, calls `preventDefault` (so the menu cannot toggle a second time) and toggles unless an App modal is open; it skips IME composition and ⌘B with another modifier. A contenteditable inside the canvas iframe is not forwarded.
    - Text edit menu (PRODUCT §5 'Text edit menu'): `createWindow` registers one `context-menu` listener on the main window's `webContents`, and the prototype window host (`prototypeWindow.ts`) one on that window's (not the thumbnail window); each builds the menu with `editMenu.ts` and calls `Menu.popup({ window })` for its own window. `editMenu.ts` holds the rules and imports nothing from Electron. The canvas, in either window, is a cross-site OOPIF with a parent (`isTopFrame` in `canvasFrame.ts`), so it gets no menu, except that the prototype window host passes `frameFields`, so an editable field in its frame content gets the menu; the field check script runs in the top frame, where the frame content's element is the focused one.
      - Cut/Copy/Paste/Select All are click items calling `webContents.cut()`/`copy()`/`paste()`/`selectAll()`, not roles. These run the same Chromium editing commands as the Edit menu roles, so the chip field gets its usual DOM `copy`/`cut`/`paste` events. A role cannot be guarded: roles and `replaceMisspelling` act on whatever is focused when the item is chosen, so a field removed while the menu is open lets a suggestion or Paste land in another field (seen in the real app). In an editable menu, edit items and suggestions first run a fixed `executeJavaScript` check (only the two finite coordinates interpolated) that `document.activeElement` is or contains `elementFromPoint(params.x, params.y)`; anything else does nothing. Look Up and a non-editable menu's Copy write nothing and are unchecked. Known limit: a different field that takes the old point and focus passes the check. `params.x`/`y` are used as CSS px because zoom stays 1.
      - Click items get their `enabled` from `editFlags` and their hints from `accelerator`; macOS adds icons only to role items, so the items have none.
      - `popup` gets no `frame`: passing it lets macOS add Writing Tools and similar items. Undo/Redo are left out because `editFlags.canUndo` does not know the chip field's own history.
      - A renderer `preventDefault` on DOM `contextmenu` stops Chromium from sending `context-menu`, so the menu would vanish; `app/src` has no such handler.
      - Selection: `body` is `user-select: none`; `input`, `textarea`, `.composer-input`, `.conversation-thread .bubble`, `.conversation-thread .msg-agent` and `.selectable` set `user-select: text` (`textSelection.test.ts`). Other read-only text opens selection only through the `selectable` class, put on the text element itself (a notice's text span, not the notice that also holds its buttons) and never on a button, heading, menu item or control label, so a drag that starts on a control still presses it; Markdown agent text (`.msg-agent`, the handoff card's `.md selectable`) keeps its code blocks' icon-only Copy button inside. Each screen's tests pin where the class goes. Chromium puts `.sr-only` text in `params.selectionText`, so a composer chip's screen-reader kind prefix is `user-select: none` and the Look Up name holds only the chip's visible name; `lookUpLabel` also drops U+200B, the filler before a line-start chip, which JS `\s` does not match. The macOS dictionary panel's own query (`showDefinitionForSelection`) was not observed. `user-select: none` on a disabled `<input>` does not stop selecting its inner text, so disabled fields behave like read-only ones.
    - No zoom, reload or DevTools roles: View's Zoom In/Out, Actual Size, Fit and Reload Canvas send `view-command` to the renderer, which zooms the board and reloads only the canvas, so `webContents` zoom stays 1. Playwright keys do not reach native accelerators, so automated runs click menu items from main.
    - While the prototype window has focus, `menuState`'s `prototypeFocused` turns off every item acting on the main window's canvas, conversation, view or navigation, so a menu key pressed there cannot change the main window behind it; app-level items, Edit and Close Window follow the focused window (§3 'Prototype window').
  - Renderer: React + shadcn/ui (Radix; no Base UI, no Buzz JS hooks) + Tailwind. Canvas-derived colors become style values only through `swatchColor`; other canvas strings never become style values, and text goes in as React text nodes only.
  - Main window: created once; `close` hides it unless `before-quit` set the quitting flag (fullscreen leaves fullscreen first), so Cmd+W never closes a project.
  - Background reveal (`backgroundReveal.ts`, `index.ts`; PRODUCT §5 item 0): with `--doan-launch=background` in argv and `!app.isActive()` at `createWindow`, the window is created with `show: false` and `windowShown` starts false, so `window:visible`, thumbnails and notifications treat it as hidden; a wait for activation is set. `onboarding start` follows `revealFor`: active → show; inactive with a visible window → neither show nor raise, bounce once; inactive otherwise (hidden, minimized, app hidden, or a fullscreen Close Window still waiting to hide) → wait and bounce once per wait (`bounced`, so the `start` right after a background launch still bounces). The wait is released by `did-become-active` (registered at module level; ⌘Tab emits no `activate`), which restores and shows the window, and every other `showWindow` path clears it so a stale wait never shows the window later. `activate` (a Dock click or a reopen) shows the window only while `app.isActive()`: `open -g` to a running app delivers a reopen without activating Doan, and a real Dock click has already made Doan active when `activate` arrives, so an inactive `activate` leaves the window and any wait as they are. Activity is `app.isActive()`, not `getFocusedWindow()`: a frontmost Doan with its window closed has no focused window and gets no `did-become-active`. The shell (watchers, local server) lives until `will-quit`. `webContents.send` is guarded by `isDestroyed()`. Window options are in `index.ts`, appearance in DESIGN.md.
  - Names: `app.name` stays `doan`, keeping userData (`~/Library/Application Support/doan/`, beside `runtime.json`, §4) and Keychain names, while the bundle is `Doan.app` (`productName` `Doan`; CLI at `Contents/Resources/bin/doan`). The menu therefore labels About, Hide and Quit itself, and `index.html`'s `<title>` sets the window title. On case-insensitive APFS `/Applications/doan.app` and `/Applications/Doan.app` are one path, so existing CLI links keep resolving.
  - userData `folders.json` (machine-local, no schema, §11): unreadable or broken → start empty; written through a same-directory temp file and rename; a later write may replace an unreadable file (accepted: only the folder list is lost). A failed write keeps the change in main's memory and sends `folders:save-failed` to the main window, whose Retry toast asks `folders:save-again` to write the list main holds then; the Retry's own failure is its answer, not another push. A failure before the main window exists (the startup rewrite) is only logged.
  - userData `auto-folders.json` (`folderStore.ts`; `string[]` of auto-registered roots, §4 'Auto-registration'): kept apart so `folders.json` stays a plain `string[]` an older app can still read (an older app treats auto-registered roots as hand-added, accepted). Each save writes the unpruned marks, then `folders.json`, then the pruned marks, in the same serialized chain as other folder writes, and a failed mark write skips `folders.json`; an interruption leaves only marks missing from `folders.json`, which are ignored, never an unmarked auto root. Marks are pruned only when `folders.json` read cleanly (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26). An unreadable file reads as no marks and is logged; the next write replaces it. Adding, opening or locating a root by hand (including `duplicate`/`existing` results and a re-root's new path) clears its mark. Remove from Doan needs no mark step: removed roots' marks are pruned on the next write.
  - Root spellings (`openFolderStore` in `folderStore.ts`, its `migrate` step wired in `index.ts`; `rootMigration.test.ts`, `folderStore.interrupt.test.ts`): saved roots and marks are read as their real paths (`realpath`), because registration stores real paths and a run starts only on one, so a root saved through a symlink (by hand, a script or an older app) would otherwise never start. Two spellings of one folder become one entry in the first one's place, and a root that cannot be resolved keeps its saved spelling, so it still shows as missing. When a saved root or mark resolves to another path, one ordered startup step runs before any folder is read, listed or pruned: `canvas-seen.json` gets each alias's record copied to the real path, then `ui-state.json` is rewritten with the alias's memory moved (in both the real path wins per key and the alias fills gaps); then `auto-folders.json` is written without the marks a hand-added spelling clears (skipped when there are none), then `folders.json` with the real paths, then `auto-folders.json` with the resolved marks. Collapsed spellings stay hand-added if any of them was. Any failure, a later Doan's `canvas-seen.json` included, leaves this run on what the files on disk give (memory already moved is read under the spelling this run lists, `FolderStore.spellings`), and the next launch retries; with nothing to move the step writes nothing, so it runs once.
  - Auto-registered roots in the shell (`shell.ts`): entries carry `autoRegistered: true` on every kind. `readFolder` reports the errno; only `ENOENT`/`ENOTDIR` mean gone, and a gone auto root is removed in the same refresh, watcher path included, so it never publishes `notFound`, and removal goes through Remove's root clean-up (`rootsLeft` in `index.ts`: selection, background work, the prototype window, held starts and thumbnails; its work items go with the list). An `uninitialized`, config, CLI or permission failure keeps it registered without the upward re-root a hand-added root takes; the renderer hides non-`list` auto roots (`shownFolders`) but restore, per-root memory and the loading guard read the unfiltered list.

- **CLI lookup and local package**: `cliCommand(env, app.isPackaged, process.resourcesPath)` returns `DOAN_CLI` if set; else, in a packaged app, `<resourcesPath>/bin/doan`, never `PATH`, so another `doan` version cannot break the `init`/`ls` contract; else (development) `doan` on `PATH`.
  - Packaging: `app/package.json` `build` bundles the CLI, the skill stub (§6) and the notices (§9 'Notices'); `app/scripts/package.mjs` stamps `cli.Version` from `package.json`. Output is the unsigned arm64 `app/dist/mac-arm64/Doan.app` (`mac.identity: null`); only the CI signing workflows sign, through command-line overrides (§9).

- **First-run onboarding** (product behavior PRODUCT §5 item 0; requests, replies and codes in `schema/app-socket.v2.schema.json`, §4).
  - Ownership: `OnboardingService` (`app/src/main/onboarding/service.ts`, Electron-free) is the one owner of first-run state for the window and the CLI. The renderer imports only pure modules and never reads the marker or `.git/config`. While first run is open main refuses `openSettingsPage`.
  - Shared project handling: `ProjectAdder` has one instance in `OnboardingService` and one in the Add Projects page's `AddPageSession` (`app/src/main/addPage.ts`). They share `ProjectLocks` — per-parent clone leftovers, re-read after each presence check so neither drops the other's paths, and the clone targets, so a second clone into a target already cloning is `busy`.
  - Completion record (`<userData>/onboarding.json`; `completion.ts`, `completion.test.ts`): only a v1 record with an absolute `projectRoot` ends first run. A newer-version or unreadable file is never overwritten; a corrupt one is copied aside first and kept when the copy fails. Writes use an exclusive 0600 temp file and rename.
  - Session: one per app process. A `requestId` stays bound to its arguments for the session: a resend gets the original answer, refusals included; other arguments are `request_conflict`. `status` keeps only the latest finished operations (`KEPT_FINISHED`) because the CLI caps replies at 64 KiB; older ones stay answerable by id. Messages are fixed strings; agent-auth `detail` and git stderr never reach a reply.
  - Sign-in, check, cancel go through `AuthIpc` with the main window as owner, so agent-auth's owner and attempt checks apply unchanged and a CLI cancel can name only its own operation's attempt. Prompt links skip loopback addresses (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`): Codex prints its local listener before the auth URL.
  - Projects: URL inspection compares registered projects' `.git/config` remotes and never runs git. Selection re-checks the canonical path and remote, registers through the one-path addition decision (`addFlow.ts`) and sets up only with explicit `initialize`, through the bundled `doan init`; a broken config is never initialized. When `ls --repo` on the selected folder fails, an `error` state is `config_invalid`, a `cliError` with `code` is `project_unreadable` and one without is `project_unavailable`. `project_unreadable` carries the CLI's message as `detail` on window IPC only (`OnboardingService.answerWindow`, `onboarding:changed`, the Add Projects page): `OperationBook` keeps it beside the operation and drops it when the operation retires, so socket answers, `status` and replays never carry it, and the v2 schema refuses it.
  - Remotes (`gitConfig.ts`, `repository.ts`, with tests): `.git` is `lstat`ed first and only an absent entry is `not_repository`. Config features Doan does not resolve (`includeIf`, `worktreeConfig`, `insteadOf`, a bad include) make the read `uncertain`, and an uncertain or unreadable read is `unknown`, never a mismatch. User input accepts only the three GitHub root URL forms. Raw remote URLs stay in main.
  - Clone (`clone.ts`, `clone.test.ts`; with `git init` for Keep out of Git (§12 'Storage'), the only git Doan runs, PRODUCT §3 Out of scope 6):
    - `/usr/bin/git` counts only when a developer directory has `usr/bin/git`, checked on disk without `xcode-select`, so the CLT installer never opens.
    - `cloneArgs` turns off hooks, fsmonitor, the `ext` protocol and submodules and puts the URL after `--`; the environment disables every prompt but keeps the user's SSH setup and host-key checking; the process is a detached group with ignored stdin. stderr stays in main.
    - Git clones into a hidden temp sibling, then the destination is claimed with an exclusive `mkdir` and the temp renamed over it. That rename is the commit point for cancel. Failures remove only this run's files; a competitor's stay.
    - Leftovers this run could not remove block the next clone into that parent from either instance until gone; after a restart, folders whose ownership cannot be proven are never deleted.
  - Quit and reload: `closeForQuit()` runs synchronously when quit is accepted, before conversation and auth teardown, so a pending completion ends `cancelled` without writing the record; `reopenAfterAbortedQuit()` reopens it when conversation persistence aborts the quit, and clones are aborted only after `agentAuth.quit()`. A renderer reload, navigation or crash cancels only the Add Projects page's uncommitted clones (`addPage.ts` `rendererGone`); first-run clones keep running; Cmd+W stops nothing.
  - Renderer: setup consent is a token bound to the inspection and path the screen showed; `initialize: true` is sent only with a matching token. The Add Projects page's footer primary (`projectFooter`) carries the inspection id the setup screen was rendered with and the press passes it unchanged, never the one current at the press, so a plan made again meanwhile needs another press. Automatic focus recovery never lands on a `data-consent` button (consent, clone, write).
  - Buddy step: renderer-only (`onboardingScene.ts`); `doan onboarding` and the v2 socket have no buddy step. Every buddy pick, in first run, the buddy screen and Settings > General, goes through App's one `useBuddySelection` (`buddySelection.ts`): it shows the pick at once, saves through the preferences IPC, applies only the latest pick's reply and reads a null reply as a failure (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25 'An older install failure replaced newer feedback'). Only a confirmed Settings > General switch sends a change time (`buddyChangedAt`, in the same patch as the buddy, so both are written or neither), and it shows only once saved; first run, the buddy screen and their Retry send none. The hook also derives the lock's end from the saved time and when this run first read that value, kept per value so a time in the future (the clock set back) locks at most 24 hours from first read (`buddyCooldown.ts`); main does not enforce the lock, because one renderer picks buddies and the lock only means something in Settings. A screen that only shows a buddy saves nothing. Continue and the stepper's forward pills both wait for the pending save, because leaving the step drops its notice and a later failure would revert the accent unannounced. Cover Flow input (offsets, arrows stopping at the ends, wheel deltas accumulated and snapped) is `coverFlow.ts`.
  - Buddy screen (`buddyIntro.ts`): main decides it once at launch, before creating the window, from the completion record, the preferences notice, whether the file had a `buddy` key ('Preferences' below) and whether first run is open. While it is open, main treats it as first run for the menus (`wizardOpen`) and `openSettingsPage`, and App ORs it into its first-run and modal checks (`buddyIntroLock.ts`), so links, `doan open` and notifications get first run's answers. The state lives in main (`buddy-intro:read`), so a reloaded renderer shows the screen again; only `buddy-intro:close`, sent after the save succeeds, releases it.

- **Agent setup** (`app/src/main/agentSetup/`, tests `plan.test.ts`, `runner.test.ts`; product behavior PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Agent setup'; approach from ego lite's onboarding): main runs it once per launch after the login-shell probe; only `about:read` waits for it. HOME is injected and every link value is an absolute path from the canonical HOME.
  - Eligibility (`installEligibility`): packaged, directly in `/Applications` or `<HOME>/Applications`, not translocated; otherwise nothing is written. Versions are not compared, so the copy launched last owns `current`.
  - Layout (`plannedTargets` in `plan.ts`): `~/.local/share/doan/current` links to the bundle's real `Resources` folder, and the skill link, the CLI links and the agents' skill links go through it. `/usr/local/bin/doan` is planned only when the login `PATH` lacks `~/.local/bin`; the Claude, Gemini and Codex targets only when `~/.claude`, `~/.gemini` or `~/.codex` exists (`~/.agents/skills/doan`, for Codex, Cursor and Copilot, always). Only `current` changes when the app moves or is replaced. Provider folders are never created; targets resolving to one real path count once.
  - **Codex rule** (`codexRuleText` in `plan.ts`): one `allow` rule each for `doan ls`, `check`, `active`, `work`, `open`, `skill`, `design-skill` and `help` runs them outside the sandbox without asking, so the app socket is reachable. These are the read and signal commands the guide (`skill.md` 'Commands', 'Design presets') and the skill stub have an agent run without asking the person; `check` also records the session association outside the repository (§4 'Shell check session associations'). Every other command (`init`, `config`, `export`, `handoff`, `onboarding`, `remove`) matches no rule and follows the person's Codex settings: the sandbox, or approval to leave it. There is no rule for bare `doan` and no `prompt` rule. Codex compares a rule's tokens with argv in order, so the CLI refuses any flag or `--` before the command (§12 'Parsing, output and structure').
  - Ownership (`classifyTarget`): a link inside `~/.local/share/doan/` is ours whatever it names; elsewhere a link is ours only when its value is an exact normalized absolute path under `<HOME>/.local/share/doan/`. `/usr/local/bin/doan` is ours only with the exact CLI value, so nothing is ever replaced as root. Connect's old links are recognized (forms in `plan.ts`). `doan.rules` is ours when its content equals `codexRuleText()`. A file that exactly equals an earlier release's text (`EARLIER_CODEX_RULES` in `plan.ts`) is replaced in place through `swapFile` with its mode kept; an edited file is foreign. A test pins the current text, so changing it fails until the released text joins that list. Everything else is foreign and never changed.
  - Dependencies: a skipped or failed target skips targets that depend on it (About names only the cause); a cancelled administrator dialog blocks nothing. The Codex rule and the Claude allow rules need `current`, `~/.local/bin/doan` and a planned `/usr/local/bin/doan`, because they let the `doan` an agent finds on PATH run unsandboxed or unasked.
  - Ancestors: a parent link that resolves to a directory is followed (dotfiles-managed `~/.claude`); a broken, looping or non-directory ancestor makes the target `write-failed`.
  - Writes (`swap.ts`): each target is re-read right before writing and missing targets are created exclusively (the rule via `link` of a staged file, since `link` on a symlink source follows it on macOS). A replacement never renames onto the target: it moves the target aside, verifies it moved what it read in the folder it read (`dev:ino`), then creates the new link exclusively; anything else it moved goes back or, for a folder, stays aside and is reported. A same-user process is outside the threat model.
  - **Claude allow rules** (`CLAUDE_ALLOW_RULES` and `mergeClaudeAllow` in `plan.ts`, `swapFile` in `swap.ts`): the three `doan work` commands and `doan open`, in the syntax of Claude Code's permission docs (wildcard and compound-command rules); every other `doan` command still asks. Claude Code writes the same file, so the merge never rewrites it: the decision parses JSON, but the write inserts only the missing rules into the original bytes (into the last `allow`, else the last `permissions`, else the top-level object, as duplicate keys parse), with a non-recursive scan so deep JSON cannot overflow. Every rule present means no write; a file that has only some gets the missing ones on the next launch. A missing file is created exclusively. The file is read as bytes and decoded strictly; non-UTF-8, invalid JSON or a wrong-shaped `permissions`/`allow` (a `null` included) is refused with an About item (`reason: "claude-settings"` in `notice.ts`). A link is never written through: it is read through only when it resolves to a regular file, and if that file already has every rule nothing is shown. The About line for a link names all four rules. A folder or other non-file is `foreign`.
  - `swapFile` follows `swapLink`'s rule: re-observe right before, move the file aside, continue only if the moved file has the same `dev:ino` and bytes Doan read, publish the merge with exclusive `link(2)`, keep the original mode; a different moved file goes back, and a file that appears at the empty name stays. Either is `changed`, and the next launch merges again. Accepted residual: a writer holding a descriptor opened before the move-aside and writing after the check writes into the aside entry and loses that write; saves that open the path or rename a new file over it are kept at every point.
  - `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` is not consulted, as for `~/.claude/skills/doan`.
  - `/usr/local/bin/doan` is create-only. When the folder is not writable, one administrator dialog runs only SIP-protected `/bin/mkdir` and `/bin/ln` under `/usr/bin/env -i`, never Doan's executable as root. A cancel is recorded in `<userData>/agent-setup.json` and never asked again; a failed login-shell probe plans no system link.
  - A run that throws becomes one `crashed` item (`notice.ts`), so a failure never reads as success.
  - The app never edits shell rc files. Its agent-config writes are its own `~/.codex/rules/doan.rules` (a separate file, since Codex reads every `.rules` file under `rules/` and `default.rules` holds the person's approvals) and the allow rules it inserts into `~/.claude/settings.json`; it edits no other agent config, deletes nothing else and runs no git.
- **App updates** (`app/src/main/update/`; product behavior PRODUCT §5 item 0 'About' 'Updates'; feed §9 'Update feed'): `electron-updater` (exactly 6.8.11, the line whose `builder-util-runtime` matches electron-builder 26.18.0) is a runtime dependency that main bundles like the others (§7 'Setup').
  - Pure rules (every source file but `service.ts`) import neither Electron nor Node; the renderer imports `view.ts`, so it is an `EXTRA_ROOTS` entry of `rendererImports.test.ts` (§7 'Settings IPC' 'Renderer import boundary'). Only `service.ts` touches Electron and the updater, through injected dependencies.
  - Eligibility (`updateEligibility`): packaged, `Contents/Resources/app-update.yml` present, and agent setup's `installEligibility` for this bundle (`setupEligibility` in `agentSetup/runner.ts`, asked synchronously at launch without waiting for setup's login-shell probe). Agent setup's reasons win over a missing feed because About's setup notice already explains them. Only an enabled copy constructs the updater (`StableAwareMacUpdater`, a `MacUpdater` subclass, not the `autoUpdater` getter), so other copies make no update request.
  - Updater settings: `autoDownload` and `autoInstallOnAppQuit` on; `allowPrerelease` only when the running version is a prerelease. A prerelease copy's check reads the feed twice, one read after the other because the provider reads `allowPrerelease` mid-request: once with `allowPrerelease` (its own channel's newest tag) and once without it (the stable release), and takes the newer; equal `x.y.z` cores go to the stable release. Only `ERR_UPDATER_NO_PUBLISHED_VERSIONS` from the first read (no tag of the channel among the feed's newest entries) falls to the stable read; any other first-read error fails the check as before. A failed stable read is logged and the prerelease answers. A stable copy reads once. Tests drive electron-updater's real `GitHubProvider` over a fake GitHub (`service.test.ts`). A launch tick and an unref'd interval ask `shouldCheck` (`CHECK_INTERVAL_MS` in `schedule.ts`, the tick `TICK_MS` in `service.ts`; counted from the last attempt, not the last success, all in memory; off when `updates.autoCheck` is false). A manual check during an automatic one only marks it manual; a check while downloading or ready is ignored. `checkForUpdates()`'s `downloadPromise` is caught, since its failure also arrives as an `error` event.
  - Errors: the renderer gets only `UPDATE_ERROR_CODES` and a source (`native` when the error carries an NSError `domain`, i.e. Squirrel.Mac; `js` otherwise); the full error goes to the main log (`doan: update:`). The GitHub provider wraps a missing release in `ERR_UPDATER_INVALID_RELEASE_FEED` with the whole feed appended, so classification reads only the message before `\nXML:\n`, and only a missing `latest-mac.yml` is `feedMissing`; a missing release is `other`, since that copy would claim a cause. electron-updater emits `update-downloaded` before Squirrel.Mac stages the zip, so in ready, restarting and a stopped restart a native error is a download failure (Retry), a native `installFailed` an install failure, and a js error (a later check) changes nothing.
  - Gate (`installGate`; only conversations hold a restart, PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Updates'): `ConversationService.restartHolds()` counts running turns and unanswered permission and question requests of open turns from memory. Main re-reads it in a microtask after each conversation event and each work end, because the service applies an event's state after its publish callback, and again at every press.
  - Install order: a press re-reads the gate; blocked answers `{kind: "blocked"}`. Otherwise the state becomes restarting. When Squirrel.Mac has already staged the download the quit starts at once; else the service waits up to `RESTART_STAGE_WAIT_MS` for Squirrel.Mac's native `update-downloaded`, re-asking the gate when it arrives (blocked returns to ready), and a wait that runs out ends in a stopped restart; `MacUpdater.quitAndInstall` would wait forever for a staging that failed, so nothing is torn down before staging. After a staging failure a press calls `downloadUpdate()`, which reuses the cached zip and stages again. Then `installPending` and `app.quit()` run the ordinary `before-quit` → `quitInOrder` (conversations saved, state flushed, `quitting` true so the window closes instead of hiding), and its last step is `quitAndInstall()` instead of `app.quit()`. A save that cancels the quit installs nothing and ends in a stopped restart. If the install has not quit Doan after `INSTALL_QUIT_WAIT_MS` or the updater reports an error during it, the service calls `app.relaunch()` and quits, since everything is already torn down; `autoInstallOnAppQuit` retries on the next quit. Order tests: `index.test.ts`.
  - IPC (main window only, `senderAllowed.ts`): `update:get` and the `update:state` event carry `UpdateSnapshot`; `update:check`; `update:install` answers `{kind: "allowed"}`, `{kind: "blocked", reason}` or `null` when nothing is installable; `update:openNotes` opens `https://github.com/SinhyeokKang/doan-design/releases/tag/v<version>` built by main from the snapshot's plain semver version; `update:popover` asks the shown header to open its popover after a blocked menu restart. Preload's `onUpdate` subscribes before reading, and the first read is delivered only when no event came first.
  - Header popover (`UpdateHeaderButton.tsx`): `header()` gives `popover`, what a press opens (null when ready and unblocked, so the press restarts), and `opened`, what an already open popover says now (`popover`, else "Ready to restart"; null while restarting). A push normally replaces only an open popover's words. One that follows an observed restart with a stopped or blocked restart opens the popover only through the shown header; an initial read in that state does not open it (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-08). The words sit in a polite live region. Every popover over the header (this one and Agent usage) holds the header's no-drag through `useHeaderNoDrag` (`headerNoDrag.ts`): each header element keeps a set of open owners, and only the last release (a close, or an unmount while open) removes the inline `-webkit-app-region`. It is set inline because Electron keeps a drag region changed only through selectors (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-06).
  - Release Notes failure (`SettingsAbout.tsx`): `update:openNotes` answering false or rejecting shows the line from `releaseNotesFailedLine` and calls the page's `announce()`. Every press and every change of the state's `kind` or version bumps a request number, and only the latest request's answer is shown.
  - The automatic-check switch is `updates.autoCheck` in preferences (§7 'Settings IPC' 'Preferences', default true); main forwards a saved value to the service. Copy details adds `Updates: <state>, last checked <ISO 8601 UTC | never>`.
  - Menu: Doan > Check for Updates… / Restart to Update under About Doan, label and action from `updateView(...).menu`, enabled with Settings… (`updateMenu` in `menuState.ts`).
- **GitHub star card** (product behavior PRODUCT §5 'GitHub star card'): main computes `updateEligibility(...)` once (`releaseEligibility` in `index.ts`) and passes it to both UpdateService and `createStarPrompt` (`starPromptStore.ts`), so only a copy that checks for updates counts, writes or asks; any other copy gets a store that does nothing.
  - Pure rules (`starPrompt.ts`: due, after an answer, the 3-day clamp, reading the state) import neither Electron nor Node. The store reads `<userData>/star-prompt.json` once and writes it synchronously with temp + rename; an unreadable file, a newer `v`, an invalid field or a failed write turns the card off for that run and no card is shown. Read and write failures are logged.
  - Counting: the conversation publish callback calls `starPrompt.event(event)`; only `turn.done` counts. That callback sees only saved events and a turn has one terminal event, so a turn counts once. Turns that end in the same task send one `star:moment` (`{ second }`, main decides which card). After done nothing is counted or written.
  - IPC (main window only, `senderAllowed.ts`, all argument-free): `star:open` opens `REPOSITORY_URL` (`repository.ts`, which imports nothing so the renderer can show it; `update/service.ts` builds release pages from it and a test pins it to `app/package.json` `build.publish`) and records done when `openExternal` succeeds; a failure changes nothing. `star:dismiss` records Not now or Don’t ask again. `star:busy` answers whether `ConversationService.restartHolds()` holds a turn or an answer, since the renderer's lists are per root; a rejected read counts as busy.
  - Renderer: `StarPrompt.tsx` sits right after the toast element in App, so it is the last Tab stop; nothing reachable by Tab may follow it. On a moment it waits `STAR_IDLE_MS` of no key or pointer input, then `starPromptMoment` decides from window visibility and focus, first run, an open menu, list, popover or dialog (`overlayOpen` in `canvasTool.ts`, the tool keys' rule), `star:busy` and pick or capture; a skipped moment waits for the next. Esc is handled only on the card's own keydown. The visible words are `aria-hidden`; an sr-only `role="status"` inside the card receives them after mount (a live region that mounts with its text is not read) and, after a failed open, the words plus the failure line. Capture snapshots hide the card like the toast.
- **Login-shell probe** (`probeShell`, `app/src/main/onboarding/machine.ts`; shared by agent setup, agent-auth tool facts, conversations and clone): the login shell prints only `PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME` and `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION` between random nonce lines, stdin ignored. It ends on the end nonce or `exit`, not `close`, because a daemon the shell started can hold stdout. Executables are found by scanning that `PATH` in Node, not with `command -v`, because zsh prints alias definitions. Timeout and output cap: `machine.ts`, pinned by `machine.test.ts`.

- **Onboarding and project preload contract**: the renderer sends enums, opaque IDs and stored roots, with explicit exceptions for validated folder drops and `removeServe` paths present in main's last config read; main builds paths, commands and clipboard strings. Copy methods report success only after `clipboard.writeText` settles.

### Settings IPC

- **Pages:** the app has one main window, with one renderer entry (`index.html`) and one preload (`window.doan`); the only other page is the prototype window's (`prototype.html`, preload `window.doanPrototype`, §3 'Prototype window'), and the capture window has neither. Settings is a page of the main window (`SettingsPage.tsx`), and a worktree's `.doan/config.json` is edited on the Project Settings page (`ProjectSettingsPage.tsx`, rules in `projectSettingsRules.ts`). Product rules: PRODUCT §5 item 0.
- **Navigation:** pages keep an origin and every exit resolves through `addPageReturn` (`app/src/renderer/addProjectsNav.ts`). Every way into Settings goes through App's one `openSettings`, and into Project Settings through `openProjectSettings`; main's `openSettingsPage` shows a hidden window first. A Project Settings page whose worktree leaves the list returns through the `gone` event, which passes the lock. Picking another worktree on the page (`projectSettingsReentry`) mounts a new page instance and keeps the origin; a reroot is followed inside the same instance, and App moves its root and the origin with it (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25 'Locating a project kept the old root's selection').
- **Lock and incoming targets:** while General owns a buddy-confirmation dialog, Agents owns a `stop_required` dialog, MCP Connectors owns an Add or Remove dialog, or the Project Settings page holds a picker, its Share or Create a Git repository confirmation, or a write, App's single `locked()` check turns navigation into a no-op. On the Settings page outside targets wait in `SettingsTargetQueue` (latest wins) and apply on unlock; on a locked Project Settings page they are dropped, as on a locked Add Projects page. Categories never handle targets, so main's operation never outlives the UI that answers it (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25). The Project Settings page reports its lock to App, which also holds the Undo toast while it is locked, since the page is not a modal (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25 'Native hiding bypassed renderer-only lifetime checks'). Main's menu state treats the page as `screen: "settings"` with that lock.
- **No conversations from Settings:** Settings shares the main window's channels, so "Settings never calls `startConversation` or `sendConversation`" is a renderer convention pinned by tests (`App.settings.test.tsx` and the category tests' stubs), not an IPC boundary.
- **Boundary:** `handle` in main validates the registered main window's webContents, main-frame identity and `senderAllowed`. Preload exposure is not authorization. Unknown channels, subframes, the thumbnail window and all other windows are refused; the prototype window's main frame gets only its own allowlist (§7 'Prototype window IPC'). The invoke allowlist is explicit: `app/src/main/senderAllowed.ts`, except the conversation channels (`conversationMethods` in `conversation/ipc.ts`), which `index.ts` registers itself and gates by sender role (§7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'IPC contract'). Main fixes every URL, clipboard string and notification text; the renderer sends enums, choices and requests.
- **Links:** `setup:open-external` and `notifications:open-settings` open only main's fixed URLs (`settings.ts`), and `setup:open-install` opens only a script main writes from its fixed command table (`installCommand.ts`, §7 'Install and Update commands'). The one renderer URL is `shell:openLink` for links in agent replies (`app/src/main/openLink.ts`): main reparses it and opens only `https:` within `MAX_LINK_LENGTH`. `update:openNotes`, `star:open` and `repository:open` (Home's credit line, which leaves the star card's record alone) take no URL either: main builds them from `REPOSITORY_URL`. New windows stay denied.
- **Config bridge** (`app/src/main/configBridge.ts`): `doan config --json` is the only config writer; reads, picks and writes require a stored healthy root. A pick returns a one-use choice bound to root, kind, epoch and page session; removal builds its path from main's last read, never the renderer's string; serve addition confirms the read scope first. Calls serialize per root and recheck epoch/session before running and before caching; a page session change (each page instance starts one on mount and ends it on unmount) or a replaced renderer clears the cache, so later replies answer `unavailable`.
- **Storage writes** (PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files'; CLI side §12 'Storage'): Project Settings writes the mode with `doan config --storage` alone through the config bridge, under the page lock; every setup path passes `--storage local` to `init` only for a local plan (`initArgs`, `plannedChanges`, `CLONE_CHANGES`), and `--git-init` only when the plan the person saw listed `.git/` (the Set up form's own list, the consent token's inspection; Project Settings after its confirmation). The plan lists `.git/` only for an `ls` `repo_not_found` with `error.storageReason: "not_git"` (`FolderState` `notGit`) below HOME, compared as real paths (`homeOrAbove`, `app/src/main/storage.ts`; `app:home` returns HOME's real path). A `--git-init` run gets the login shell's `PATH` and the inherited `DEVELOPER_DIR` (`cliEnv`, `app/src/main/cli.ts`), the sources clone uses, so the CLI finds the same git; when the login shell cannot be read it gets an empty `PATH` and answers `git_missing`, as clone does. A failed `--git-init` setup re-reads the folder or group before showing the error. A clone into a repository that already has a config applies Keep out of Git with `doan config --storage local` after the clone. A successful storage write from any path re-reads the whole group (`groupRoots` in `app/src/main/storage.ts`: the card's `git.common` plus the worktrees a listed root names), so every worktree's Project Settings shows the new mode (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-03 'Locate on the Project Settings page…'); opening Project Settings re-reads its group too (`config:session-start` carries the root). An open page re-reads its config when its root's listed `storage` or `storageReason` changes, quietly (controls stay enabled, so focus is kept), and a change that arrives during its own write is read after the write, success or failure. A folder not set up yet has no mode of its own; `projectKeepsLocal` takes it from a listed root of the same repository and root path that names the folder in `git.worktrees`, so TypeScript still reads no Git metadata. The exclude is in the Git common directory, outside the folder watcher, so a change made outside the app shows on these re-reads only (BACKLOG).
- **Project Settings page:** Set up opens `ProjectSetupDialog` over the page; when it closes, even from outside, focus returns through `modalClosed` → `focusAfterModal` → `focusCurrent` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-24). Focus guards recognize an open confirmation by role (`dialog[role="alertdialog"]`), not a class (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30).
- **Preferences** (`app/src/main/preferences.ts`): only TypeScript reads and writes `<userData>/preferences.json`, so `preferences.test.ts` is its schema. Main reads it once, synchronously, and keeps the snapshot, because notifications read the switches on every conversation event. Writes are validated patches, serialized and written by temp file plus rename; a wrong known field breaks the whole file rather than half-applying (except an unknown `permissionMode`, §7 'Permission modes', and an unknown or non-string `buddy`, which reads as absent because a later build may add buddies and is dropped by the next write; and a `buddyChangedAt` that is not exactly what `toISOString` writes, which also reads as absent because losing it only lifts the buddy lock while a broken file would reset every other preference), and a newer `v` is never written. Patches accept only known buddy ids, and a `buddyChangedAt` only beside a `buddy`; a patch without it keeps the saved one. The store also reports whether the file had a `buddy` key at all (`buddyKey()`, an unknown id included, as read or as last written), which decides the buddy screen (§7 'First-run onboarding'). There is no change event, since Home and Settings share one renderer.
- **Notifications** (`notifications.ts`, `notifyService.ts`): visibility is composed from focus, shown, minimized and app hidden, since Electron may report a hidden window focused. Main holds one Notification per conversation because macOS delivers no click for a collected one. A click sends `notifications:click`; the renderer resolves it with `notificationTarget`. An agent question is an `approval` notification, so the Approval requests switch gates it, with its title chosen by event type; `question.resolved` closes it as `permission.resolved` closes an approval. The development build is always refused; packaged builds depend on the bundle's code identity (BACKLOG.md).
- **About** (`about.ts`): `about:read` uses AuthIpc's cached tool facts, so no provider CLI runs, and waits for this launch's agent setup; main and renderer share `aboutNotice`. `about:copy` builds the text in main.
- **App-level sign-in controller** (`app/src/renderer/agentSignIn.ts`): one per renderer, holding the `agent-auth:prompt` subscription, held prompts and the attempt ids this renderer owns (sessionStorage, surviving a reload; only those links open automatically). Main sends a prompt to its owner window once and a sign-in outlives the screen that started it, so this state cannot live in a component.
- **Renderer import boundary:** the renderer CSP has no `unsafe-eval`, and `app/src/formats/validate.ts` compiles ajv schemas with `new Function` on load. A renderer value import that reaches it, or a Node builtin, leaves the built window blank while unit tests, which run without the CSP, still pass. Main modules the renderer imports keep shapes and pure rules in validator-free files, and `app/src/renderer/rendererImports.test.ts` walks the value-import closure of every renderer page's entry (`index.html`, `prototype.html`). It follows `new Worker(new URL("…", import.meta.url))` too: the dot field's module Worker (`dotFieldWorker.ts`) loads under the same CSP (§7 'Setup'), and its own closure is pinned to `dotFieldWorker.ts` and `dotField.ts`.
- **Project addition:** first run and the Add Projects page read a dropped folder with preload's `webUtils.getPathForFile` and inspect it like a picked folder. `add-projects:*` channels are main-only and checked by `addPageRequest` (`app/src/main/addPageRequest.ts`).
- **New folder:** `onboarding:new-folder` takes `{parent, token}`. `parent` is only where the save panel opens (anything but an existing absolute folder falls back to HOME); main opens the panel, judges and makes the folder at the path the panel returns (`newFolderTarget` in `app/src/main/onboarding/projects.ts`: inside a set-up project, then an existing item, then a non-recursive `mkdir`, so nothing is ever replaced) and registers nothing; the renderer inspects a made folder like a chosen one. The step cancels its token through `onboarding:new-folder-cancel` when it goes, main drops a renderer's pending tokens when it reloads or is gone, and main checks the token after the panel returns and again before `mkdir`, so a late Create makes nothing. Its outcomes stay renderer-side errors, not `OnboardingOperationErrorCode`s, so the socket schema and the CLI do not change. Both channels are main-only.
- **Window visibility:** main-only `window:visible` reports native show/hide and app hide, since `document.hidden` misses native hides.
- **Window fullscreen:** main → renderer `window:fullscreen` (`{fullScreen}`) on `enter-full-screen`/`leave-full-screen`, plus an invoke of the same name for the current value (the event wins if it arrives first); App mirrors it as `data-fullscreen` on the root. It is the window's OS state, never saved.
- **Menu enablement** per screen, modal and lock is `menuState` (`app/src/main/menuState.ts`).

### Home, workspace and project IPC

- **Screens:** Home, `workspace(root)`, Add Projects and Settings share one window (PRODUCT §5 item 1; docs/design/onboarding.md 'Projects home'). While `leaveLocked` holds, App navigation is a no-op and in-flight navigation is superseded.
- **Memory:** at most one workspace is loaded. Home and the pages hide it with `visibility: hidden` and `inert`, never `display: none`, so iframe dimensions do not trigger the height freeze (§3); canvas, viewport, selection and draft survive and `doan active` still reports the selection. Removed roots are pruned from navigation memory and never recover it on re-add. A problem root enters a workspace only through its Overview (`CanvasController.openWorkspace(..., overview = true)`); a canvas opens only on a `list` root, and a `loading` root is waited for before either judgment.
- **Problem kinds:** `folderState` (`folderState.ts`) makes a failed `ls` an `error` entry (shown as Config error) only when its envelope carries `error.config` (§12), whatever the code, and keeps that path on the entry. Every other non-zero failure envelope except `repo_not_found` is a `cliError` with the envelope's `code`, which main alone reads as "Go answered for this folder" (Add, Locate and first run save such a folder as they save an `error` one); a run that did not answer with such an envelope is a `cliError` without `code`.
- **Problem reasons:** a `cliError` entry's `reason` is never empty: `cliReason` (`folderState.ts`) takes the failure envelope's message (an exit-0 one, or a non-zero one without `error.config`), else stderr's first non-blank line, else a short cause of the run (spawn error, timeout worded from `CLI_TIMEOUT_MS`, exit code, signal, unreadable answer). Details for `error` and `cliError` render the text in a `<pre>` or a toast, as text only (§3). The sidebar row, the Overview recovery menu and Project Settings take their actions from `cardRow` (`homeCards.ts`).
- **Problem overlay** (`ProblemOverlay.tsx`, drawn from the pure `problemView.ts`): a z-index 5 layer in `.workspace`, which is not a stacking context, so it sits above the panel handles (4) and under toasts (6) and portalled menus and tooltips (50); the Home conversation strip rises to 6 over it. `inert` on the covered content does not stop window key listeners or the app menu, so while it shows App also drops the covered screen from the menu state, turns off `toolPage` and `onViewCommand`, and sets `CanvasController.setCovered`, which stops every canvas key including Esc's clearing of the selection. Its inert layout effect runs before the effect that moves focus when the overlay goes, or the composer still refuses focus. It keeps its own React key space beside the root-keyed Overview (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-07).
- **Main boundary:** every invoke here requires the registered main window's main frame (§7 allowlist); New folder's `parent`: §7 'Settings IPC' 'New folder'. Renderer-sent project paths (chosen folder, drop, candidate, clone destination) enter only through first run's or the page's inspect, select and clone, where main checks them with `canonicalFolder` and the inspection generation and accepts a candidate only from its own inspection. Confirmation accepts only main-issued opaque IDs; reveal, locate and remove accept stored roots. No renderer-provided path is used to construct a config path.
- **Add:** registration is the serialized one-path decision (`app/src/main/addFlow.ts`, `addProjects.ts`). `folders:open` keeps its root-or-null contract for runtime tests.
- **Removal:** the renderer asks first (`removalConfirm`, `homeConversations.ts`); `folders:remove` accepts only stored roots. Immediately before removing registrations, main checks the current group for a group request and checks each root for active project work, including an unindexed first send. A changed group needs fresh confirmation. If a registered root is still loading and its group is unknown, whole-group removal waits for a retry. A busy root keeps the registrations. A removal interrupt names the conversation and its live project turn. Its acknowledgement can precede runner exit or binding judgement. The service shares the close attempt with Retry and waits for the process reservation and project work to settle, bounded by its exit wait plus binding grace. A timeout leaves the group registered. Success returns `{ kind: "removed" }` with no toast or Undo state. It and `doan remove` (§4 'Removing a project over app-socket v7') both go through `removeRoots` in `index.ts`, whose `rootsLeft` (shared with a vanished auto-registered worktree, §7 'Auto-registered roots in the shell') clears the selection, stops removed projects' background work, closes the prototype window on their canvases, discards their held starts and drops their thumbnails, so state keyed by a removed root cannot outlive it on one path only (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25). `doan remove` also sends the main-window event `folders:removed {paths}`; the renderer then forgets those roots exactly as after its own Remove (`afterRemoved.ts`, idempotent, so roots it already forgot set no state).
- **Recovery:** `folders:locate` only replaces stored roots; `folders:reveal` takes a stored root only. For a root whose entry is `error` (shown as Config error), it selects the config path the CLI named in `error.config` when that path exists, file or directory, and otherwise shows the root (`revealTarget`, `view.ts`). The path may be a parent folder's config, reached through the upward re-read. Main only `lstat`s it and never reads it or builds a config path itself (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-22). `app:home` returns the real path of main's HOME (process HOME first, for isolated runs), so `~` shortening and `homeOrAbove` compare against stored real-path roots. `folders:reopen` (Retry) re-reads only a stored root.
- **Menus:** a missing `menu:state` value is false and an invalid one keeps the last valid state.
- **Toasts:** one `role="status"` surface whose timer counts only time visible natively and in the document. While no toast shows its clock dispatches no tick, so App does not render at rest (a `useReducer` dispatch re-runs the component even when the state is unchanged). Wall-clock text computed while rendering (relative times, a time of day) follows App's one-second `useVisibleClock` instead, which stops while the window is hidden natively or in the document and refreshes once when shown. `toast.showIn(dialog, message)` (a blocked link, with App's `topDialog()`) portals an actionless toast into that open modal dialog, the only part of the page that is not inert, at the window's corner (`position: fixed`), and an App modal does not hold its timer there. Once that dialog closes or leaves the document the toast returns to its usual place, where the modal holds it again, and its hover and focus pauses are read again from the new element. The prototype window's own toast is held the same way while its delete alert is open.
  - Motion (shared with the GitHub star card, `--shadow-floating`): the toast element is keyed by `slot`, which changes only when a toast appears where none was, so a replaced toast keeps its element and live region and does not move again; only its action button is keyed by toast id. A gone toast stays `leaving` for `FLOATING_EXIT_MS` (120, pinned to `--time-fast`) as `data-closing` + `inert`, so tests and selectors can still find `.toast` then; a new show removes it at once. Focus returns through the unmounting button's ref cleanup (`restoreFloatingFocus` in App), which runs while the button still holds focus.
- **Home composer:** rules in `app/src/renderer/homeComposer.ts`; components are `HomeComposerView.tsx` and `ConversationStripView.tsx` because macOS's case-insensitive resolution sent `./ConversationStrip` to `conversationStrip.ts`. The composer copies the new-conversation default while its input is empty and keeps it until sent, so a Settings change never retargets an input already started; the copy stays `undefined` until preferences are read.
- **Home conversations:** `homeRun.ts` (`HomeRun`, subscribed at App mount) subscribes before `readConversation`, merges by seq and buffers unclaimed events until a start reply claims them, so nothing is lost before a reply or the first read; the merge is main's own `mergeEvents` (`state.ts`, which has only type imports), imported by value. State comes from main's conversation list (§7 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'List and seen'):
  - at most one read per root is in flight, and changes during a read schedule one more, so an event burst never costs a read per event; no order is assumed between replies and change notices;
  - after `markConversationSeen` a conversation stays locally seen until a list read started after the reply; a failure undoes that;
  - restored attachments keep main's ids; the composer rechecks them and releases unused ones;
  - main binds a new canvas only after the turn, so the renderer opens it itself (PRODUCT §5 item 1): while the turn runs, and after it until a list read started after the end shows the conversation no longer `working` (main's judgement), since a read started before the end may predate it. Its `BIND_GRACE_MS` copy bounds that wait and is pinned to main's by `homeRun.test.ts`, since the renderer cannot import `project.ts`.
- **Project Overview conversations:** `overviewRun.ts` (`OverviewRun`, owned by App) holds each root's main conversation; threads, drafts and queues live in `ConversationStore`, shared with the canvas conversation panel; choices are pure in `overviewConversation.ts`. Threads subscribe before reading and merge by seq.
  - The automatic pick waits for both the root's list and ui-state, so no new-conversation state shows and then changes; a list refresh never re-picks.
  - A start or send reply carries the root's revision from before the call and never replaces a pick, New or entry request made since (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26).
  - On promotion the conversation is marked seen through the `conversation.bound` seq only when that turn's end was already marked, in either arrival order.
  - Copy uses `copyText.ts` (a `copy` listener plus `document.execCommand("copy")`): main denies every permission check, so the async Clipboard API is refused, and main never writes a renderer-named string, so there is no preload copy channel.
- **Canvas conversation panel:** `CanvasConversationPanel.tsx`; `canvasRun.ts` (`CanvasRun`) keys state by canvas ID, else path, so a moved canvas keeps it.
  - A start reply carries the canvas revision from before the call, and every select, New and entry request moves it (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26).
  - Send waits only while the terminal session lookup is pending; a new conversation's send does not wait for the root's list, because a start leaves the canvas's other conversations alone.
  - Each window has its own `CanvasRun`, so the prototype window can show another conversation than the main window's panel. The pick is `pickShownConversation` (`canvasConversation.ts`), shared by the first pick, a deletion of the shown conversation and the sidebar canvas row (`sidebarRowRequest` names a slot's conversation first). A list read keeps what is shown, and the first pick waits for the host's `remembered` (ui-state's `conversationByCanvas`, `undefined` until read). Only the main window's host has `remember`, called from the Seen check, because ui-state has one writer. Following a new conversation (`newConversationFollow.ts`) runs only from the `defaultNew` state, for summaries without `startedBy` that `defaultNew` did not list and for `startedBy: "promotion"` ones whether listed or not (a promoted conversation was listed as a project conversation, and one already on the canvas would have been picked instead of the new state), never for a handoff or an unreadable history, and never moves a draft whose start is in flight (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-14).
  - While the automatic New selection previews a drawing conversation, the main window tracks that thread in the Overview’s shared store. The preview leaves the underlying canvas selection, draft and seen marker intact until a list confirms the binding. The prototype host receives only the drawing ID and cannot preview the project thread.
  - The board follows one conversation (`boardPresence`, `agentPresence.ts`), keyed by root (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01); App passes the panel's pick, also while folded. The folded Show conversation mark (`canvasStrongestSignal`, `rightPanelOpen.ts`) is a separate choice over every conversation of the canvas. The board notice has no Stop; only a conversation's composer stops its turn. `CanvasController.setWork` keeps the last shown work source per canvas across a moment with no picked work, and forgets it once no work turn is left on the canvas, so a switch to another conversation still counts as a change of source and does not swing the view, while a later separate work turn still follows its first frame.
  - Entry arrivals carry an ever-growing sequence (`panelArrivals`), so a repeated entry still focuses. A pending pick or Add carries a `FocusIntent` that a `focusin` elsewhere drops for good; a composing field gets its chip without any focus or caret call (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-23). Chip notes are overlays, so the field's DOM is never touched.
- **Home tests without a DOM:** no DOM test dependency (jsdom or similar) is added, because the app's devDependencies are pinned ('Setup' above) and dependencies are not added without a request. App's Home flow lives in controller modules that App calls, and tests drive them end to end with a fake `window.doan`; components are checked as static markup with mocked handlers. Real focus movement (including Radix focus return), OS paste, drag and drop, `field-sizing` growth and popover positions are not covered by these tests and are checked only by short real-app probes (`/runtime-test`).
  - The scripts injected into canvases (`prototypeScript.ts`, `canvasScript.ts`) need a real DOM for their drift tests (the prototype-link fixtures and the click table, §11), so those Vitest files launch the app's own Electron binary through `app/runtime/electronDom.ts` (Playwright `_electron`, one hidden window, a temporary user data folder, no network) instead of adding a DOM package. The binary must already be downloaded; a missing one fails the test rather than skipping it, and CI downloads it after `npm ci` (§9 'CI gate'). One launch serves a test file, and `close()` confirms the process exited.
- **Buddy (mascot):** product behaviour in PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Buddy'. `app/src/renderer/buddies.ts` is the one list of buddies (names, lines, asset paths, rig numbers, working-notice variants, default and first-run orders); the screens read it and the saved preference and never branch per buddy. Doany's motion files stay in `public/doany/`, because the golden trace, `doanyRender.test.ts`, `.doan/config.json` and canvases name those paths; every other buddy file is in `public/buddies/`, whose sizes, frames and size cap `buddyAssets.test.ts` checks (the Welcome group photo in `public/buddies/group/` is outside the cap). Sources and provenance: `.doan/assets/buddies/README.md`.
