  - The draft `.doan/assets/buddies/motion-preview.js` is the source of truth for the Home rig: all five buddies' shaders, layer anchors and the time model (breathing, blinks, gaze, hop, accessory spring); its code is MIT, the artwork is not (`public/doany/README.md`). `doany.golden.json` holds its outputs (the idle WebGL trace and the motion logs), compared exactly by tests. `node app/scripts/doanyGolden.mjs` regenerates the golden from the draft and `--check` fails when they differ, so a draft change is followed by a regeneration; the shared gate runs this check and the docked buddy's. The generator runs the draft with Doany's drawn bounds lifted, so the logs pin the motion model and `drawnPose` owns the bounds.
  - Rig numbers go into the shader source as strings in the draft's notation and each buddy compiles its own program, rather than becoming uniforms, so the app's source stays byte-identical to the draft and `doanyRender.test.ts` reads the anchors from it. The friends' eye and anchor checks run on the 1254² sources in `.doan/assets/buddies/<id>/`, because the 512² app copies fall below the checks' pixel thresholds.
  - Every buddy draws the shared `buddies/eye.png` as the `eyeTex` sampler on texture unit 1, bound before each draw; the bind order is part of the golden trace. Body, accessory and eye load together, and any failure falls back to the still image.
  - The app bounds only what it draws (`Doany.tsx` `drawnPose`): each buddy's lift scale and accessory tilt range from the registry, because an accessory can reach the canvas top at the apex (Doany's beret with the pointer far left) and press bursts push it past; `doanyRender.test.ts` sweeps every press sequence of every buddy for a 1.4 device px margin. The rest pose is unchanged, so the rest frame still equals the draft. A held Enter or Space does not re-hop.
  - The contact shadow is an `<img>` outside WebGL, so it stays on the floor while the body hops. The rig never touches the DOM: it passes the hop lift through `DoanyView`'s lift setter and the component writes it to the slot's `--hop-lift` (declared on `:root` in `styles.css` for the `cssVarUsage` gate), back to 0 on context loss, the still image and reduced motion.
  - `doanyRig.ts` gates time on `checkVisibility({ visibilityProperty: true })`, among others, because Home under the first-run overlay is `visibility: hidden`, not off-screen. `Doany.tsx` folds native visibility (`onWindowVisible`) into that check, because a window closed with the app running, or a hidden app, keeps `document.hidden` false while Chromium still runs its frames.
  - A move to a display of another scale changes `devicePixelRatio` without any CSS resize, so Home's buddy, the docked buddy and the agent indicator's dot field also size their canvases again on a `(resolution: <dpr>dppx)` media change (`devicePixels.ts`). A `device-pixel-content-box` ResizeObserver did not fire for a scale change emulated over CDP.
  - `Buddy` remounts (`key={buddy}`) when the buddy changes. Each mount draws on a clone of React's canvas and releases its WebGL context on dispose: StrictMode's double mount would reuse a lost context, and Home remounts every visit, which would reach Chromium's context limit. A buddy that had drawn and then loses its context is mounted again (`keepBuddy`) once the GPU is back, as the docked buddy is rebuilt: on `webglcontextrestored`, or, when none comes, after waits doubling from 1 s, at most 8 tries. One that never drew stays on the still image until Home mounts it again.
  - `hop()` runs after `send()`'s availability guard, so a refused or pending send never hops.
  - **Docked buddy** (PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Docked buddy'). Its own draft core, `globalThis.dockedBuddyCore` in `.doan/assets/buddies/docked-poc.js` (no DOM), is the source of truth for its time model and surface state machine; the Home draft, rig and golden stay untouched. `dockedBuddyCore.ts` ports it (`advanceDocked(state, inputs, now, random)`, time and randomness as arguments) and `dockedBuddy.golden.json` replays it bit for bit; `node app/scripts/dockedBuddyGolden.mjs [--check]` regenerates or checks it and also fails when the draft's base shader template differs from `motion-preview.js`. `dockedBuddyShader.ts` is the Home template plus the foot pin, inserted at one anchor; it throws unless the anchor occurs exactly once, because a silent miss would compile the Home shader.
    - Ownership: `dockedBuddyShell.ts` holds one buddy for the app. App starts it while `screen === "workspace"`, with `<main>` as the stable container, and stops it when the workspace screen goes, which disposes the rig and releases the WebGL context. One imperative ground node (shadow, 88 px clip window, 56 px host with one WebGL canvas and the still image) moves between empty React slots (`.docked-buddy-slot` in the docked composer card and `CanvasTools`) by `append`, so the context survives moves and React never touches it; slot ref callbacks are idempotent for StrictMode's double calls. While the Overview is open the toolbar is not a surface, because the canvas page stays mounted under it. A buddy change rebuilds only the rig; `setDockedBuddy` keeps the surface, places and hiding.
    - Time: the shell runs its own `requestAnimationFrame` and never depends on App renders (App renders at most once a second while idle and not at all while hidden). App pushes inputs only when they change; the composer writes its reasons to step aside as slot `data-*` attributes, watched by a MutationObserver. While it runs on its own it advances at most about 60 frames a second (a self-continued frame sooner than one 60 Hz interval less 4 ms is skipped; an input change applies on the next callback), so a 120 Hz display does not double its cost; the core still gets every real timestamp. The loop parks while the document is hidden or the window is not natively visible (`onWindowVisible`; a closed window or hidden app keeps `document.hidden` false), the container fails `checkVisibility` with `opacityProperty` (the first-run overlay hides siblings with opacity), the workspace is covered, motion is reduced, or `dockedNeedsTime` says nothing is left to animate; any input change advances it once. A wake cancels a pending frame and requests a fresh one, so a callback that never arrives cannot park the loop. A rig that lost its context or failed to build (no WebGL, a shader, a texture) shows the still image and is rebuilt once per stretch the workspace is seen; without that the still image would stay until the app restarts. When a rig that was ready loses its context, the rebuild waits for the GPU to come back: on `webglcontextrestored`, or, when none comes, after waits doubling from 1 s, at most 8 tries (stop and a buddy change cancel them). A rebuild at once would meet a GPU still restarting, and two GPU resets in a row make Chromium refuse WebGL to the page for about two minutes with no restore event.
    - Floating elements are judged by geometry, not state, because the effort and permission menus and tooltips expose no state: the rects of `[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]`, `.slash-popover` and `.doan-picking` against the 56 px box. The node list is cached and found again on a body child change, a slot attribute change, a hint change or a cached node leaving the page.
    - The body takes clicks through a transparent hit element clipped (`clip-path`) to the still image's opaque pixels, so the browser's own hit test decides; app-document pointer events cannot, because the board's out-of-process iframe swallows them under the toolbar. Everything else in the buddy is `pointer-events: none`, and the hit's `mousedown` is prevented so the composer keeps focus. The shell's z-index (2) sits below portals, toasts, overlays and modals; queue pills, Jump to latest and the panel's notice rows sit above it. The hit element is the host's first child, painted before the WebGL canvas, so it needs no compositing layer or clip-path mask layer of its own. Capture area hides it through the `[data-capture-snapshot]` rule.
    - Rest: the controller's last person move (view, selection, typing, board pointer) plus composer input and person-driven thread scroll. A thread `scroll` counts only within 1 s of the person's wheel, pointerdown or keydown, so following new agent output does not end the rest.
- **Centred stages:** `stageInsets`; Home measures a fixed reference group and applies only the top inset, so typing never moves the composer. Values: DESIGN.md.

### Navigation persistence and IPC

- **UI state:** `<userData>/ui-state.json`, shape `UiState` in `app/src/restore/uiState.ts`; selection and drafts are never saved.
  - Main replaces the whole state on each save, so the renderer sends no save until the startup read has settled (`memoryRead` in `App.tsx`; a failed read counts) and then saves the merged state: an early save would drop every field not yet read, and leaving a field out cannot protect it (POSTMORTEM). A startup read that never answers therefore blocks every save.
  - Validation on read and save rejects an oversized or invalid top level and drops malformed optional fields and unsaved roots one by one; a failed read yields Home and a later save may replace the file. Main (`app/src/main/uiState.ts`) serializes and debounces temp-file-plus-rename writes, and quit synchronously flushes the newest value without older writes overtaking it.
  - `UiState`'s optional fields are device-local, not a shared format (no `v` or fixture change); main intersects roots with registered roots, and an older app may drop them on save. The saved panel and sidebar widths are preferences, never overwritten by the clamped shown width. `overviewPanel` holds the Overview panel's width apart from `rightPanel`; `inspector` holds the canvas inspector's open flag and width for every canvas, and unlike `rightPanel` anything but `open: true` reads as folded (`parseInspectorState`); `sidebar` carries `width`, `hidden` (`true` only) and `conversationsFolded` (`true` only, the viewed canvas's conversation list folded for every canvas; absent means unfolded), each parsed on its own so a bad value drops only that field, and any one alone is kept. `conversationByCanvas` maps root → canvas key (the canvas ID, else its path, as drafts are keyed, so a moved canvas keeps it; `conversationCanvasKey`) → the conversation last seen there; a canvas whose ID another listed canvas shares is neither read nor written, and an id no longer listed is ignored. The main window writes it only from the Seen check and merges the startup read per canvas before its first save; an alias root renamed to its real path merges per canvas too (`renameUiRoots`). The prototype window only reads one id through main (§3 'Prototype window').
  - Removed roots lose all memory and Locate copies none. `conversationByCanvas` also drops a canvas the main window moved to the Trash and every entry of a conversation it deleted (`withoutCanvas`, `withoutConversation`), and every save drops an entry whose canvas or conversation the root's read lists no longer name (`withoutUnlisted`: a conversation deleted in the prototype window, a canvas deleted outside Doan), so the map does not grow toward the size cap. A list still being read or that failed drops nothing, and a canvas listed for a moment without its ID (unparsable or unreadable mid-write) still names the entry whose conversation's canvas is at that path; while that root's conversation list is unread, such a row keeps the canvas list from dropping any of the root's entries, since none can be matched to it. Startup follows the saved destination without consulting activity.
- **Restore:** `app/src/restore/restorePlan.ts`, tests `restorePlan.test.ts`.
- **Shell activity:** main-window-only `shell:activity` (`app/src/main/shellActivity.ts`) accepts only registered roots and combines shell check associations (§4) with app conversation histories under the same validation and safe reads. Coverage is partial by design: no provider transcripts, no activity text, no polling or store. The only live source is what agents send themselves with `doan work` (§4 'Work signals over app-socket v3'), kept in memory for display.
- **UI IPC:** `ui-state:get` / `ui-state:save`; main owns the path and validates against saved roots.
- **Canvas row revision:** `CanvasRow.rev` (main's row revision of that path, §13) is IPC-only, not a CLI field.
- **Seen canvases** (PRODUCT §5 item 1; tests are the schema: `canvasSeen.test.ts`, `gitSignal.test.ts`, `seenRecords.test.ts`, `seenProof.test.ts`, `readState.test.ts`). `CanvasRow.unseen` (`"new"` or `"changed"`) and `saveFailed` (`"save"` or `"id"`) are IPC-only and set only on the main window's copy of the list; thumbnails and conversations read the shell's own entries. Placement in the sidebar is `app/src/renderer/canvasNotice.ts`.
  - **Fingerprint** (`seenFingerprint.ts`): `sha256:<hex>` of the raw HTML bytes, the same string the local server records for the bytes it sent (`ResponseRecord.hash`), so a shown document and a disk file compare as strings. Linked CSS and assets do not count; a re-save of identical bytes is no change. Cached by file identity and times, never deciding a verdict.
  - **Local records** (`<userData>/canvas-seen.json` v3, v1 and v2 are converted; shape in `app/src/main/canvasSeen.ts`): per root, `migrated` and per canvas ID `{html}` (the hash last shown) or `{unseen: "new" | "migrated"}`. The project is never written: in a Git project a read-state file there dirtied the working tree on every view. Writes are a temp file and rename. A damaged file is moved aside (`canvas-seen.corrupt-<uuid>.json`) and the run starts empty, so every root starts as a first registration; a file with `v` > 3 is never overwritten and the run judges in memory only; each is told once (`ReadStateProblem` `records` / `unsupported`). Unregistering keeps the root's record. Alias roots merge into the real path per canvas, the real path's value winning, `migrated` if either has it (§7 'Root spellings').
  - **Verdicts** (`canvasSeen.ts` `judge`, `absorbFirst`, `markShown`): only a valid canvas ID listed once in its root keys a record; a missing or duplicate ID keys nothing, keeps its last verdict and is never written under a path or a guessed ID. The first listing after start or registration is the baseline: its IDs without a record are written as seen (their HTML once it can be read). An ID that appears later waits `GRACE_MS` (2 s) and is written `unseen: "new"` at once, so the dot survives a restart, unless the git signal changed from 2 s before its appearance to the end of the grace (git writes the working tree before HEAD or a ref) or the canvas page showed it meanwhile.
  - **Git signal** (`gitSignal.ts`): HEAD's branch or detached commit plus `FETCH_HEAD` and `ORIG_HEAD` by size, mtime and content hash, from the nearest `.git` at or above the root (stopping at HOME, as Go `gitLocation`), following a linked worktree's `gitdir:`. Refs and `packed-refs` are never read, so a commit on the same branch is no signal. Every git file is read with `readRegularFileSync` (a FIFO must not stall main); no repository or an unreadable one gives a constant signal. No git command runs.
  - **Migration**: a root without `migrated` that this device knew under an older Doan (v1 or v2 memory) reads its former shared records `<root>/.doan/seen/` once (`seenRecords.ts` `readSeen`, read-only): valid records are copied, and a damaged, conflicted, unreadable or later record, or a missing marker, takes this device's last verdict (`unseen: "migrated"` for a changed canvas, `"new"` for a new one, else seen), without a toast. A root without that memory (a new worktree, another machine, after a damaged file) never reads the folder. Then `migrated` is written and the folder is never read again.
  - **Visible proof** (`seenProof.ts`, `document/bindings.ts`, `windowDebugger.ts`): `read-state:showing` (`{root, path}` or null, main-window-only) only states which canvas the canvas page means to show. Main records the hash of what the main window's document bindings prove shown: exactly one direct child of the main frame with the row's URL, a complete, stable response of a finished loader of the current generation, for that root and path; while a reload swaps the iframe nothing is proven until the next bindings change. Thumbnails (another window) and prototypes never count, and a page showing A while the disk holds B records A. A page behind a record that already holds the disk's content writes nothing.
  - **Failures and Retry**: a failed write keeps a still-unseen canvas's dot as `saveFailed` while that canvas stays shown, and is not retried automatically. `read-state:problem` tells the window, which toasts it only on that project or canvas; `read-state:retry` (`{root, path?}`) runs Retry and runs once on each project entry, answering what still fails.
- **Thumbnail IPC** (`app/src/main/thumbnails.ts`): main requires an exactly shaped payload, saved root and listed canvas; renderer paths never choose cache filenames. `thumbnails:changed` carries no image; the renderer gets bytes again and shows them through `Blob` URLs (CSP `img-src blob:`).
- **Frame-count evidence:** a row's static `framesTruncated` (`app/src/main/frameCap.ts`) is kept apart from layout `truncated`, which also counts clipped strings; a missing canvas ID does not erase independently supplied frame facts.
- **Quick Open:** index, matching, filters, grouping and cap in `app/src/renderer/quickOpen.ts`. `QuickOpenDialog.tsx` is named to avoid a case-insensitive collision with it. Its `mousedown` prevents default outside the input to keep it focused; `outsideDialog` closes only when press and release both fall outside the dialog and the header strip. A project still being read is not in the index yet, so `readingCount` counts the searched ones (`searchMatch`'s scope) for the reading row; that row is a `role="status"` that stays mounted after the listbox, outside it because a listbox holds only options, and while it shows the list gives up its height (`--qo-reading-row`) so the dialog keeps its size. Conversations come from `HomeRun`'s per-root lists (`listState`), the same roots as the index, so Quick Open adds no IPC; a canvas conversation is indexed only when `conversationCanvasMatch` (`canvasConversationRows.ts`, the Canvas conversations rule) finds exactly one row. A pick goes through `HomeRun.openFound` with the root and canvas rows the results were built from, which checks the live list and the match again before opening; notification and strip Opens keep `canvasRow`'s own id-or-path lookup.
- **Navigation recovery:** explicit navigation cancels unapplied startup restoration, including a late ui-state read, which then never replaces newer memory.
- **Input continuity:** a removed focused control restores focus only when focus is lost, so deliberate moves survive.
- **Design system:** values follow Buzz as a reference; Doan writes its own CSS.
  - `app/src/renderer/tokens.css` has three `:root` layers: palette (the only literal colours), roles, and legacy names aliased to roles for older drafts. The renderer reads roles only. The file stays plain `:root` declarations (no Tailwind directives or `@import`) because it is served to canvases and scanned by `doan check`.
  - `app/src/renderer/ds/` rules are opt-in `doan-*` classes, loaded by CSS `@import`, never from JavaScript (CSP above). `@theme inline` maps shadcn names to roles, never a buddy palette ramp or `--buddy-*`. `ds/checks.ts` runs pure checks over the real renderer files in Vitest.
  - Buddy accent: the palette has one ramp per buddy, and a switch layer `--buddy-*` sits between palette and roles, holding only the steps roles read. `:root` points it at violet and `:root[data-buddy="<id>"]` blocks repoint it, changing only `--buddy-*` names; roles read `--buddy-*`, and the legacy `--accent*` names are not part of it. The renderer sets `<html data-buddy>` (none for Doany): main opens the window with `?buddy=<saved id>` and the entry applies it before React mounts (`buddyPaint.ts`), so the first frame already has the saved accent without a preload round trip, and App re-applies it on every preferences snapshot and pick. `ds/checks.ts` resolves tokens per buddy (`rootTokens(css, buddy)`) for the contrast and accent-place checks. The only rules that read a palette ramp directly are the three `.side .row` selectors, through the sidebar roles that stay violet (DESIGN.md 'Open decisions'); Welcome reads no accent.
  - Main sets `nativeTheme.themeSource = "light"` before creating windows. Geist is bundled with its license for the app window only.
  - The main and prototype windows are transparent (`backgroundColor` `#00000000`) over native `sidebar` vibrancy, and every translucent fill composites over that material. Once `app.getGPUFeatureStatus().gpu_compositing` leaves an `enabled*` value (`gpu-info-update`), both windows stay opaque for the rest of the session (`app/src/main/opaqueFallback.ts`): vibrancy is dropped, the native background takes the page's `--bg-app`, and each window's debugger emulates `prefers-reduced-transparency: reduce` on the page's own session (again after every page load), so the existing reduced-transparency rules give the look and no rule is duplicated for this case. Canvas iframes are separate targets and keep the system's media, so a canvas's own reduced-transparency rules do not switch (measured in the main and prototype windows, canvas opened before and after the fallback). Software compositing may lose alpha in a transparent window; that was suspected once and not reproduced (killing the GPU process into `disabled_software` on Apple Silicon kept alpha). The prototype window hands itself to the fallback only after its injection debugger attaches, because a second attach throws. Each GPU process loss and each change of `gpu_compositing` away from or back to enabled is appended to `<userData>/gpu.log` (`app/src/main/gpuLog.ts`), because a Finder-launched app drops stderr.
- **Renderer styles:** stylesheets load as `index.html` links (CSP above). Presentation checks compare the sandbox iframe's actual media preferences, not only its computed color-scheme.

### Canvas row menu IPC

Product rules: PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Canvas row menu'. Main: `app/src/main/canvasTrash.ts`, `canvasExport.ts` (reply shapes are the types there); renderer: `canvasRowMenu.ts`, `canvasRowMessages.ts`, `CanvasTrashDialog.tsx`, `ShellSidebar.tsx`.

- **Channels:** invokes `canvas:trashPlan`, `canvas:trash`, `canvas:export` and `canvas:revealExport`, and the event `canvas:exportRunning` `{root, path}`. The invokes are main-window main-frame only (`senderAllowed.ts`); the event goes to the main window only (`send`). The renderer sends `{root, path}` and never a file path.
- **The renderer's root and path are keys into main's own folder list** (`trashTarget`): a registered root whose entry is a list, holding this exact path as a row that `canvasKind` accepts (`.doan/canvases/` or `.doan/ds/`, subdirectories included). Main builds every file path from the listed row. Export takes the same two checks.
- **Move to Trash checks** run in this order at `canvas:trashPlan` and again at `canvas:trash`: the root and row above; no symbolic link in any segment from `.doan` down, checked with `lstat` against the disk now, and the canvas a regular file; its real path inside the real root by path segment; then the busy grounds below. Only the canvas file moves; `canvas:trashPlan` answers ready or a refusal and `canvas:trash` trashed or a refusal. A second request for a row still moving is refused.
  - **Check-then-use limit:** `shell.trashItem` takes a path, so a segment replaced by a link between the check and the call is not stopped, unlike the server's `O_NOFOLLOW_ANY` open (§3). Accepted: one action the person confirmed on a row they chose.
- **Busy grounds (main is the boundary; the renderer's `trashBlock` only draws the disabled item):** any conversation of this root for the same canvas (`sameCanvas`: the ID when both sides have one, else the path) that is running, waiting, or `working` (preparing, or a queue about to go out); a project turn of this root not yet judged (`ConversationService.projectEvidence`: running or within `BIND_GRACE_MS`) whose `changed` list or reported `edits` name the path (edits made root-relative as `conversation/project.ts` does, `/tmp` and `/private/tmp` alike); a start on that canvas main has not accepted yet, which no list names before acceptance (`ConversationService.startingCanvases`); or a `doan work` item with the same root and path. Nothing of another root counts, because worktrees share ids and paths (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01). The renderer's `trashBlock` counts the same conversation set (a test pins it against `trashBusy`) and takes the project part from `homeRun.drawing`, which main does not have, and this window's own panel starts from `CanvasRun.startingOn` (a prototype window's start is left to main's refusal).
- **Export:** `canvas:export` opens the save panel with a file name only as `defaultPath`; the app remembers no folder. Once a file is chosen main sends `canvas:exportRunning`, so the progress toast and the disabled item start then and not while the panel is open, and runs `doan export <path> -o <file> --force --repo <root> --json` (`runExport`, `app/src/main/cli.ts`; `--force` because the panel already asked about replacing). `EXPORT_TIMEOUT_MS` is 120 s against a measured slowest case of about 1.5 s (5,000 assets in one directory). A second export of the same row is refused until the first ends, a panel cancel runs nothing, and no CLI, a crash, a timeout or output that is not an envelope is a failed reply that frees the row.
  - `interpretExport` reads the envelope: `external` as a count and, for `export_refused` and `export_too_large`, the message lines that start with `- `, each `- <item>: <detail>` cut at its last `": "`. This line format is the contract between `cli/internal/cli/export.go` and the app, pinned on both sides (`export_test.go`, `canvasExport.test.ts`); a `usage_error` counts as "inside `.doan`" only when the chosen folder has a `.doan` segment (case-insensitive), as the CLI judges it. The CLI's absolute output path never reaches the renderer.
  - Show in Finder: `canvas:revealExport` takes an opaque id main issued for a file it just wrote (the last 16 are kept) and never a path.
  - The prototype window's Export is a second instance of the same flow (`exportFlow`; `createPrototypeExport` beside `createCanvasExport`) with its own deps: its target is a frame the window plays, its running event and save panel belong to the prototype window, and it keeps its own lock (one per window) and its own Show in Finder ids, so neither window can reveal the other's file (§3 'Prototype window' 'Export'). `interpretExport` also reads `frame_not_found` and `not_prototype` there.
- **Trashed mark:** `CanvasController.trash` (`TrashMarks`, `canvasView.ts`) is `begin` before `canvas:trash` is sent, because the folder watcher can drop the row before the reply, and `settle` with the reply. It is per request: a refused reply lifts only its own mark, so an earlier request's mark is not lost. It is one-shot: it is read by `canvasViewUpdate`, which then answers `clear` `trashed` instead of `moved` even when exactly one other row has the canvas ID, and a sweep drops it once the path has left the list (a loading or failed folder keeps it). A `trashed` clear calls neither `canvasRemoved` nor `canvasMoved`: App shows the Overview from `open: null` and records no history visit (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27), and the reply's toast is the only one.
- **Menu and dialog:** the open menu is one `{kind, key}` state shared with the project menu, and the project menu's close effect judges by kind, so a canvas menu is not closed by it; it closes when its row disappears or the page locks (`staleMenu`). The confirmation is part of App's `modal`. The row's button is hidden only visually (clipped, not `display: none`): a `display: none` button cannot take the focus Radix returns when the menu closes, or the focus App returns when the dialog closes (`focusCurrent` needs a box), and focus fell to `body`.

### Prototype window IPC

Rules and boundary: §3 'Prototype window'. Channels, payloads and the prototype preload's API are `app/src/main/prototype/ipc.ts`; validation is `app/src/main/prototype/requests.ts`; the allowlists are `senderAllowed.ts`.

- **Two senders.** The main window sends the play request and, for the drawing relay, reads the playing target and reports the project conversation drawing it (main-window main frame, `mainOnly`). The prototype window's main frame sends every other prototype request (`PROTOTYPE_WINDOW_REQUESTS`), its Export, Show in Finder, visibility and conversation panel requests included. Where the two overlap and how each role is checked: §3 'Prototype window' 'Sender role'. Main sends the prototype window its view, still image, export progress and visibility, the filtered conversation broadcasts, the narrow reads' changes and the relayed ids; it sends the main window that the prototype window closed (which returns focus to a live Play, PRODUCT §5 item 2 'Prototype window'), the playing target, checked reveals and Last used picks. The playing target also goes to the main window with its frame (`prototype:playing`, `PrototypePlaying` in `ipc.ts`), pushed by `targetRelay.ts` once per change (a play, a link move, Back/Forward, close) and readable on the same channel; the toolbar Play drawer marks that row Playing only when its root and canvas are the open canvas's. Export, Show in Finder, visibility and the panel's requests other than its open state belong to the window host in `index.ts`, not the run controller, because they act on the window, the CLI, the main window or settings rather than the run.
- **Main builds every URL.** A play names root, canvas path, frame and the raw mark; a drawer choice names a frame the current run listed; a link move names its generation and the raw value, which main takes only as a frame the current run listed ('Links' in §3); Reload names the generation it means; the mode request names only `fit` or `responsive`, and the same mode does nothing; Export names the root, canvas path and frame the view showed, which main compares with what plays, and Show in Finder only an id main issued. The view carries the mode and the run's mark width and height, and the still carries its box. The window never sends a URL, and refused or stale requests (another generation, a role mismatch, a malformed payload) do nothing.
- **Preloads stay separate.** Each preload spells its channel names literally (pinned against `ipc.ts` by their tests) and imports no value from the other, because a shared value would become a chunk that a sandboxed preload cannot require.
- **No format of their own.** The window's channels are app-internal and add no shared schema, userData file or CLI output. What reaches the window from outside goes through public contracts: `doan open --prototype` is app-socket v6 (§4), and Export runs `doan export --prototype` (§12).

### Deep links

`doan://open?root=<absolute root>&canvas=<repo-relative canvas path>[&frame=<frame id>]`, built in Go by `canvasLink` (`cli/internal/cli/work_rules.go`, no frame argument, because the CLI does not know frames; the guide keeps links out of answers and reaches a frame with `doan open --frame`) and read in main by `parseOpenLink` and `resolveOpenLink` (`app/src/main/deepLinks.ts`). Both sides read `schema/fixtures/open-link/cases.json` (§11). `doan open` (§4) reaches the same navigation without a URL. It is separate from the reply chip form `doan:<canvas-id>/…` (§7 'Renderer primitives'), which needs no root because the thread already has its project. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Deep links'.

- **Security boundary.** Any web page can open a `doan://` URL, so a link only navigates to a listed canvas of a registered root: no write, send, conversation start, project addition or setting change. `doan open` holds to the same boundary, though only a process with the runtime token can send it; `doan open --prototype` only shows a listed canvas in another window, and has no `doan://` form, so no web page can reach it. Giving links or `open` any action needs this boundary decided again. The handoff that starts a conversation is not a link or `open`: it is its own token-protected socket version (§4 'Handoff over app-socket v5') that also needs a running Doan conversation's key.
- **Parsing.** Parameter order is fixed (root, canvas, frame) and percent-encoded (Go `QueryEscape` with `%20` for spaces); unknown, duplicate or missing parameters, malformed percent escapes or UTF-8, a relative or `..` root, a canvas outside `.doan/canvases|ds/*.html`, control characters, an empty frame or a URL longer than `OPEN_LINK_LIMIT` UTF-16 code units are `malformed`. The root must equal a registered root literally (§4 'Selection ownership'), and the canvas must be in that root's list; the refusal names the project as the sidebar card does (`homeCards.ts`).
- **Registration.** `build.protocols` in `app/package.json` puts `doan` in the packaged app's `CFBundleURLTypes`; development runs never call `setAsDefaultProtocolClient`, so only packaged copies open links, and Launch Services picks which copy.
- **Receiving.** `app.on("open-url")` is installed before `ready`, because Electron delivers the launch URL before it (Electron 45 `electron.d.ts` 'open-url'); a running app gets it in the same instance through the single-instance lock. `openLinkQueue` keeps only the latest URL until the renderer calls `openLinksReady()` (after launch restore and the first folders snapshot) and no folder is still loading. Each link then shows the window and sends the resolved result to the main window on `deep-link:open`.
- **Renderer** (`openLink` in `App.tsx`, copy `openLinkRefusal` in `quickOpen.ts`): navigation reuses Quick Open's `openCanvas` path, with a frame as a view move and highlight that changes no selection, panel, draft or `doan active`.
- **Shared decision.** Main's `resolveOpenTarget(link, entries)` (`deepLinks.ts`) is the registered-root and list check for links, v4 requests and v6 plays; a root still loading is its own result, not a list refusal (a link, which waits until no root loads, treats it as unlisted; a request answers `not_ready`). The renderer's `decideOpen` (`app/src/renderer/openDecision.ts`) is the rest, in this order: a locked page (Add Projects, Settings, Project Settings; named so `doan open` can say which), first run, then dismissing dialogs, then the list again, because the renderer's copy may lag main's. Dismissing dispatches a cancelable `cancel` on the top `dialog[open]` (every app dialog's Cancel and Escape go through `onCancel`); one that stays open makes the move `busy`. First run and locked pages are checked before any dialog is dismissed, for links too, so a move that cannot happen leaves the wizard's own dialogs as they are. A link refused as busy is dropped with a toast that names what blocks (`blockedLinkMessage`, `doan open`'s reason without "in Doan"), shown in the top open modal dialog when there is one ('Toasts'); a request is answered `busy`.
- **`doan open` request** (`openRequests` in `deepLinks.ts`, `openCanvasRequest` in `App.tsx`; preload channels `doan-open:request` and `doan-open:answer`): main hands a listed canvas to the renderer with a request id; the renderer answers by that id once the move is decided, without waiting for the canvas to load. Requests are independent: each gets its own reply, the renderer takes them in arrival order and the last one's canvas stays. `openLinkQueue` (latest link only) is not used. The answer timeout is `OPEN_ANSWER_TIMEOUT_MS` and the request carries `expires`, the wall-clock ms at which main stops waiting and answers `busy`; the renderer closes no dialog and moves nowhere for a request past it (checked again before the move), and main drops a late answer, so a request the CLI saw as `busy` moves nothing. The one remaining race, a renderer answer sent just before `expires` that main's timer beats, leaves the screen on the canvas while the CLI says `busy`. Only an opened request brings the window forward, a hidden app included; a refusal brings nothing forward and shows no toast.
- **Frame result.** `found` or `missing` is decided from the canvas row's static frame list (`row.frames`, the listing), not the rendered DOM, because the answer is sent before the canvas has loaded. A frame that a canvas script creates reads `missing`.

### Agent conversation runtime and IPC

Main side in `app/src/main/conversation/` (entry `service.ts`). Contract: PRODUCT §3 'Agent conversation foundation contract'; screens: PRODUCT §5 items 1–3.

`routePrepare` (`prepare.ts`) runs `new`/`newDesign` on the requested provider's Doan connection ('Agent authentication' below) and a `resume` without a stored conversation on the **legacy external** path: the installed, logged-in Claude Code or Codex CLI with its shell-selected profile and session association. A stored conversation keeps its owner's path; there is no cross-profile resume, and an external session is never moved onto a Doan connection (unrelated to 'Handoff' below, which starts a new Doan conversation from a running one). Apart from keeping Doan-connection runs out of the rest of Doan's userData ('Agent authentication' 'Fence' below), the app does not sandbox the agent or promise that repository settings/hooks cannot execute code.

- **Transport and environment:**
  - Installed provider CLIs are accepted at or above `MINIMUM_VERSIONS` (`minimumVersions.ts`), compared on the numeric `x.y.z` core of `--version`; output without a core is `unknown` (`sessions.test.ts` 'agent CLI versions'). `BUNDLED_CLAUDE_SDK` is an exact pin checked by a test against `app/package.json`; runs do not compare it.
  - Stored sessions use floors (`CODEX_ROLLOUT_FLOOR`, `CLAUDE_REGISTRY_FLOOR`) that do not rise with the minimums, because a stored session keeps the version that wrote it: Codex never rewrites a rollout's first `session_meta` `cli_version` on resume, and a live Claude registry record below its floor or malformed makes that profile's resume `unknown` (fail closed: its `sessionId` cannot be trusted, so it may hold the session being resumed). A floor never sits above its minimum: lowering a minimum below a floor lowers the floor to it, or every resume on the lowest accepted CLI would be refused. Raising a floor abandons older conversations and needs its own decision.
  - Claude runs a fresh SDK/CLI per turn with the installed executable and user/project/local setting sources, so Ask first applies the user's own permission settings; Codex keeps one installed `app-server` between turns. ACP runs open one server per turn (`session/new`, or `session/load` for a stored session) and stop its group, with the tool commands it started ('Quit and process cleanup'), when the turn ends, so the account lease never outlives a running turn; Stop sends `session/cancel` and stops the group once the prompt ends or after `CANCEL_WAIT_MS` (`acpRunner.ts`).
  - Steering (Send now; providers in `STEER_PROVIDERS`, `conversation/steerSupport.ts`, renderer-safe with type-only imports; ACP runners have no `steer`): `runner.steer(prompt, turnId, messageId)` resolves when the provider accepts and rejects on any refusal; the read is reported separately through the runner input's `steerConsumed(messageId)`, called in line before the same frame's events, and never after a terminal event.
    - Claude: the turn's streaming-input generator stays open and yields the steered message with `uuid: messageId` and no `priority` (`later` runs a separate turn, `now` drops the current answer; observed on Claude Code 2.1.312 and 2.1.317). The read is the first top-level frame whose `user_message_uuids`, or the singular `user_message_uuid`, holds that uuid (`system/status` when folded, `system/init` when the CLI runs a follow-up turn; Claude Code 2.1.284 first names a turn's prompt on `stream_event` `message_start`). A success result is terminal only once every steered uuid was read, so a follow-up CLI turn stays inside the same Doan turn and ends at its result. A success result that leaves a uuid unread waits at most `STEER_READ_WAIT_MS` (`runners.ts`) for the read, counted from the first such result since the last read; past it the Doan turn ends as done and the owned group is stopped, so nothing runs unseen (the CLI was never seen to skip the echo, but an open stdin would otherwise keep the turn running until Stop).
    - Claude turn prompt: the turn's own prompt carries a uuid too (`promptTransport`, registered before it is written) and is read by the same rule. A success result before it is read closes a turn the CLI opened by itself, such as a background task's notice after a resume, whose frames carry no client uuid (Claude Code 2.1.284 and 2.1.322); it holds like an unread steer, and the frames before the read go out as this turn's events, so the process never works unseen. Past `STEER_READ_WAIT_MS` with the prompt unread, the turn fails `prompt_unread` (`endPromptWait`) and the group stops; a failed or aborted result with the prompt unread stops the group too, so the CLI cannot run the prompt later in a turn nobody sees. A turn whose prompt was not read is never done, so no suggestion or background line follows it; a `rate_limited` failure still reads usage to learn when the limit resets.
    - Claude held end: an end that leaves steers unread (that expiry, a failed or aborted result, Stop, sign-out) is held by the service (`Live.held`, `holdsEnd`) until the group is gone, at most `HELD_END_MS` (`service.ts`), because the CLI may have taken a message without its echo reaching Doan. `exited()` releases the reservation first (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27), reads the session transcript for those uuids, reports the found ones through `steerConsumed`, then writes the held end as it came, never as `process_exited`; only the rest go back to the queue. Past the bound nothing is read and all go back. `readInTranscript` (`io.ts`) looks for a `user` line's `uuid` (a prompt or a steer run as its own turn) and a `queued_command` attachment's `source_uuid` (a folded steer): the CLI writes them when it takes the message off its queue and writes only a uuid-less `queue-operation` on receipt, so a message it never took is never found (fixtures `claude-transcript-*`). Only bytes appended since the turn's first Send now are read (`markTranscript`). Quit ends the turn at once; steers the transcript shows once the group is gone only leave the queue and are not written to the thread (`lookLater`). A group gone before its turn's end (a crash) reads the transcript the same way before `process_exited`. A legacy-external profile's transcript is read, never written. A Stop of a running turn sets the list's memory-only `stopping`, so the renderer hides the turn indicator at once (`turnStatus.ts`) while the end may be held. A failed or aborted result is terminal at once, and a failure in a follow-up turn fails the whole Doan turn. `queued_turn_count` is not used: it was 0 while a steered message was still waiting. The generator closes on every terminal path; a terminal that leaves steered uuids unread also stops the owned group, so the CLI cannot run a hidden turn with a message that went back to the draft (an open stdin otherwise keeps the CLI alive and the next send busy, POSTMORTEM 2026-10-28).
    - Codex: `turn/steer {threadId, expectedTurnId, input, clientUserMessageId}` with no overrides; refused before the native turn id is known (`startingTurn`) and after completion (`-32600 no active turn to steer`). It goes through a dispatcher path whose 10 s timeout rejects only that request, because the protocol-failing `request` path would end the turn as `session_unavailable` while the server holds the reply during a permission wait. The read is a same-`turnId` `userMessage` item whose `clientId` echoes the id; it can arrive before the steer reply, and after a timeout rejection. An RpcError rejection forgets the id.
  - Launch environment (`app/src/main/launchEnv.ts`, PRODUCT §3 'Launch environment'): the login-shell probe, version checks, spawned agents (both paths), the bundled CLI (`runCli`, which keeps the app's own `PATH` to find a dev `doan`) and clone start from `launchEnv(process.env)`, an allowlist of what a Dock launch gives the app plus the proxy, CA and locale keys `launchctl setenv` can add; clone also keeps `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` and `DEVELOPER_DIR`. `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` never reaches the packaged app's environment (§9 'Fuses'), so main reads it once at startup with `launchctl getenv` (`restoreLaunchdCaCerts`) and puts launchd's value, or none, into `process.env` before anything is spawned; a launching terminal's value is replaced, in the unfused dev Electron as well (for the agent environment only: there Node already read the terminal's CA at startup, so main's TLS keeps trusting it). Main's own Node TLS trusts that file too: `trustExtraCaCerts` appends it to the default CAs with `tls.setDefaultCACertificates` (Node 24.5+) before any connection, and a file that cannot be read or parsed leaves the defaults and logs a warning. The login-shell probe also reads `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION`; Claude runs (both paths) get the shell's value or `true` (`conversation/environment.ts`; Doan-connection runs carry it on the lease), Codex and ACP runs never get it. `open -a`, the executable and `npm run dev` pass the launching terminal's environment to the app, and an allowlist rather than a denylist of known tools keeps any terminal's keys out. Accepted: other `launchctl setenv` keys are dropped even on a Dock launch, and kept keys other than `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` carry the launching terminal's values. Not covered: helper commands that run no agent (`ps`, `osascript`, `ditto`).
  - Each admission probes the login shell's PATH/configuration and executable version. The spawned agent gets PATH, the selected configuration and this app's `DOAN_RUNTIME`, with `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID` and `CODEX_SESSION_ID` never passed (`environment.ts`; they name the shell's own session for associations, PRODUCT §3 'Association recording'), and `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION=1`, so `doan work start` and `done` from an app conversation send nothing (§4). Doan-connection runs get the same marker from `agentAuth/environment.ts` `providerEnv`, where it is in `RESERVED` so provider extras cannot override it. `ConversationService.openRunner` adds `DOAN_CONVERSATION_ID=<conversation id>` on both paths, so `doan work update` names its conversation (§4 'Suppression'); a Codex server reused across turns keeps the same value, and main tells the turns apart. It is in `RESERVED` too, and so is `DOAN_HANDOFF`, which only Doan-connection runs get ('Handoff' below).
- **Identity and admission:** root, cwd and configDir are resolved canonically before matching. The association used is the latest by timestamp (a later row wins a tie) for the root and stable canvas ID, or path when both records lack an ID. Only the final `SESSION_TAIL_BYTES` of `sessions.jsonl` is read, so older associations are unavailable.
- **External occupancy:** a matching live Claude session (transcript plus the profile's `sessions/<pid>.json` registry, checked against OS PID/start identity) is occupied even with a different cwd. Each registry record is judged against the session being resumed (`registryIdentity`, `registryRecordVerdict` and `mergeOccupancy` in `sessions.ts`), because only a record that may hold that session can attach a second writer to it. A record's identity (`pid` matching its file name, `procStart`, `pidDomain`) is read and its liveness checked before anything else, so the record of an ended process (no process, or a start time other than `procStart`) is skipped whatever its other fields, and so is a record that vanished after the listing while the listed `sessions/` directory is still the same one (dev/ino). `procStart` may be absent (Claude Code 2.1.323 writes none); the pid alone then decides liveness, so a record with no process behind it is skipped, and a live one, whose pid may have been reused, is judged as below. Such leftovers are common: a CLI that Doan's immediate SIGKILL, or the SIGKILL 0.5 s into a SIGTERM stop, reaches before its own cleanup leaves its record behind (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-13). A live record is fully validated: intact, it is occupied when its `sessionId` is the target and skipped otherwise, whatever its `kind` (Claude Desktop's Code sessions write `interactive` records with `entrypoint` `claude-desktop`); below the floor or malformed, it is unknown. A record from another PID domain (a `pidDomain` other than absent or `"darwin"`, the fixed value Claude Code writes on macOS; Linux and WSL add a machine id and PID namespace) cannot be checked for liveness: intact and not the target, it is skipped, otherwise unknown. An unknown file name (only `<pid>.json` and the `<pid>.<sha256>.key` it keeps beside it are known), an unreadable identity (a `procStart` that is there but not a date included), any other read error and broken JSON are unknown. Verdicts merge after every record, occupied over unknown over free; an empty registry is free, and a missing or unreadable one is unavailable, never an empty one. For Codex, `thread/resume` is the actual writer admission (`-32600 … already has an active writer` while a terminal holds the thread). Before it, `codexOccupancy` (`io.ts`) reads the writer lock `<CODEX_HOME>/thread-writer-locks/<threadId>.lock` that Codex holds with `flock` (observed from 0.158.0, the minimum): no lock directory or no lock file is free; an odd directory is unknown; a present file is checked with a bounded read-only `lsof` (a file left by a killed writer is free, a failure unknown). `lockf` is not used, because taking the lock even briefly can make Codex's own resume fail. A daemon-mode terminal keeps the lock about a minute after `/exit`. Doan's own `app-server` holding the lock is not occupancy: a send that reuses the conversation's live runner skips the check, and holders in a group led by a detached direct child of Doan's main process are ignored. `resumeTarget` is advisory: it reserves and opens nothing, and a Codex preview does not prove writer availability.
- **App-local ownership and request timing** (`ownership.ts`):
  - `{runtime, canonical configDir, sessionId}` is reserved before a start/send is accepted, independently of per-conversation busy state. New starts hold temporary reservations promoted when their session ID is known; a collision is refused and a spawned loser is cleaned up without releasing uncertain ownership. There is no global or per-canvas conversation cap: a canvas's conversations run side by side. The other limits are one working project conversation per root, one turn per conversation and one conversation per agent session: a `resume` start of a session a conversation in the index holds is refused `session_in_use` (`checkSessionFree`, after preparation and again at acceptance) until that conversation is deleted, and `resumeTarget` answers `no_record` for it, so nothing is offered.
  - Membership, canvas identity, that the conversation is not deleted and that the app is not quitting are rechecked after every asynchronous step. Admission refusals precede `user.message`; after acceptance, failures persist as `turn.failed`.
  - A spawned process keeps its reservation until the whole owned group is observed gone; a terminal event, idle state, kill request or cleanup timeout is not that observation. Only known no-spawn failures release at once.
  - **Send after a finished turn:** a send to an idle conversation whose previous process still holds the reservation waits up to `EXIT_WAIT_MS` (twice the slowest exit observed in the packaged app), then reruns every admission check (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-28). Cancellation and timeout return `busy` before the live entry changes (`service.test.ts`).
  - **Context window usage:** a transport keeps the last `{used, size}` its provider reported in the turn and emits one `context.usage` event right before the `turn.done` or `turn.failed` that `finish()`/`finishAcp()` actually emit; a stopped turn's `interrupted`, the service's own terminals (Stop, sign-out, quit, crash folding) and the runners' own `turn.failed`/`interrupted` carry none, and the state starts empty each turn. Sources: Claude, top-level `assistant` frames' `input_tokens + cache_creation_input_tokens + cache_read_input_tokens` (a `<synthetic>` frame is not a call) with `result.modelUsage[message.model].contextWindow` (`modelUsage` also holds auxiliary models); Codex, `thread/tokenUsage/updated` for the current `threadId` and `turnId` only (a resumed thread replays the previous turn's notification first), `last.inputTokens` (it already includes cached input) over `modelContextWindow`; ACP, `usage_update` `{used, size}` as reported (Antigravity omits it for a model whose window it does not know); Cursor sends none. Values must be non-negative safe integers with `size >= 1`, or the turn keeps its earlier valid value. It is read from frames the run already receives, so it adds no control request and no wait at turn end. Observed on Claude Code 2.1.284 and 2.1.318, Codex 0.158.0 and 0.166.0, Copilot 1.0.89 and 1.0.105, Antigravity ACP 1.3.0 (fixtures `app/src/main/conversation/fixtures/*-context.json`).
  - **Next-message suggestions:** every Claude run passes `promptSuggestions: true`, and the CLI sends at most one `prompt_suggestion` after a done turn's `result`, 1-3 s later, while it exits after its input closes (observed on Claude Code 2.1.318). The runner keeps reading after the terminal and hands the first such frame, cleaned by `promptSuggestionText` (`conversation/promptSuggestion.ts`: whitespace folded, control and bidi characters removed, over 1,000 code units dropped, never cut), to `Prepared.suggested`. The service keeps it in memory per conversation, fenced by the run's live entry and turn ID, a done turn with no newer send, a conversation not deleted, no quit, and the conversation's Doan connection still being the provider's current one (`connectionChanged`, from the auth publish in `main/index.ts`); `conversationSummary` exposes it as `suggestion` only while ended, not working, with no queue and no returned draft. It is never an event: history validation refuses unknown types, so storing it would make older builds unable to read the conversation.
  - **Ending a finished turn's process:** the CLI's own wait for a suggestion can outlast `EXIT_WAIT_MS` (its cap is 30 s), background work can keep it far longer, and nothing tells a process generating a suggestion from one exiting without one. So the conversation's next send asks the runner once per turn to end the finished turn's process (`Runner.endAfterTurn`, `service.ts` `endAfterTurn`) and then waits for `process_exited` as before. It runs before the send's other checks, so a send later refused, a send that waits for a judgement and a queue going out at the turn's end all end it; asked during the turn-end usage read, the decision waits for the read to close the input. `afterTurnCleanup` (`process.ts`) decides: a done turn with its prompt read and an empty background set has only its suggestion left and is killed at once (`OwnedGroup.kill()`, also while a stop still waits for its own SIGKILL), because the CLI takes 0.5-1 s to handle a SIGTERM and SIGKILL left every transcript whole and resumable (PoC, Claude Code 2.1.284 and 2.1.322); anything else goes through SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 0.5 s. An interrupt cannot be used: the SDK refuses it once input is closed. The reservation still releases only on the group's exit, so two processes never share a session (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-28).
  - **Background process groups:** a background command, and a background subagent's commands, run in process groups of their own (the shell's pid), which the stream never names. The CLI stops them through its SIGTERM handling; a SIGKILL that reaches the CLI first leaves them running with parent 1. So a group whose background set is not empty (ambient tasks included) is never killed first, on any path (a send, Stop, the line's Stop, sign-out, deletion, quit): it always gets SIGTERM, and immediate SIGKILL is used only with an empty set. The CLI's SIGTERM only signals them, so one that ignores SIGTERM would outlive it; the stop also ends the groups the CLI's descendants started ('Quit and process cleanup').
  - **Background work after a done turn** (Claude; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Background work line'): the runner keeps reading after the terminal and replaces its set on each `system/background_tasks_changed` (`backgroundTasks`, `transport.ts`), a level signal carrying every live task each time that the SDK says not to pair with `task_started`/`task_notification`. `ambient` entries count for how the process ends but not for the line. A done turn ending with shown tasks reports `Prepared.backgroundChanged(true)` after its terminal event; the service keeps `background: {turnId, stopping?}` on the live entry and lists it while not busy, except for a queue going out at the turn's end or a process the next send already asked to end. It is memory only, never an event, for the suggestion's reason, and goes when the group is gone (`exited`). When the set empties after the terminal and Doan did not ask for a SIGTERM stop, the runner kills the group in that tick: the CLI calls the model for the finished work's notice 16-119 ms later (PoC), which no person asked for (PRODUCT §3 Out of scope 4), and the empty set orphans nothing.
    - Not running elsewhere: the conversation is not `working`, and its Doan-connection lease turns quiet (`Lease.quiet`, `agentAuth/manager.ts`), so sign-out and replacement do not answer `stop_required` for it, yet `stopRuns` stops it and waits for its lease. Restart to Update does not count it and quits. Project Remove calls `stopBackground` and does not wait; delete closes the runner; quit closes it and waits; only sign-out (the lease drain) and quit wait for the group.
    - The line's Stop is `conversation:interrupt` with the done turn's `turnId`: on a not-busy entry whose background names that turn it sets `stopping`, stops the runner and answers `busy` if the group is still there once the stop's own wait ends. A `turnId` that is not current does nothing, and a second press while stopping returns at once, so a late or double press never stops a newer send.
    - A terminal session continue that picks the session such a group holds keeps the `session_in_use` refusal (BACKLOG 'Known defects' for its wording). Codex's experimental `thread/prediction/updated` is not used: it needs an ephemeral fork that Doan would start itself (PRODUCT §3 Out of scope 4).
- **Canvas lifecycle:** a conversation stores its root plus `{path, id?}`; `resolveListedCanvas` requires exactly one listed canvas with the stable ID, so a move updates stored metadata and a missing or ambiguous target refuses admission. `newDesign` starts unbound; a canvas reaches it only through promotion. A start or a promotion on a canvas leaves the canvas's other conversations as they are, running turns included. Only a deletion closes a conversation's run (`closeRun`: it closes the runner, cancels a send waiting on its process and returns the queue as an `interrupted` draft before the deletion discards it).
- **Project conversations and promotion** (`project.ts`; rules in PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Project conversations', cases in `project.test.ts`):
  - `projectBusy` is computed from live turns, not index metadata, so a start not yet indexed counts. A conversation without a canvas holds its root from admission for the whole turn, even if promoted meanwhile, except while it waits for the person: `receive()` keeps each live turn's open permission requests and questions (`Live.asking`, passed as `ProjectTurn.asking`; a `permission.request` or `question.request` adds one, the resolved event of the same kind removes it, keyed by kind so one kind's resolution never clears the other's id, and a terminal event clears them), and a turn with any open request holds neither through `projectBusy` nor through its unjudged evidence; an ended turn's unjudged evidence still holds. Limits: a turn with an open request whose other work goes on (parallel subagents) does not hold the root, and a provider that cancels a request without ending the turn leaves it counted as open until the turn ends. `project_busy` is checked at admission and again right before acceptance (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27).
  - Judgement time: `promotionDeadline` (`project.ts`). Once the turn ended and its process group was seen gone (`exitedAt`, recorded by `exited()` before or after `ended()`, which then rearms the timer), the turn is judged at `max(endedAt, exitedAt) + PROMOTION_SETTLE_MS`, never later than `endedAt + BIND_GRACE_MS`; with the group still there (a Codex server between turns, background work) at `endedAt + BIND_GRACE_MS`. A group that is gone can write nothing more, so only the watcher's delay is left; `PROMOTION_SETTLE_MS` is 1.5 times the slowest write-to-refresh measured in the dev app, mostly the `doan ls` the refresh runs. A folder refresh before it still judges at once, and the busy-loop catch-ups (`judgement()`, `checkProject()`, `projectEvidence()`) and the handoff judgement (`armHandoff`) use the same deadline. The rule depends only on the group, so it covers ACP turns too (not measured).
  - `canvasesAtStart` is overwritten at each accepted project turn; the key name stays, but it means "at the latest turn's acceptance".
  - Turn evidence (that snapshot, the turn's `edit` activity paths, canvas rows whose `rev` changed via `refreshed(root, changed)`) lives outside the live entry, because a send replaces the live entry before acceptance and an unjudged turn must keep its root even when that send is refused. A watcher event without file names raises no `rev`, so it is never evidence. A waiting send of the same conversation starts with fresh evidence after the judgement; a Stop or provider sign-out refuses it with `busy`.
  - Promotion to an existing canvas needs both the turn's edit activity and a file change, because Claude Code and Codex report an edit activity before the permission answer, even for a refused edit, and a watcher change alone cannot say who wrote the file; a single canvas new since the snapshot is promoted without either. Edit evidence is unconfirmed for other providers, and a refused edit plus someone else's change to that file in the same turn still promotes.
  - Overlapping turns (a turn answered after another started): each evidence keeps a `taken` set, and a promotion write adds its canvas to every other evidence of the root, so a turn never takes a canvas another conversation was bound to after its snapshot. At judgement, `contested` holds the listed canvases new to another unjudged evidence of the root; a single new canvas in it is not promoted, and `contestedCanvas` names it so it also goes into the `taken` of each other evidence whose snapshot lacks it, and neither turn takes it. A judgement blocked by two or more new canvases adds nothing. `promotionTarget` and `contestedCanvas` stay pure over these inputs. A canvas conversation's turn in the same root with any handoff request not yet resolved adds to `contested` every path its requests name and every listed canvas its snapshot lacks, so a project turn judged meanwhile cannot take a canvas a canvas conversation is handing off to; as between project turns, a project turn's own new canvas made in that window is not promoted either. A started handoff adds its target to every evidence's `taken`, as a promotion write does ('Handoff' below).
  - The promotion write goes through the journal (`bind` or `promote`) and the index rename; a refused write changes nothing.
- **Handoff** (`doan handoff`, §4 'Handoff over app-socket v5'; pure rules in `handoff.ts`, wiring in `service.ts`; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 2 'Handoff'; cases in `handoff.test.ts` and `service.handoff.test.ts`):
  - Key: `openRunner` gives each runner of a Doan-connected conversation, canvas or project, a random 64-hex `DOAN_HANDOFF` and keeps key → conversation in memory only, deleted when that runner's process exits; a legacy-external run gets none. A key and not the conversation id, because the id sits in `index.json`, which the userData fence keeps from Doan-connection runs but not from legacy-external runs or other agents the person runs ('Agent authentication' 'Fence' below); neither is a boundary against the person's own processes, which the socket token is. Project conversations get one because a Codex server outlives a promotion, so a promoted conversation's next turn would carry `HANDOFF_GUIDANCE` without a key. The name avoids `KEY`, `TOKEN` and `SECRET` because Codex's default `shell_environment_policy` drops variables named so from the shell. A request counts only when its key is the current runner's and that runner's turn is the accepted, running one, so a previous Claude process still exiting gets `not_running`.
  - Each accepted turn of a Doan-connected conversation keeps a snapshot of the root's listed canvases and its taken requests in request order (`HandoffTurn`, memory only); each taken request writes `handoff.requested` in the asking turn. When that turn ends done, every request is judged on its own with `handoffDecision`, at once when its path is already listed, else, as a promotion, again at the next folder refresh or at the `promotionDeadline` ('Project conversations and promotion'); `handoffDecision` refuses a path not listed as exactly one canvas, one in the snapshot (also by path) or the conversation's own canvas; conversations the target has do not count. No request starts until every one is decided, so a later refresh never judges a request that is already starting: refusals are written at once, then the starts run one at a time in request order (`startHandoffs`), checking the turn's identity and state again after each await (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27). The order is not about concurrent starts, which are already safe (`start()` reads `entries` and accepts in one tick). A failed, stopped or interrupted turn, a deletion and a sign-out or account replacement that stops the provider's runs (`stopRuns`) cancel every request not resolved yet, between two starts too; conversations already started stay. Quit and a history that stopped taking writes drop the turn without writing anything, and a restart fold reads each request without its `handoff.resolved` as cancelled.
  - Each new conversation goes through `start` with internal options `{origin, handoffText, turnId, path}` (`HandoffStart`) on the source's provider, model, effort, Fast and current permission mode, never touching Last used. It passes every admission check of a start. Right after `prepare`, before a runner or lease is opened, and again right before acceptance, `checkHandoffSource` cancels it (`handoff_cancelled`) unless the conversation's handoff turn is still the asking `turnId`, that turn's request for `path` is `starting` and the source still exists: after a cancel, a later turn may ask for the same path, and that request must not lend the earlier start its state. A start that fails after a stop of its provider counts as cancelled. Its first `user.message` holds the handoff as written with `origin` and no anchors; the text is never read for `{{n}}` markers, so a marker-like text neither refuses the start nor names an anchor, and history validation accepts such text only with `origin`. Its first turn is marked as started by the handoff and cannot hand off (`handoff_turn`), even after a steered message.
  - Queue: while a turn's handoffs wait, the source's queue is held and `working` stays true; a send to the source that arrives meanwhile waits for the resolution, as for a project judgement, and then goes to the source. After the turn's last resolution `settleHandoff` handles the queue once, by `handoffQueueOutcome`: a single started request moves it, with its attachment ids, to the new conversation (`moveQueue`), where it goes out after the first turn; a single refusal, or several requests none of them cancelled, send it to the source as after any done turn; a single cancel, or a cancel among several, returns it as a draft. Several requests' queue goes to the source rather than a new conversation because Doan cannot tell which canvas it is meant for, and a cancel returns it because it must not go out right after a Stop or sign-out. A queue moved to a conversation whose first turn already ended (before its start was accepted) follows that end as `ended()` would (`queueAfterEnd`): sent after done, a draft after a failure or interrupt, and taken by a usage-limit read or schedule that end started when it settles. A target whose run closed or whose history stopped taking writes gets it as its own draft; a target deleted before the move takes nothing under its id, and the queue comes back as the source's draft, never sent.
  - `handoff.resolved` is written with the requesting turn's id and the request's `canvas: {path}` once decided: `{outcome: "started", target: {conversationId, canvas}}` (the target in its own object, because the event envelope's `conversationId` is the history's own and a payload field of that name would overwrite it), `{outcome: "refused", reason}` (a judgement reason or the refused start's own error code) or `{outcome: "cancelled"}`. After the turn's last resolution main sends one `conversation:handoff` (`handoffNoticeKind`, none when nothing started) to the main window, and to the prototype window when the source or a target is its playing canvas ('IPC contract' below); only the main window's renderer acts on it: `HandoffNotice` (root, source and target, each conversation and canvas) for one started conversation, `HandoffMultiNotice` (root, source and `targets` in request order) for two or more, told apart by `"targets" in notice`. The renderer (`app/src/renderer/handoffView.ts`) decides between moving and a toast for one, waiting at most `HANDOFF_WAIT_MS` for the project read; for several it never moves and shows the summary toast only away from the source canvas page.
  - Missed handoffs (`missedHandoffs` in `handoff.ts`): each `HandoffTurn` records whether the turn was sent with `HANDOFF_GUIDANCE` (`guided`) and the paths of its own `edit` activities (`receive()`). When a guided turn ends done, a `MissedWait` in `missedWaits` waits for the handoff's `promotionDeadline` (only the deadline, brought forward by the group's exit, not the first folder refresh, which can come before the new canvas is listed) and writes one `handoff.missed` per canvas found; the renderer shows it on that canvas's card ('Canvas cards' below). It is kept apart from `HandoffTurn`, which the next send replaces, so it holds neither the queue nor `working` and outlives that send, and it is keyed by turn id, because a conversation's next turn can end before the earlier wait is due. Deletion, quit and a history failure drop it, and the write first checks again that the app is not quitting and the conversation exists with a canvas and still takes writes, because `store.append` would recreate a deleted history. Edit paths map to listed canvases through `canvasesAt` (`project.ts`), which follows IDs only for canvases in the snapshot, so a new canvas renamed before the deadline gets none. Claude Code's and Codex's shell commands report no path and edit evidence is unconfirmed for other providers ('Project conversations and promotion'), so a canvas made that way gets none.
- **Canvas cards** (pure rule `drawnCanvases` in `drawn.ts`, wiring in `service.ts`; renderer `threadItems.ts`, `canvasCardText.ts`; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Canvas cards'; cases in `drawn.test.ts`, `service.drawn.test.ts`, `threadItems.test.ts`, `canvasCardText.test.ts`):
  - Every accepted turn (Doan-connected canvas and project conversations, legacy external ones) gets a `DrawWatch` in `drawWatches`, keyed by turn id and kept apart from the live entry, `HandoffTurn` and `MissedWait` for the same reasons as `MissedWait`. It snapshots the root's listed canvases at acceptance and never copies the conversation's canvas metadata, which a refused send may change (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-15). `receive()` collects every `edit` activity path of that turn, whatever `insideDesign` says, because `insideDesign` is computed without the `/private` spelling of a canonical root; `canvasesAt` already drops paths outside the root and unlisted ones.
  - `drawnCanvases` keeps the edited canvases listed exactly once (`listedOnce`, shared with `missedHandoffs`) and absent from the snapshot by path and ID, minus those already announced; each gets one `canvas.drawn` `{canvas}` with the turn's id. It is judged when an edit arrives and at every folder refresh of the root, so `canvas.drawn` can come before or after the turn's terminal event; activities come before a permission answer, so an edit alone writes nothing until the file is listed. The watch lasts until the turn's `promotionDeadline` (with a last judgement there), brought forward or extended as for promotion, and is dropped on deletion, quit and a history failure. Each write first checks again that the app is not quitting, the conversation exists, its history still takes writes and the watch is still current (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27); a failed write logs and drops only that watch, leaving the turn and `failedPersistence` alone. A canvas listed after the watch ended (the deadline, a quit or a restart) gets no card. Two turns that edit the same new path both write it; `contested` applies only to promotion.
  - `state.ts` does not fold `canvas.drawn`, and unseen ignores it ('Unseen' below), so a card written after the end never re-marks a seen conversation. The renderer's `turnOf` leaves it out of a turn's `lastSeq`, because it is a watcher's write rather than runner progress and must not hide the running stage; a later real event still supersedes that stage.
  - The renderer builds one card item per turn and path (key from turn id and path, so a status change keeps the element and Open's focus) from `canvas.drawn`, `handoff.requested`, `handoff.resolved` and `handoff.missed`, which also draws histories written before cards. A late event joins its own turn's stack without closing the open activity group; the stack sits after the turn's other items and before a Send now message and a waiting request. A path-only card (a request) borrows the ID of the single listed row at its path, because `sameCanvas` tells a row with an ID from a canvas without one.
- **Permissions and terminals** (`transport.ts`, `dispatcher.ts`; cases in their tests): a Codex file denial returns decline, not cancel, so the turn can continue. An unsolicited interrupted completion fails with `process_exited`; at most one terminal event is persisted per turn. Malformed Codex protocol fails closed. There is no model-turn deadline for silence or stdout noise. A resumed legacy-external session keeps the approval/sandbox policy stored with it.
- **Questions** (product rules PRODUCT §3 'Resume and permissions', §5 item 3 'Questions'):
  - `question.ts` holds the shapes and `QUESTION_LIMITS` and imports nothing, so the renderer shares it; `questionAnswer.ts` checks an answer against the pending questions and is shared by every runner and the renderer's Send rule. A question past a limit is refused, never cut, because Claude keys its answers by the question's own text.
  - Claude: `canUseTool` sends `AskUserQuestion` (`CLAUDE_QUESTION_TOOL`) to its own path, never `permission.request`, because Allow would return the input with no answer. Pending questions sit in a map apart from permissions, so `answer` never allows a question and `answerQuestion` never answers a permission. Doan gives the questions ordinal ids and maps them back to their text (`claudeQuestion`). An answer allows with the chosen labels and Other text keyed by each question's text; a skip allows with empty `answers` and an `unanswered` annotation when the turn's `system/init` capabilities advertise it, else denies (`claudeQuestionResult` in `transport.ts`); no text goes into `answers`, because the model reads any as the answer. An unreadable question is denied with a message telling the agent to ask in its reply; one after Stop, the turn's end or `close()` and every one at expiry is denied "Request expired.". `AskUserQuestion`'s `tool_use` produces no activity, since the card stands for it. Observed on Claude Code 2.1.312: every permission mode, and a run without one, calls `canUseTool` for it.
  - Codex: every `item/tool/requestUserInput` not addressed to another thread or turn (a missing `threadId` or `turnId` included) is answered at once with `{answers: {}}` (the `decline` disposition), which Codex reads as no answer and goes on from; one for another thread or turn gets the `-32601` error any other owner's request gets. Codex 0.166.0 sends none in Doan's modes, because its Default collaboration mode disables the blocking tool and Doan never sets `collaborationMode`, so there is no Codex question card. A declined request whose server id a held permission still owns fails the protocol (the permission's answer would be a second response); MCP elicitations decline the same way.
  - ACP: only Antigravity asks, through `session/request_permission`. `acpQuestion` (`acp.ts`) reads the shape: a non-empty `toolCall.title`, two to eight options that are all `allow_once` with distinct names and no reject, within the limits; anything else stays a permission request, which can still be answered. The branch lives in the ACP runner's request handler (`acpRunner.ts`), not in `AcpConnection.onRequestOf`, because the connection does not know the provider; Cursor and Copilot requests of the same shape stay permissions. `acp.test.ts` pins the shape; real Antigravity tool permissions carry `kind`, `rawInput` and a `reject_once`. A question shares the runner's request table with permissions, tagged by kind. Send replies `selected` with the chosen `optionId`; Skip, Stop and expiry `cancelled`. The question's `toolCallId` is recorded as already shown, so its later completion produces no activity step. Cursor's `cursor/ask_question` is still answered `skipped`.
  - A question marked `secret` has its answer passed to the provider but left out of `question.resolved` (`storedAnswers`), and the thread masks its field and folded answer. No runner produces one now.
  - Claude cancellation: a question or permission whose `canUseTool` `options.signal` aborts (the CLI's `control_cancel_request`, or the SDK closing every open request as it cleans up) expires at once with Stop's record (`question.resolved` or `permission.resolved` `expired`), so its card folds as expired; one whose signal is already aborted is denied "Request expired." with no card. A request already answered or expired has left its map, so nothing is recorded twice.
- **Permission modes** (`conversation/permissionMode.ts`, renderer-safe with type-only imports; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Permission picker'): four common ids, mapped per runtime there. Modes are passed only for doan-owned conversations; a legacy-external run gets none, so a terminal session's own overrides stay.
  - Claude: a picked mode is the turn's `permissionMode`, Full access with `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions`.
