When folder settings disable bypass the SDK starts in default without failing, so no failure code exists for it. After a Plan approval the SDK's `system/status` `permissionMode` is followed only on a turn sent in Plan while the stored mode is still `plan` (a mode the person picked during the turn wins); `bypassPermissions`, `auto` and `dontAsk` read as Ask first.
  - Claude run mode (`claudeRunMode.ts`, pure; `runners.ts` forwards, `service.ts` applies): an Ask first turn passes no mode, so the profile's or project's `defaultMode` decides; project and local settings' `auto` and `bypassPermissions` are ignored and start as `default`, the profile's apply, `bypassPermissions` without `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions` (Claude Code 2.1.312 probe). The conversation keeps the mode Claude reports in its own id (`auto`, `dontAsk`, `acceptEdits`, `plan`, `bypassPermissions`) as `claudeRunMode`; "none" is only the key's absence, never a `default` string. It is set or cleared only from a doan-owned Claude turn sent in Ask first while Ask first is still stored, by the first top-level `system/init` of that send (a later init comes from a message steered after the reply began, which the CLI runs as its next turn in the same process and which can report a `setMode` allowed there); by a Plan turn's approval that moves the stored mode to Ask first (into another mode the key stays, since it records an Ask first turn); and by an Ask first turn's approval only while that send's first init and the mode the process reported last are both `plan` (so a `setMode` status after the approval, an "Always allow" on a Write, is not one). Any other `system/status` is ignored, and nothing counts after the turn ended, Stop included, because the CLI keeps sending frames until its result. The stored mode and the run mode go in one index save and one `changed`. A picker change, a send in another mode, a failure before the init or a connection change leaves the key; the renderer shows it only for Claude while Ask first is shown (`permissionPickerView`), and `summary.ts` carries it only for doan-owned Claude entries.
  - Codex: `turn/start` approval and sandbox overrides persist into later turns and across `thread/resume`, and `null` does not reset them (0.163.0), so every doan-owned turn states both, Ask first included (`codexTurnPermission` in `agentAuth/fence.ts`). Outside Full access the sandbox is one of Doan's permission profiles, named by `permissions` (experimental API): `doan` for Ask first and Plan, `doan-workspace` for Accept edits ('Agent authentication' 'Fence' below); such a turn never also sends a `sandboxPolicy`, which combined with a profile closes all of userData. Full access sends `sandboxPolicy` `dangerFullAccess` to a server inside the outer fence. Approval is Ask first's own policy, `on-request` for Accept edits and `never` for Plan and Full access. `config/read` returns null for unset policies and the real default depends on project trust, so each server reads Ask first's approval policy once from an ephemeral `thread/start` (no rollout, no model request) sent after `config/read`, with `mcp_servers.<name>.enabled = false` for every server `config/read` names so the probe starts none (an empty `mcp_servers` override merges and turns nothing off, and a name the config lacks fails the start; 0.171.0); a failed or incomplete reply refuses the open with `session_unavailable` rather than running without a stated policy.
  - ACP: the mode lists come with the model list from `session/new`; after `session/new` or `session/load` the runner sets the mapped mode (Copilot also `allow_all`) with `session/set_config_option` on every turn, Ask first included, because all three servers keep the last mode across `session/load`. A mode the provider cannot map sets Ask first; a value the session already reports is not sent again. When the server does not list the mode Ask first maps to, the picker is hidden (`permissionOptions`); when it lists no mode the table names, nothing is set.
  - Storage: conversation metadata `permissionMode` (absent means Ask first, whose stored id is `default`), set by send or by `conversation:setPermissionMode` when the picker changes; queued messages carry none and use the stored mode when sent, and a steered message runs in the running turn's mode. An unknown id reads as Ask first for that field only, an exception to the index and preferences rules below, because a newer build's id would otherwise invalidate the whole file; it reads narrower, never wider, and the next save drops it. Last used per provider is ui-state `home.permissions`. `claudeRunMode` is a separate optional key beside it (above); an unknown value drops that key only.
- **Fast** (`models.ts`, `ipc.ts`, `runners.ts`, `transport.ts`; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Fast switch'): Claude and Codex doan-owned conversations only.
  - Reading: `ModelOption.fast`. Claude rows with `supportsFastMode === true`; Codex rows whose `serviceTiers` hold id `priority` (its `description` kept; `name` and the deprecated `additionalSpeedTiers` are not used). The Claude list read passes `settings: { fastMode: true }`, since without it the reason is always `sdk_opt_in_required`, and `applyClaudeFastUnavailable` maps `initializationResult()`'s account-level `fast_mode_disabled_reason` (`free`, `extra_usage_disabled`, `preference`) to `fast.unavailable` on every Fast row. An initialize without the `forceLoginOrgUUID` pin Doan's runs carry can answer `on` before Claude Code's org check settles; with the pin it waits for it (observed 2.1.319). A list is cached like any other, so a changed account setting shows after a new read.
  - Sending: `ipc.ts` computes the turn's effective Fast: the stored or newly picked choice, and this run's list says the turn's model (the request's, else the stored one, else the list's default row) has usable Fast. Starts, handoff starts and queue flushes judge through `QueueOptions.fast` (`ModelCatalog.fast`). Claude gets `settings: { fastMode: true }` on effective-Fast runs only, on every run including resumes; a project `.claude/settings.json` `fastMode` does not opt an SDK run in. Codex: every doan-owned `turn/start` names `serviceTierForTurn` (`"priority"` or `"default"`), which holds for that turn only; `serviceTier` would persist into the thread and its `thread/resume` (observed 0.166.0). A legacy-external turn names none.
  - Outcome: a requested Claude turn that ends done or failed turns its last result's `fast_mode_state`/`fast_mode_disabled_reason` into `fast.unused` (`claudeFastOutcome`); `on`, a missing or unknown state, and an interrupted turn give none. `plan`, `extra_usage`, `model` and `policy` add `turnedOff`: the service saves the index without `fast` before appending the event, and a failed save takes the persistence-failure path, so no line claims an off the index does not hold. Codex reports no tier a turn ran at, so nothing is recorded for it.
  - Storage: conversation metadata `fast?: true`, the person's choice, kept across a model without Fast. Last used per provider is ui-state `home.fast`, written only by the switch and by a `turnedOff` event received live (never a replayed one); changes made before the startup read are laid per provider over the read map (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01).
- **Provider built-in commands** (tables and pure rules in `providerCommands.ts`, renderer-safe with type-only imports; lists in `skills.ts`; wiring in `service.ts`, `runners.ts`, `acpRunner.ts`; renderer `commandView.ts`; product rules PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Built-in commands'; cases in the `*.commands.test.ts` files):
  - Tables: `COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` (per provider, the CLI version a live check ran and the commands it confirmed), `COMMAND_ALIASES` and `UNSUPPORTED_COMMANDS` (card names). A row joins only after a live check at that version. Lists (`allowedCommands`) need the installed version at or above the table's; Claude and Codex list the whole table, because Claude's SDK command list lacks `/review`, which runs, and Codex's app-server reports none; the SDK's `builtin` rows only lend a description unless it is the placeholder "Built-in command.". Main judges a send and a stored turn (`commandName`) by the whole table, not the installed version or an ACP list, since the history keeps neither and this run and a restart must read a turn the same way.
  - Delivery: a command, or an ACP conversation's unconfirmed one (`passthroughName`), goes out as its own text without `turnPrompt` and gets no title. Claude and ACP runners send that text. Codex maps it (`commandRequest`): `/compact` to `thread/compact/start`, which answers `{}` and opens a turn of its own that the runner takes from the next `turn/started` within `COMPACTION_START_MS`; `/review` to `review/start` with target `uncommittedChanges`, or `custom` with the text after it. A command with anchors or attachments, and a card name at start, send, queue or schedule, is `bad_request`.
  - Codex `/review` mode: `review/start` carries no permission mode, and Codex keeps a turn's mode for later turns, so a review runs in the mode last stated. `statedModes` keeps the mode a Doan conversation's non-command turn stated once it ended done; a failed or stopped turn that stated another mode, and a turn whose end was not persisted, drop it. A `/review` whose known mode is missing or not the request's is refused `command_mode_stale`. Memory only: the history does not record a turn's mode, so after a restart the first `/review` waits for a message. Legacy-external conversations are not checked.
  - ACP list lifetime: servers push `available_commands_update` at their own times (Copilot before `session/new` answers and with every prompt, every server during `session/load`). The runner passes each push for its session; one before the answer is kept per session id (only the latest, at most `EARLY_SESSIONS` sessions) and passed once the answer names the session. The service keeps each conversation's last list with the connection that pushed it, in memory; a push from a run whose provider connection has since changed is dropped, an unreadable push keeps the same connection's list, and the list answers only for the conversation's own root and provider (`acpCommands`, `acpPassthrough`). Before a conversation's first session there is none.
  - Claude session pin: a Claude run ends the turn as `session_unavailable` when a top-level `system/init` or `result` names a session other than the one it asked for. The one exception is a send whose text is `/clear` (aliases go out as `/clear`): the first `system/init` after a top-level `conversation_reset` with `trigger: "clear"` moves the run to its `session_id` once; `conversation_reset.new_conversation_id` is not a session id. At a done end the service follows the move only after `claudeClearedSession` (`io.ts`) finds the new transcript in the old one's project folder with every recorded cwd the conversation's, and no other indexed conversation holds that session; then it rewrites the binding and moves the reservation, else the turn fails and both stay. A plan approval's reset (`trigger: "plan_mode_exit"`) is not followed.
  - Turns and boundaries: `readConversation` answers a `commands` field (`CommandTurns` in `commandTurns.ts`) holding the boundaries after command turns. The renderer names each command turn from its first message and sets `usageStale` (the last boundary is a clear and no later turn reported `context.usage`, so the record's last value is the previous session's) in `threadCommands` (`commandView.ts`). Both sides read a stored message as a command through `messageCommand` (`providerCommands.ts`), which never reads a handoff's first message (`origin`) as one. Boundaries are not stored: in this run the provider's event decides a command turn this run sent (Claude `compact_boundary`, Codex's `contextCompaction` item, Copilot's "Compacted conversation history" text; `commandRecords.ts`, Codex's in `transport.ts`), and a clear counts only once the conversation followed the session; after a restart every table `/compact` or Claude `/clear` turn that ended done is one. A boundary found in this run is pushed on `conversation:boundary` (`BoundarySignal` in `types.ts`; preload `onConversationBoundary`, also the prototype window's), never written; the renderer (`ConversationStore.boundaries`) merges it with the read by turn id.
  - Claude text results arrive as `system/local_command_output` (the stdout tag stripped) or as a `result` with `num_turns: 0`, both as the reply; after a compaction boundary the CLI's own summary and output are not shown.
- **Quit and process cleanup** (`process.ts`): only detached groups Doan spawned are signalled, and for an ACP server or a Claude run also the process groups its descendants started: Antigravity runs each tool command in a group of its own and ends neither a background command on `session/cancel` nor any command on SIGTERM, and Claude Code's SIGTERM handling leaves a background command that ignores SIGTERM running ('Background process groups'), so a stop reads the process table (`listProcesses`) before its first signal and sends those groups the same SIGTERM and SIGKILL (`descendantGroups`). A kill reads no table and signals the owned group and the groups an earlier stop found: Claude kills only with an empty background set, ahead of the model call the CLI makes 16-119 ms later, and a read takes about 20 ms. They are found only while the server lives, since its exit hands its children to launchd; once the leader has exited the signal goes at once, and a command a tool shell backgrounded and left before the stop is not found. The caller's cleanup wait ends once the group and those groups are observed gone, at most 2 s, but release follows the owned group's liveness alone, since a group can outlive its leader. Quit closes every run first, then waits until `appQuit` interruptions are persisted; a persistence error that a retry could fix cancels that quit rather than pretending it saved, and the next quit writes it again. A history that had already stopped taking writes gets no `appQuit`, as it gets no interruption from a Stop, so its known refusal does not cancel the quit; the same holds for a refusal every later append meets (the size limit, content that no longer parses) and for a second failure of the same write, which are logged and not written again, so no quit, update install or logout stays cancelled for good. Hiding the window keeps the backend alive and does not cancel a send's exit wait; Quit still cleans up while hidden. A crash can leave an agent writing files; no startup sweep or user-daemon takeover is promised.

**IPC contract.** The main window's main frame invokes these channels, and the prototype window's main frame is a second sender, scoped to the conversations of the canvas it plays (§3 'Prototype window' 'Sender role'); thumbnail windows and canvas frames are excluded. `conversation:event`, `conversation:stage`, `conversation:listChanged` and `conversation:handoff` go to the main window and, filtered to the playing canvas, to the prototype window. Channels and shapes: `app/src/main/conversation/ipc.ts`, `app/src/preload/index.ts` and `app/src/preload/prototype.ts`. `ipc.ts` validates roots, exact listed canvas identity, opaque IDs and bounded text/anchors; service admission rechecks mutable facts. Failures return `{error: code}`; `conversation:resumeTarget` refuses an invalid request the same way and answers a valid one it cannot resume with `{available: false, reason}`.

- `canvas` is required except for `newDesign`, which forbids it; a `subject` field is `bad_request` in every mode. Resume takes its runtime from the association.
- `model`, `effort`, `fast` and `attachments` pass `validateSendExtras` (`ipc.ts`, rules in its comment); resume and legacy-external conversations accept none, and `fast` is also `bad_request` on `conversation:queue` and Send now. The renderer sends `fast` on an existing conversation only when the person switched it, and a model or effort only when the person changed it, so a send after a restart, before any list read, still runs on the index's choice. At acceptance main saves the choice in the index before appending `user.message`.
- `conversation:answerQuestion` (preload `answerConversationQuestion`) takes `outcome` `answered` or `skipped` and, when answered, each question's chosen option indexes and Other text. `ipc.ts` checks only shape and limits; the runner holding the question checks the fit with `questionAnswer` and its `answerQuestion` returns `ok`, `stale` or `bad_request`, which the service turns into errors without rereading history; a runner without `answerQuestion` is `stale`. The renderer shows no failure for `stale`, because `question.resolved` follows and folds the card.

**List and seen** (`summary.ts`; cases in `summary.test.ts`).
- `conversation:list` folds every history on each call (no cache; a known cost). Only a turn accepted in this run can be `running`, so a leftover live entry never revives an earlier run's crashed turn. `working` is main's wider view (including a judgement or queue still pending); anything that limits a root counts `working`, not `phase`. `background` (a done turn's background work, 'Background work after a done turn') and `stopping` (a Stop of the running turn was taken) are memory-only facts of the run, never folded from history. `preview` never shows an activity's command (a running command reads as running one); while waiting it reads `Needs your answer:` with the permission summary, which can name the command being approved, or the first question; it reads only the head of a long message, so a hostile message costs linear time.
- Unseen compares `seenSeq` with the last event's seq other than `conversation.*` markers, `handoff.resolved`, `handoff.missed` and `canvas.drawn` (not the last terminal event; `toSee` in `seenEvents.ts`, which the renderer's seen end imports so the two cannot differ), so a crash-folded turn is unseen while a bind, an earlier build's archive or restore line, a handoff resolution or a canvas card written after the end never re-marks a seen end; only a conversation whose turn ended can be unseen. An entry without `seenSeq` counts as seen, so an upgrade lights nothing up (and a migrated archived entry, which loses `seenSeq`, reads as seen; 'Device-local storage and limits'). `markSeen` only moves forward.
- `waitingSince` (the earliest still-pending request's time, only while waiting) orders waiting conversations for the canvas panel's pick and its folded mark, since output and partial answers move `lastActivityAt`. `startedBy` is `handoff` when the first `user.message` carries a handoff origin and `promotion` for a canvas conversation whose session origin is `newDesign`; absent, a person started it. An unreadable history reads `promotion` from the index only, so an unreadable handoff start reads as the person's.
- `conversation:delete` answers `busy` both for a working conversation and while the app quits. Main sends `app:quitting` (true) in `before-quit` before awaiting the conversation service's quit, and never sends false after an aborted quit, because the service stays quitting until restart; the renderer shows no refusal toast after it.
- Renderer deletion (App's `confirmDelete`; the mark is `ConversationStore.startDelete`/`deleted`/`cancelDelete`, availability and texts in `conversationDelete.ts`): the id is marked deleting before the call, so a list that arrives before the reply does not move the deleted conversation's draft onto the new conversation draft (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26 ordering). Success and `not_found` run every run's `deleted()` (revision bump, ui-state memory, pending picks, promotion lines, Home strips) and release the mark in their own `finally`; other refusals release it and re-sync. The confirmation closes in an outer `finally`, so a throw cannot leave an undismissable dialog or a stuck mark.
- `conversation:listChanged {root}` is sent after the write it reports; conversation events carry only seq-bearing records, so the list needs its own notice.

**Queue** (`queue.ts`, `drafts.ts`; product rules in PRODUCT §5 item 1 'Project conversations').
- `conversation:queue` accepts only while the conversation is `working` (`busy` while the app quits); otherwise `not_running` and the renderer sends instead. Main holds queued attachments from then on. `queue_limit` refuses an item that would push the queue plus the returned draft past one message or the file bound.
- Items stay on disk marked `sending` until the combined send is accepted, so a crash right after acceptance returns an already sent message as a draft rather than losing one.
- A queue's flush sends a tick after the terminal event's own handling. That deferred send goes on only while the same live entry still holds the same hold, because every path that hands a queue back as a draft (a Stop, a stop of its provider, a handoff's cancel, a deletion) first drops the hold, and the copy the flush captured must not go out beside the draft; a conversation deleted meanwhile gets its queue back as a draft instead (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-14).
- Send now (`conversation:sendQueuedNow`, `ConversationService.sendNow`) marks only that item `sending` on disk, rechecks admission after every await, and answers only once the agent read the message (the `user.message` appended with the running `turnId`, the item removed and its attachments consumed), `not_running` when refused or when the turn ended first, `stale` for an item already sending or gone. Right before a turn's terminal handling (and on deletion, quit and persistence failure) unread steers are unmarked, so the item is back in place before `ended()` judges holding and the done flush sends it in the original order; no read can follow a terminal, so nothing waits on the provider's reply. A read is authoritative: while the same live turn runs and the item is still queued, a read that arrives before the steer reply or after an answered refusal still appends it. The terminal events are published before a turn-end `not_running`, so the renderer sees the ended turn and shows no refusal line. The list's `queued` count excludes `sending` items. The renderer moves a pressed row out of the queue into the thread at once (`ConversationStore.sending`, `withSending` in `threadItems.ts`), above an open permission or question card; since the appended `user.message` carries no queue item id, it drops that entry when the IPC answers success, and on a refusal or turn end the row returns to its queue slot. That thread entry is renderer state only, so a reload during Send now shows the message nowhere until it is read or refused.

**Usage-limit schedule** (`usageLimit.ts`, `service.ts`; product rules in PRODUCT §5 item 3 'Usage limit').
- Pure rules, each with its reason in a comment, in `conversation/usageLimit.ts`: `limitResetFromUsage`, `limitState`, `sendDecision` (ACP providers, legacy external and starts never schedule), `turnHadActivity` (Claude Code's limit refusal is a `<synthetic>` assistant frame with no activity, fixtures `claude-rate-limit-*`), `claudeResetFromDetail`, `autoSchedule` and `sendAt`. A reset that passed still counts when it is the one a scheduled send went out for, so the same-reset retry can see it.
- Reset source: after a `rate_limited` turn the runner reads usage on the run (`usageAtTurnEnd(read, true)`; Claude on the open `Query`, Codex on the live app-server), and the service asks `UsageService.limitWindows(provider, connectionId)`, which waits only for that provider's running reads and returns nothing for another `connectionId`. That read skips the 5-minute interval and the hidden-window rule (`shouldRead`), never a blocked connection. Claude Code's limit text is the fallback; Codex's text is not parsed. The SDK's `rate_limit_event` stays unused.
- State lives in memory only, never in events, `index.json` or `drafts.json`: a new field on `turn.failed` would make older builds unable to read the history (Device-local storage below), and a reset in `drafts.json` would make a restart send by itself. Limit state is keyed by `connectionId` and cleared by `connectionChanged` (called for Claude Code and Codex from the auth publish in `main/index.ts`) and sign-out. A `Schedule` per conversation holds `at`, `reset`, provider, connection and whether the same-reset retry was used; its items stay ordinary `drafts.json` queued items, so a restart returns them as `restarted` drafts.
- Scheduled is a state of its own, neither `working` nor holding the root: a held queue would make every Send `busy` and a project conversation would hold its root until the reset. `ConversationSummary` carries `limitResetsAt` (the connection's live reset) and `scheduledAt` (the reset, not the send time; set only when scheduled, not working and `queued > 0`). `conversation:send` under a limit answers `{ scheduled: { itemId, at } }` without accepting the message (no history write, attachments held as for a queue) and refuses picker fields as `bad_request`; `conversation:scheduled` returns the items (`ScheduledItem`, with `turnId` only on the item repeating a failed turn's own message, which the renderer uses to hide that turn's Edit and resend). A `rate_limited` refusal of a start carries `resetsAt`.
- Entry: a no-activity `rate_limited` turn with a known reset and a single `user.message` without a handoff origin re-queues that message first, its files re-issued by path (`QueueFiles.reissue`, which refuses and releases every issued id when any path is gone or changed kind); the queue already behind a limited turn waits for the same reset whatever the turn did. The post-limit lookup (`LimitLookup` on the live entry) is ended by Stop, `stopRuns`, `connectionChanged` and deletion, and the turn ID and stop generation are checked again after its await (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27). The `rate_limited` `turn.failed` is published with `deferNotice`; the notification goes out exactly once, unless the turn's message was scheduled again.
- Timers: one unref'd timeout per conversation at `sendAt(reset)`, split past 2^31−1 ms, through an injectable `ScheduleClock` (tests use a fake clock and microtask drains, no real waits). The callback rechecks the schedule's identity. `powerMonitor` `resume` re-judges every schedule by the wall clock, because timers stop while the Mac sleeps. A due schedule that cannot go (its own conversation still `working`, a pending judgement included, or `project_busy`) waits and is tried again on the next list change (`wakeDue`). Sending reuses `flush`/`submit`; a Stop before acceptance marks the live entry `cancelled`, `checkSend` refuses it after prepare and open, and buffered events that already ended the turn skip `sendTurn` (this also covers a person's own send and an ordinary queue flush).
- Clock hour cycle: the renderer's `Intl` does not follow macOS's 24-hour setting (measured on Electron 45: `AppleICUForce24HourTime` on, the renderer resolved `h12`). `main/clockHourCycle.ts` reads `AppleICUForce24HourTime`/`AppleICUForce12HourTime`, else the system locale's hour cycle, and passes it to the main window and the prototype window as `--doan-clock-hour12` in `additionalArguments`; each preload exposes `clockHour12` (`window.doan`, `window.doanPrototype`), and `localClock()` reads either, falling back to `Intl` only where neither is present (tests). A change of the setting applies after a restart.
- Renderer (`conversationStore.ts`, `limitReset.ts`): a `scheduled` read runs for the shown conversation when `scheduledAt` or `queued` changes, each read with its own token so a late answer never overwrites a newer one; a failed read and any list transition away from scheduled clear its cache whether shown or not, so a same-reset retry is read again; a count that dips and returns moves the read mark through `readsStarted(root)` (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-05).

**Renderer primitives** (`app/src/renderer/`). The thread and the docked composer take everything through props; only their hosts (the project Overview, the canvas conversation panel and the prototype window's conversation panel) call preload. They call it through the call objects the hosts pass (`ConversationStore`, `CanvasRun`, `DockedSupport`, `useProjectSkills`, the docked buddy shell and `visibleClock` take them too): `window.doan` in the main window, and in the prototype window, which has no `window.doan`, the members of `window.doanPrototype` that share its names and signatures (`SharedWithMain` in `preload/prototype.ts`; the typecheck and `renderer/prototype/calls.test.ts` pin the match). The prototype window has its own `ConversationStore`, so its drafts, question choices and attachment chips are its own; a conversation a later list no longer names goes through the store's `deleted` path there too, so its draft never moves to another conversation.
- Streamed deltas: a live `message.delta` merges into the `ConversationStore` thread and reaches the runs' listeners (seen marks, promotions), but does not call the store host's `changed`, because a redraw of App (or of the prototype window) per delta was the largest main-thread cost while a reply streamed. The hosts that draw a conversation redraw from `ConversationStore.subscribeThread`, so anything outside them must not depend on a delta: App reads only what other events change (titles from `user.message`, drafts, lists). Every other event still calls `changed`.
- Composer turn (`composerTurn` in `dockedRules.ts`, `composerAction` in `composerRules.ts`, shared by both hosts): the merged events decide before the list, which lags them. A terminal while the list still says `working` (a promotion or handoff judgement, a queue going out) reads `holding`: there is no turn to stop, so an empty field shows Send unavailable, text queues, and Stop shows only while queue rows are drawn, which it returns as the draft. Rows are counted as drawn, those going out included, since the list's `queued` excludes `sending` items; `ConversationStore.syncQueues` keeps a queue's rows while the conversation holds after the turn they were queued under and gave nothing back, until the next turn opens; the store runs it when a `turn.started` event merges as well as after list reads, because the start arrives before the list read and the rows would otherwise read the new turn as running and offer Send now for the message it carries. Holding queues, so the project limit on starting a turn does not apply yet (`dockedAvailability`), and project skills are read again on running → holding, not on holding → idle (`useProjectSkills.ts`). Focus on Stop when it turns into the unavailable Send moves to the field.
- Thread focus (`ConversationThread.tsx`): a permission or question card that folds while focus is in it (answered, skipped or stopped from it, or expired, a CLI cancellation included), or while its Other field holds unsent text, moves focus by `composerRules.focusAfter`: the next waiting request, else the composer. Focus is read as the render begins, before its commit removes the card's controls, and focus the person moved elsewhere stays. The background work line's Stop follows the same reading: when the line goes while its Stop has focus, focus moves to the composer field; Stopping keeps the button (`aria-disabled`, not `disabled`), so focus stays on it.
- Tooltips (`components/ui/tooltip.tsx`): a trigger whose control is `aria-disabled` (`true` or `"true"`) or carries `data-own-description`, and has its own `aria-describedby`, keeps only that description while the tooltip is open, because Radix's Slot would add the content's id and the reason would be read twice; every other trigger keeps Radix's description.
- Progress ring: `ProgressRing.tsx` draws only the track and value and holds the danger threshold (`ringDanger`), shared by the Agent usage ring and the context ring (`ContextRing.tsx`). Box size, inks and the rotation that starts the arc at 12 o'clock come from each ring's own class, so the usage ring's `.usage` rules do not reach the 16px context ring.
- Composer: docked rules live in `dockedRules.ts`, not `dockedComposer.ts`, because macOS's case-insensitive resolution would collide with `DockedComposer.tsx`. Both `composerShape` shapes render the same keyed field and change only `data-shape`, because remounting would drop focus and IME composition; the shape returns to capsule only when the field is empty, so it never flips at the capsule width.
- Chips reach the canvas panel's composer from a canvas-script pick or, for the size bubble's Add to message, from the current selection through the same `targetAnchor` (§3 'Picking and locate'); both go in with the field's `insert`.
- Chip field (`chipField.ts`, `anchorField.ts`): letter input and IME composition are left to the browser and read back after `input`; model edits never run during a composition, and line deletes are computed from segments and drawn rows, never from Chromium's range. A chip requested during a composition goes in only when no new composition started in that task, because Korean ends and restarts a composition per syllable.
  - Chip anchors live in a WeakMap, never in DOM attributes. A line-start chip gets a zero-width filler text node because Chromium cannot place a caret before a non-editable item that starts a line; fillers are tracked by node, so a U+200B the person typed is kept.
  - Clipboard: `text/plain` (chip names) plus `application/x-doan-field+json` `{v: 1, scope, segments}`, validated on read; a field pastes chips only when its `chipScope` equals the clipboard's `scope`.
  - Project skill highlight and lists: `render()` draws the runs `fieldRuns` (`skillWords.ts`) finds: skill words in editable `<span class="skill-word">`, and a list item's depth spaces and marker in `list-depth` and `list-mark` spans (the line rules are the pure functions in `composerLists.ts`, shared with the sent bubble and `plainTitle`); segments, units and the clipboard are unchanged. `misdrawn()` compares those runs (class, list kind, depth) and the filler after a marker with the drawn spans, so an equal drawing is never redrawn. A marker whose body is empty or starts with a chip gets the zero-width filler, for the same caret reason as a line-start chip.
  - List caret and keys: ← and → jump over a line's prefix (depth spaces and marker) and `selectionchange` moves a caret stranded inside it to the body start, because Chromium otherwise stops inside the hidden marker and its Backspace deletes half of it; for the same reason Backspace at the body start is handled in `beforeinput` (`removeMarker`). Making the marker `contenteditable=false` was rejected: ← from the body jumps to the line above and typing into an empty item is lost. Shift+Enter (`continueList`), Tab (`indentItem`, through the `ComposerField` handle after the host's `onKey`) and that Backspace are each one history step of their own. Ctrl+Tab moves focus in code (`moveFocus`), because a contenteditable in Electron 45 does not move focus on Ctrl+Tab.
  - Keys that end a composition: with the macOS Korean IME a Shift+Enter or Tab pressed while composing arrives as a keyCode 229 keydown, the syllable's commit and `compositionend`, an immediate synthetic keyup, then the same key again as an ordinary keydown (with its `insertLineBreak`) in the same task, before a 0 ms timer set at `compositionend` (measured in Electron 45). The composing key only records the press (a composing Tab is prevented so focus stays); the resent key does the edit and clears the record whoever handles it (the `/` list may take it); the timer acts on a record that is still there, once, for an input method that does not resend. Do not dedupe by keyup: the synthetic keyup comes before the resend. Nothing is redrawn during a composition or, for an equal value, between `compositionend` and its deferred repair (a host render in that gap would split the next Korean syllable); a different host value, such as the clear after a send, still applies at once.
  - Known gap: Chromium exposes no `aria-autocomplete` on a contenteditable combobox; the ARIA 1.1 wrapping structure that would expose it was not adopted because it adds remount and focus risk.
- Sent bubble lists (`UserMessage` in `ConversationThread.tsx`, `bubbleBlocks` in `composerLists.ts`): list lines draw as `role="list"`/`role="listitem"` blocks, not `ul`/`ol`, with the marker and depth spaces as real text spans, because Chromium leaves `ul`/`ol` markers and depth spaces out of a copied selection. Items lay out inline-block, not as grid or subgrid, because Chromium copies grid items on separate lines (`- ` then the body). Each list sizes its marker column from its widest marker (`data-mark`, read by `attr()`, since the design checks allow custom properties only on `:root`), and the drawn indentation stops at four depth steps so a deep item keeps a usable width.
- Agent replies render as Markdown without `rehype-raw` (raw HTML shows as text, images as alt text); `https:` links are the only anchors, opened through `shell:openLink`. `[name](doan:<canvas-id>/frame/<frame-id>)` and `[name](doan:<canvas-id>/element/<doan-id>)` become anchor chips: `replyUrlTransform` keeps a `doan:` href for the thread's `a` component only, `parseReplyAnchor` (`app/src/main/conversation/replyAnchor.ts`, no imports, shared with eval) reads it (lowercase scheme, three pieces split before percent-decoding, the canvas ID pattern of §1 Conventions 2, `frame` or `element`, a raw id piece without `?` or `#` that decodes to 1–200 UTF-16 units without control characters), any other `doan:` link (including `doan://open`) shows as its text, and no `doan:` href reaches an `<a>`. `replyChipTarget` looks the canvas ID up only in the conversation root's list (locate when it is the canvas on screen, else open, missing, duplicate or unavailable); opening goes through `decideOpen` as a deep link does, and an element waits for the first layout tied to root and path. Link text never names a chip; footnote ids get a per-message prefix. Agent output is untrusted data, the same rule as selection text (§3 'Selection'). `rendererImports.test.ts` includes the thread and docked modules as extra roots.

**Worktree switch** (`WorktreeSwitch.tsx`, `worktreeSwitchModel.ts`). The sidebar project row, the Overview header and the canvas header render the one `WorktreeSwitch`,, pinned by a source test, so a fix reaches all three. `worktreeSwitchModel` builds the rows; the canvas header passes `headerRowState`, which matches the canvas by path string in each registered root's own list, and moves through `headerMove` to `openShellCanvas` with the view kept (`CanvasController.openCanvas` `keepView`). The canvas identity is the root and the path, never the path alone, so the open canvas, the conversation panel, the selection and Back/Forward follow the new root. Only the sidebar instance stops click and key propagation, because the tree's ←/→ folding and typeahead would otherwise take them; in the headers the keys must reach the canvas controller (Esc ends a pick). The canvas header keeps one instance across canvases and closes its menu through `useMenuFocus`, shared with the frame drawer and the zoom menu, so a move returns focus to the switch rather than the page.

**Input and prompt.** Limits are `LIMITS` in `app/src/main/conversation/messageText.ts`, which has only type imports so main and the renderer share it; the same module holds `messageLine` (titles, queue rows, previews and notifications), which drops each line's list marker and depth spaces with `plainTitle` before making one line, and `LIST_MARKER`, the one marker rule the renderer's list functions also read. `composeRequest` removes only blank lines around the text (`trimBlankLines`), so a list's first-line indentation reaches the agent. A queued row's command check reads the stored request text as main does, never the display line. the renderer must not import `prompt.ts`, which reaches ajv ('Renderer import boundary' above). `validAnchor` (`prompt.ts`) checks anchors; text `{{n}}` markers are 1-based and must cover exactly the supplied anchors. Every turn prompt carries `OPEN_GUIDANCE` and `LINK_GUIDANCE` (`prompt.ts`) right before the `doan check` line: the conversation is already inside Doan, so the agent does not offer to open canvases, and the line says it overrides the Doan guide's offer rule (§1 rule E); running `doan open` when the person asks still works. `LINK_GUIDANCE` teaches the reply chip link form ('Renderer primitives' above) with real, percent-encoded ids and says such a link only marks a place, not an offer to open. Only the app teaches this form: the guide does not, because terminal answers have no chips.

`turnPrompt` (`prompt.ts`) serializes `{root, canvas?}`, anchors and attachment descriptors as untrusted data separated from the user's text, attached files' names and contents included. Account, profile, session, title, model and effort never reach the prompt. It adds `HOME_GUIDANCE` while a `fromHome` conversation has no canvas, `PRESET_GUIDANCE` ('Design preset reads' below) and a reminder to run doan check. `HANDOFF_GUIDANCE` comes last, after that reminder, only on a turn that may hand off (`handoffGuidanceFor`: a Doan-connected canvas conversation, not Plan, not a handoff's first turn; a Send now prompt keeps it even after the turn asked, since the turn may still make another new canvas). Only the app's prompt teaches `doan handoff`: the guide does not, because terminal agents cannot use it. It asks for one `doan handoff` per new canvas, each its own plain command with the message as a single quoted argument, because Codex allows `doan` by a command-prefix rule that a heredoc, pipe or redirection does not match, so such a call runs sandboxed and cannot reach the socket; the commands are not joined with `&&` or `;`, because whether that rule matches a joined call is unverified. A handoff's first turn uses `handoffPrompt` instead, which puts the handoff in its own `handoffText` data field, names the source canvas as `context.from`, says the text is another agent's and not the person's, and carries no `HANDOFF_GUIDANCE`.

**Events.** Types: `ConversationEvent` in `app/src/main/conversation/types.ts` (event JSONL v1, including the `permission.resolved` `"expired"` sentinel; each type's fields are commented there). An earlier build's `conversation.archived`/`conversation.restored` are read, never written: folding expires a request still open at an archive line and folds every event after it, and the thread draws neither. A `handoff.resolved` without `canvas: {path}`, which every new record writes, resolves its turn's only request and is ignored when the turn asked for several. `handoff.missed` carries the canvas's ID, so its gone check goes by ID ('Handoff' above). `fast.unused` ('Fast' above) comes at most once per turn, before `context.usage`, which comes at most once per turn and only directly before its terminal; the thread and strip do not draw `context.usage`. A deletion removes the whole history with its index entry; no event is rewritten or appended for it. A turn can hold several `user.message` events: the first opens it, and each steered message is appended when the agent reads it. A build older than these types reads their lines as damage, so it reads a canvas conversation's history with a `handoff.missed` line as damaged even when the conversation never asked for a handoff; going back to it is not supported. Turn stages are not events and never reach the history ('Turn stages' below).

Consumers subscribe **before** reading, then merge snapshot and buffered events by seq with deduplication (`state.ts:mergeEvents`) without re-registering, applying the final terminal event even if nothing follows. Restart folding marks unfinished turns crashed, unanswered permissions and questions expired and unresolved handoffs cancelled without appending events. `foldConversation` keeps open requests keyed by kind, so a `permission.resolved` never settles a question with the same id; a question wait is `waitingPermission` (the name covers both) and phase `waiting`.

**Summaries.** Activity and permission summaries are bounded readable text with `GENERIC_SUMMARY` (`transport.ts`) as fallback, never a raw provider item type. Activity and stage summaries never carry shell command text, only paths (a Codex command, a Claude `Bash` call and an ACP `execute` title all read as running a command); an ACP tool's running stage and its completed activity share `acpToolSummary` (`acp.ts`). A permission request's `summary` is what the approval lets run, never the agent's words about it: Claude's `command`, path, pattern or URL; for an MCP tool, the tool and server first and then the arguments as JSON (a Doan connector's tool shows no arguments, 'MCP connectors' below); Codex's whole command, never a parsed `commandActions` entry (Codex's own parse can name one segment of a line), with only its login-shell wrapper (`/bin/zsh -lc <script>`, a system shell) taken off when the rest decodes as one shell word, every file of a file change, or one line per kind of its `RequestPermissionProfile` (network, then every path to read, write or deny, with a piece of another shape as JSON cut at 120 characters, `codexPermissionSummary`); an ACP request's `rawInput.command`/`commands` or every path it names, else its title. It keeps up to 8192 characters, and a cut one ends in "…" (`clip`). Control, format (bidi, zero-width, tag), line and paragraph separator and other default-ignorable characters (variation selectors, Hangul fillers), except line feed and tab, read as `<U+XXXX>` in the summary, reason and details (`visibleText` in `cardText.ts`), so control and invisible characters cannot reorder or hide part of what runs; an agent's value quoted inside a detail sentence has its curly quotes escaped too (`quoted`). The agent's own text (Claude's `description`, Codex's `reason`, an ACP title that differs) is `reason`, a second line on the card, up to 512 characters. A choice that saves something beyond the request carries `detail`, the rule and where it goes (Claude's suggested permission updates, Codex's proposed command rule and network rules, the folder a Codex file change's `grantRoot` asks for on Allow for this session); an ACP Always allow says the agent saves the choice, with the agent's own name for it, and decides what it covers, because Doan cannot see that. The card shows `detail` under the request and the strip as the button's tooltip.

**Turn stages** (product rules: PRODUCT §3 'Turn stages', §5 item 3 'Turn indicator'). Stages are not events: no event type carries them, so the event JSONL format and the one-terminal-per-turn rule are unchanged, and a turn's last stage is lost at restart by design. Types: `TurnStage` and `StageSignal` `{conversationId, turnId, seq, rev, stage}` in `types.ts`.
- Normalizers return the stage after a frame beside its events (`TransportResult.stage`), and runners call `Prepared.stage` after emitting that frame's events (Codex in `dispatcher.ts`, so queued frames keep their order). Sources: Claude `content_block_start` `thinking` to that block's own `content_block_stop`, general on `system/status` `requesting` and on a top-level tool result once no top-level tool call of the turn still runs; no Claude tool stage, because the activity event already says it with the same seq, and a general on a tool block's stop would hide it; `parent_tool_use_id` frames are ignored, so a subagent's run reads as its Task activity. Codex: `item/started` of a `commandExecution` whose `commandActions` is exactly one `read` (with its path), `listFiles` or `search` (no path); other tools have no start stage; a `commandExecution`, `fileChange` or `mcpToolCall` item's `item/completed` gives general once no such item of the turn still runs. Claude and Codex keep the running calls in `TransportState.running` (`types.ts`), as ACP does, so a sibling's end never hides a tool still running; every path that ends the turn or a result clears it, so the next turn starts with none. ACP: `agent_thought_chunk` is thinking until a message chunk, a tool call or a tool update with a status; a pending or in-progress tool call is that tool; when one ends (completed, failed or another status) while other tool calls of the turn still run, the stage is the latest-started of those, and general only once none runs (Copilot runs tools in parallel). A call the runner already folded into an activity, such as an Antigravity question, still leaves that running list when it ends.
- Service: each live entry keeps its last signal, sent through the same fence as `receive()` (the live entry is current, accepted, not terminal or closed), because Stop writes `interrupted` while the normalizer still waits for the provider's result. It is cleared on the terminal, Stop, delete, quit, process exit, failed persistence and a new turn. A repeat with the same turnId, seq and stage is not sent (Copilot sends dozens of thought chunks). `seq` is the conversation's last written seq; `rev` rises per live entry (a new entry restarts it) and orders signals within a turn, because a read reply and an earlier push have no guaranteed order (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-26). `conversation:read` adds `stage` only while the turn runs.
- Renderer: `turnStatus.ts` decides the indicator from `threadItems`' output, the turn and the stage. A turn opens at its first `user.message`, not only at `turn.started`: main pushes the two separately, and the indicator must be in the render that draws the person's message. For an existing conversation's send, main writes both in one `ConversationStore.appendAll` call, so a validation, size-limit or open/read failure writes neither and no lone message reads as a running turn; a short write is not rolled back, as for any append (only journaled changes recover a prefix). A stage counts only for the history's current turn and while no event came after it (`stage.seq >= lastSeq`); otherwise the event rule applies (last activity, the tool before an Allow-folded permission, else general). The store keeps the higher `rev` within a turn. Settling (300 ms) happens in the thread's display state and resets when the turn id changes. The status and the thread's last activity row are judged on the items above the Send now messages in flight (`withSending`), which the agent reads only later. Whether that row runs is `activityRun` (`turnStatus.ts`), from the settled status, never from the events, since an activity marks a tool's start (Claude, Codex) or its end (ACP), never both: it runs with the status's words while the status is a tool; the last step reads "Running a command" only when the event rule took the status from that step (`TurnStatus` tool `step`); an ACP row runs only while the shown and the latest status are the same stage's tool, so a row born from a tool's completion never runs with that tool's words, neither before the general stage settles nor before the next tool's stage does; a Codex MCP startup line keeps its sentence as the head until the turn ends. The elapsed time lives in a child component with its own timer, so the thread does not redraw every second; it stops while `document.hidden` and recounts from `turn.started.at` when shown.

**Errors.** Codes: `known` in `app/src/main/conversation/ipc.ts`, plus `attachment_missing` (with `missing` ids and optional `replaced` ids) and the prototype window's `not_playing` (§3 'Prototype window'). `runtime_unsupported`, `session_unavailable` and the Doan-connection `not_logged_in` each cover several causes, so a consumer takes the recovery from the provider's readiness ('Agent authentication' below), which already reflects a runtime incompatibility before its refusal returns. `account_changed`, `workspace_changed` and `sign_in_changed` refuse a conversation another sign-in owns ('Sign-out and replacement'); the renderer offers a new conversation for them, never Retry. A Claude turn fails by the `error` Claude Code sets on the assistant message it makes up for an account problem (`rate_limit` → `rate_limited`, `authentication_failed` → `not_logged_in`; `CLAUDE_ERROR_CODE` in `transport.ts`), the latest assistant frame deciding, before the result's text; the text rule (`transportError`) also reads Claude's "hit your weekly limit" and Codex's "hit your usage limit" wording as `rate_limited`.

**Model lists and attachments** (`models.ts`, `attachments.ts`).

- **Models.** `conversation:models` reads each provider's own list without a model request or prompt; Codex lists models after app-server initialization without opening a thread. Effort differs per provider: Cursor encodes it in fixed model ids and refuses a changed bracket value, Antigravity as three model ids, Copilot ACP has none.
  - Claude's `default` row (`parseClaudeModels`) takes the name of the first other row with the same `resolvedModel` (e.g. "Opus 5.5"), and that paired row is dropped; the row keeps id `default` and its `default` flag, so it still follows the account's default. Without a match it keeps its own name.
  - Older models (`olderIds` in `conversation/models.ts`, its comment holds the naming rule): every parser marks `older: true` on the rows below their family's highest version. The provider's default row counts toward the highest but never folds; a name without a version never folds. It is a guess from provider naming, so a renamed scheme can leave older models current but never hides one.
  - The renderer keeps every row in `rows` and folds only when drawing (`foldRows`), so picking and the checked-row lookup still see older ids. The "Older models" row is a value-less menu item whose select is prevented, so the menu stays open. From the first unfold or fold until the menu closes, the menu stays on the side Radix painted at that moment (`avoidCollisions` off, height capped at the smaller of 384px and the available height) and older rows are drawn on the side away from the trigger, so the row stays under the pointer; before any toggle Radix still flips it (`lockedOlderSide` in `homeComposer.ts`). Measured appearance: docs/design/conversation.md 'Older model disclosure'; why a scroll correction alone cannot: POSTMORTEM 2026-11-06 'Unfolding older models moved the line under the pointer, so a second press picked a model'.
  - A read is an owned run holding its provider lease until its group is gone; a sign-out or replacement stops it without asking, since it is not a conversation (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30).
  - The list is cached per `[root, runtime, connectionId, installed version]`; a failed read is dropped.
  - The window keeps lists per `[root, provider, auth generation]`. `AuthStatusDto.generation` is raised by every stored sign-in and sign-out (`agentAuth/state.ts`), so a list read for an earlier generation is never shown for the new one: a replacement keeps the provider and may keep the email shown.
  - Model and effort are re-applied on every send, because a Claude resume and an Antigravity `session/load` fall back to the default model. Codex's `turn/start` effort persists into later turns, so a turn whose model has no stored effort names that model's `defaultEffort` for that turn only (observed with Codex 0.164.0).
- **Project skills** (`skills.ts`). The `conversation` method `skills {root, provider, conversationId?}` (same sender, argument and `project_not_open` checks as `models`) answers `{skills: SkillSummary[], passthrough?} | {error}`: for Claude Code and Codex the folder's skills (`kind: "skill"`) then the built-in commands (`kind: "command"`, 'Provider built-in commands' below), and for the others `{skills: []}` without a read; with a `conversationId`, an ACP conversation's commands and `passthrough` names are added from memory. It goes through the same listing lease as models (`readListing`, `Runner.skills`), so it does not block a send or ask before a sign-out.
  - Claude: `initializationResult().commands` of the listing query (MCP prompts appear only later in `supportedCommands()`). The SDK gives no source field, but Claude Code (2.1.284–2.1.319) ends each description with its source; `parseClaudeSkills` keeps `builtin !== true` rows ending in ` (project)`, strips that tail and drops the name `doan`. A changed tail empties the list rather than leaking user or connector items.
  - Codex: `skills/list {cwds: [root], forceReload: true}` on the listing app-server; `parseCodexSkills` keeps `scope === "repo"`, `enabled !== false` rows without `pluginId` and ignores `errors`. A folder entry without a `skills` array is malformed (`session_unavailable`), not an empty list. The Codex listing runner calls `model/list` and `skills/list` lazily, through the same transport error mapping.
  - Repository text crosses IPC, so both parsers cap the answer (`ROWS`, `NAME`, `DESCRIPTION` in `skills.ts`): names without whitespace, control characters or backtick, descriptions cut to their first line; duplicate names keep the first.
  - `SkillCatalog` caches per `[root, provider, connectionId, installed version]` for 30 s with a shared in-flight read; `prepare` runs on every request (a hit frees its lease), and a failure stores no time. Hosts (`useProjectSkills.ts`) keep lists per root, provider and auth generation, so a late answer for an earlier root or generation never changes the current highlight; the docked composer gets `skills` through props.
- **Attachments.** `attachments:*` channels (`app/src/main/attachments.ts`); the prototype window's composer has them too, except the main-window-only `attachments:capture`.
  - Main resolves the real path, opens it with `O_NOFOLLOW` and classifies the opened descriptor: a directory, or a regular file whose image signature is checked before decoding; anything else is `not_file`. Main's answer decides the kind; the renderer's `File.type` only shapes the Checking chip, because a folder named `x.png` reports `image/png`, and the path-less pasted-image fallback applies only to a `File` with no disk path. Local files and folders are never copied, changed or deleted, and a folder is never traversed. A pasted image is size-checked, decoded with `nativeImage` and re-encoded to PNG under `<userData>/attachments/<id>/`.
  - Folders: `kind: "directory"` records carry name and path and no size, in IPC, drafts and conversation history; image and file keep their required size, the event and draft `v` stay, and older records still read (an older app may not read a directory record). A folder is never an `Entry.dir`, the paste-cleanup path, so no lifecycle path can delete it. The chooser uses `openFile`, `openDirectory` and `multiSelections`; macOS resolves a chosen symlink before returning it, while a Finder drop or paste gives the link path. Finder paste arrives as `Files` with `webUtils.getPathForFile` paths, so main never reads the clipboard for it.
  - Identity: an entry keeps its input path, canonical path and `dev`/`ino`. A recheck resolves the input path again and refuses a vanished entry or a kind change as missing, and another directory at that path or a link now pointing at one as `replaced` (`attachment_missing` with `replaced` ids, a subset of `missing`), before reading any byte; changes inside a folder are not checked. Stored drafts and history keep only name, kind and path (and a file's size), so a restore after a restart re-issues by path and keeps the recorded kind: an entry of another kind is released and its chip shows Moved or deleted. Within one run a returned queue's ids stay held (`giveBack`) and `conversation:takeDraft` hands them back with the records, so the composer rechecks the same entries and a replaced folder stays Replaced; main releases ids no record claims and those of a conversation deleted before the take. After the take the conversation draft owns them (`held` chips): removing such a chip or dropping its draft removes and then releases the id, which never deletes a pasted file under its `sent` marker.
  - `attachments:capture` takes a canvas capture. `captureRequest` (`attachments.ts`) accepts only an exact rect of at least the minimum size inside the window's content; main checks only that the rect is in the window, because the renderer (the main window's main frame) already chooses attachments. `webContents.capturePage(rect)` of the visible main window includes the cross-site board iframe (OOPIF) at the display's scale. `AttachmentStore.addCaptured` saves that PNG as given (only the size cap, no signature check or re-encode) through the save routine `addPasted` uses, as a `pasted` entry with the same lifecycle. An empty image, a rejected capture and every save failure answer `capture_failed`, so the toast names the capture instead of falling back to `unreadable` and the file name. The renderer recognises a capture chip by `pasted` plus the `canvas-capture-` name prefix; `AttachmentInfo` has no capture field.
  - A start resolves ids before admission and again right before acceptance, and writes a pasted image's `sent` marker before acceptance. At launch main removes only paste directories without that marker, never reading conversation history. A re-issued pasted image (`attachments:fromPaths`) is an ordinary file entry, so removing its chip never deletes the sent copy.
  - Every provider gets attachment paths only through the prompt's descriptors; folders add no fence path and no image block. Antigravity also gets ACP image blocks for images, because its `view_file` refuses paths outside the workspace (an outside folder is readable there only through an approved shell command). Attachments outside the repo get no Doan permission prompt; Codex in Ask first (on-request/read-only for an untrusted project) reads them without asking (observed).

**Device-local storage and limits** (`store.ts`, `io.ts`).
- `<userData>/conversations/<id>.jsonl` stores versioned events synchronously before publication.
- `index.json` is **v3** with an `owner` (legacy-external or doan) and no `state`: a canvas conversation is listed or deleted, never archived. The reader ignores unknown keys, a `state` key in a v3 entry included, so optional keys need no version change; an unknown `permissionMode` value reads as Ask first for that entry only, and an unknown `claudeRunMode` value drops that key only. v1 (read as all legacy-external) and v2 are migrated in memory on read (`parseIndex` in `store.ts`): `state` is dropped, and an archived entry loses `seenSeq`, so its whole history counts as seen and the interruption written when it was archived does not light up as an unseen end; a v1 or v2 entry with an unknown or missing `state` makes the index invalid. Nothing is rewritten at startup, because a throw while constructing `ConversationStore` ends the app; the first save writes v3. Earlier builds refuse v3 as an unknown version, so a rolled-back app cannot archive the conversations it finds or stop their turns. Event JSONL v1 and sessions.v1 do not follow the index version.
- A history read fails as a whole when one event fails its check (`store.ts` `parse`), so an older Doan cannot read a history holding an event type or code added after it. `turn.failed` code `model_unavailable` (`types.ts` `FailureCode`) always carries the `model` the provider's error named, and no other code carries `model` (`store.ts` `payload`); an older Doan cannot read a conversation that holds one. Only the Codex transport writes it, for a `TurnError` with `codexErrorInfo: "modelNotFound"` whose message names the model as `'<id>' model`; the ID comes from that message because Doan never sent it (`transport.ts` `codexFailed`), and without one the turn fails as other errors do. An older Doan cannot read a history that holds a `canvas.drawn` event ('Canvas cards') either. `turn.failed` code `prompt_unread` (a Claude turn whose prompt the CLI never read, 'Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'Claude turn prompt') is just as unknown to builds before it: after a downgrade on the same device such a conversation shows as a read failure until Doan is upgraded again, and its history is never changed.
- Every index change that adds or binds a conversation (a start, a bind, a promotion) goes through the undo journal `start-undo.json` (`JournalChange`), written before any log changes and committed by renaming the new index. Records stay `v: 1`, and this build writes `replaced: []`. A `restore` record, a non-empty `replaced` or a start that writes to other histories is an earlier build's archiving change: recovery settles it with its old meaning only beside a v1 or v2 index and refuses it as damaged beside v3, which an earlier build never writes. Rollback limit, accepted: an earlier build that opens a v3 index with a journal left over cannot read the index during recovery and stops at startup. Rollback truncates each touched history to its recorded prefix instead of deleting it; only an uncommitted start's own log is removed. Recovery validates the whole journal and prefixes before truncating anything, and failed recovery blocks reads/writes rather than resetting to empty history (`storeRecovery.test.ts`).
- `drafts.json` (`drafts.ts`; TypeScript-only, so `drafts.test.ts` is its schema) is rewritten through a temporary file and rename on every change, so a crash keeps the queue; at start every queued item becomes a draft and nothing is sent. A direct send's or a start's message is held in `sending` from when main takes it until acceptance or a refusal outside a quit (a quit keeps it, since no composer outlives it; a quit that aborts leaves the service quitting with the composers open, so from then on each composer, a start's root and canvas or a send's conversation, holds one message, its last); at start a held send joins its conversation's returned draft ahead of the queue, and a held start moves to `starts`, which a new conversation composer of its root or canvas takes once (`takeStartDraft`). A Move to Trash discards the trashed canvas's starts (`discardCanvasStarts`, matched as a composer would take them, by path alone when another listed canvas shares the id) and removing roots (`removeRoots` in `index.ts`) discards theirs (`discardRootStarts`); neither falls back to another composer. Both keys are written only when non-empty, so older builds read the file unchanged. Reader and writer share the same bounds, so what main accepted is always read back whole (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27 'Successful appends crossed the history reader's byte limit').
- **Deletion** (`ConversationStore.delete`, `ConversationService.delete`). The service refuses (`busy`) while the app quits or the conversation is `working` (the list's rule), then closes its run (`closeRun`): it marks the live entry `terminal`, because `append` opens with `O_CREAT` and a late event would recreate the deleted history, and keeps the live entry so the exit callback still releases the session reservation (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-28). The store recovers any journal, records the id in `conversations/deletions.json` (temporary file and rename; an unreadable record refuses every delete), saves the index without the entry (the commit point), then unlinks `<id>.jsonl`; the service then discards its drafts and attachment ids and closes its held notification. The index goes first because a history missing under an indexed entry would become a permanent read-failure row. A failed index save drops the id from the record again. After the commit, cleanup failures are logged and the deletion still succeeds.
  - At start, after recovery, `finishDeletions` removes the history and drafts of recorded ids the readable index no longer lists and drops ids it still lists (a deletion that never committed). With a missing, unreadable or newer index it keeps the record and removes nothing. Unindexed histories that no deletion recorded are never removed: an index lost outside Doan, followed by one new start, would otherwise make every older history look orphaned.
  - A conversation whose persistence failed stays `working` only until its process group is gone (`releaseFailed` in `service.ts`): then its queue comes back as the draft, it can be deleted, and the next send starts a new turn on a new live entry (refused like any first write while the history still takes none).
- History read and append share a **256 MiB** limit (`CONVERSATION_HISTORY_BYTES`), checked before writing. Each append rereads the full history, so cost can be quadratic; there is no rotation, retention or pagination.
- File opens reject observed links, nonregular files and identity changes, but do not protect atomically against hostile ancestor replacement. Recovery is not multi-file atomicity, power-loss durability, multiple-writer safety or proof that a forged valid prefix is authentic.
- These files are not portable history. The real-state test guard watches `sessions.jsonl` and the conversations directory.

### Agent authentication

Entry: `app/src/main/agentAuth/`; per-provider commands, profile variables, credential stores, ACP runners and session directories are in `providers.ts` (its comments give each switch's reason), minimum versions in `conversation/minimumVersions.ts`. Product scope and UI: PRODUCT §3 In scope 7, §5 item 0. Doan keeps its own connection per provider, at most one each, independent of Orca, a terminal or the default CLI profile; it never reads, copies or changes those external credentials and never falls back to them. The provider list is fixed (`PROVIDERS`). Doan uses each provider's official login and ships no OAuth client (sole exception: 'Antigravity identity exception' below).

**Providers and runtimes.**
- Claude Code and Codex keep the credential in a profile-keyed OS keychain item; a plaintext fallback file fails the sign-in (`storage_failed`) and is deleted. Cursor, Antigravity and Copilot are switched to file storage in a dedicated HOME, and that file lives only in the vault. Codex runs with `allow_login_shell=false` so the agent's tool shell keeps the bundled doan first on PATH.
- **Versions:** at or above the minimum, no maximum; `version_unsupported` and `version_unknown` refuse runs. A minimum rises only by decision, backed by a real-app check at the new version (sign-in kept, new conversation, resume, one permission request). A minimum is lowered only to a version that passes the whole surface check in the real app: sign-in kept in both directions across the installed and the lower CLI, a new sign-in that stores only in the profile's own credential location and survives a cancelled replacement, a new conversation on a chosen model, resume after a restart and on the newer CLI, a permission allowed and one denied, every mapped permission mode, Send now and Stop, MCP connectors, the silent-failure checks (one rollout per opened Codex server, the bundled `doan` first on the tool PATH, the `doan` skill found, the Claude organization pin refusing an `apiKeyHelper` repo), and for Claude Code and Codex a terminal session continued, also on the newer CLI, and refused while a terminal holds it. Claude Code also needs every release between the new and the old minimum to start under the bundled SDK, because a Claude CLI the SDK cannot drive shows only failed turns, never Update. A feature that makes Doan depend on a new provider surface either checks it at the current minimum or raises the minimum by decision: there is no per-version feature gating, so an unchecked surface can fail silently on an older accepted CLI. The one per-version choice is the recovery command, not a conversation surface: Update offers `<cli> update` only from the first version that has that subcommand (the floor in `app/src/main/installCommand.ts`), because an older CLI takes `update` as a prompt and may start a conversation; below the floor, or for a version the floor cannot place, Update opens the install page. The Antigravity server has no minimum: Doan downloads it pinned (archive and members by SHA-256) and accepts only its `initialize` `agentInfo.version`, so a person never installs or updates it. Doan installs no provider CLI except the Antigravity server; Install and Update only open a Terminal window whose command the person runs ('Install and Update commands' below).
- **Runtime compatibility:** only a Doan-connection handshake that opens no stored session (a new conversation, or a model or skill list) and whose shape Doan cannot read records the installed version as incompatible (`conversation/handshake.ts`); a malformed successful reply counts, and among failed replies only a JSON-RPC error to Codex's `initialize` that is not a login, rate-limit or session-in-use answer (`NOT_EVIDENCE` in `conversation/runners.ts`); never a timeout, lost transport, resume, Claude or Antigravity. The record is published (`agent-auth:changed`) before the refusal returns, lives in AuthIpc memory per installed version, never blocks a run, and clears on another version, a passing handshake or restart. The installed version never reaches the Status DTO.
- **Install and Update commands** (`app/src/main/installCommand.ts`, IPC `setup:open-install`): the renderer sends only the provider, one of the four CLI providers (`ExternalTarget`; the renderer answers Antigravity itself as `failed`), and main picks the path from its fixed table and its own tool facts. CLI missing → the install command; below the minimum and at or above the update floor → the update command; anything else (facts not read yet, Copilot, a version the floor cannot place) → the install page. The result is `terminal`, `page`, `failed` (Terminal could not open) or `pageFailed`.
  - GitHub Copilot has no update command: `copilot update` stores its new package under the real HOME, which Doan's Copilot (run with `HOME=<profileDir>`) never selects, while tool facts read `--version` with the real HOME; a failed binary replacement would report the new version while conversations run the old one.
  - Main writes `<userData>/terminal/<provider>-<random>.command` (0700, a new name each open, never overwritten) and opens it with `shell.openPath`, whose non-empty string is a failure. Terminal runs it in the person's login shell, not Doan's launch environment (PRODUCT §3 'Launch environment').
  - The script waits for Return, then runs the command under a guarded `set -o pipefail` (`curl … | bash` otherwise ends 0 after a failed download) and prints a success line or a failure line with the install page.
  - The script removes itself as its first command, because `openPath` resolves before Terminal's shell has read the file: an open never deletes another script. Only the first open of an app session clears leftovers, which can still take a script a window from the previous session had not read yet.
  - A check while the cause is `cli_missing` or `version_*` re-reads tool facts only and skips the account check (`agentAuth/ipc.ts` `check`), so those checks spend no Copilot request.
- **Antigravity server** (`antigravity.ts`, an approved exception): a pinned archive, SHA-256-verified on download into `<userData>/agent-runtimes/antigravity-acp/<version>/` and once per process after; a mismatch is `cli_install_failed`. Google publishes neither URL nor hashes (taken from Orca 1.4.240); `GEMINI_HOME` and `AGY_ACP_FORCE_FILE_STORAGE` are undocumented; only darwin-arm64 is pinned.
- **Profiles:** `<userData>/agent-auth/profiles/<profile>/` (0700), named by the provider or `<provider>-<id>` for a staged replacement. Every process, check, lease, sign-out and recovery resolves the profile from the stored connection, never from the provider name. Each profile gets its own doan `SKILL.md` stub; the user's global skill and PATH setup are not copied. Antigravity's stub is `<GEMINI_HOME>/config/skills/doan/SKILL.md`: its ACP server (1.3.0) reads skills from `<GEMINI_HOME>/config/skills` and not `<GEMINI_HOME>/skills` (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-07). Doan writes nothing else into a profile itself, except Copilot's storage setting (`.copilot/settings.json`, `settings` in `providers.ts`) and two more. A Codex replacement appends `[mcp_servers.doan-*]` tables to its staging profile's `config.toml` ('MCP connectors' below); the staging directory is new, so it holds none of the person's settings. And the Claude Code and Codex profiles get the terminal agent setup's `doan` allowances ('Agent setup' above), written before every provider process (`settings` in `providers.ts`), so an app conversation's `doan work update` does not ask on every frame in Ask first: Codex's `rules/doan.rules` is `codexRuleText()`, a file only Doan writes, replaced whole, so the profile allows the same read and signal commands and every other `doan` command follows the turn's mode like any shell command: Full access runs `doan export` and `doan config` without asking, where a `prompt` rule would refuse them (`approvalPolicy: never`), and the other modes ask before such a command leaves their sandbox (Plan refuses); Claude's `settings.json` gets only the missing `CLAUDE_ALLOW_RULES` ('Profile allowances' below). Cursor, Antigravity and Copilot profiles get none, so they may still ask. The Codex profile alone also gets `rules/doan-handoff.rules` (`prefix_rule(pattern=["doan", "handoff"], decision="prompt")`, also replaced whole): Codex applies the strictest matching rule (`forbidden` > `prompt` > `allow`, Codex docs 'Rules'), so `doan handoff` asks; the terminal rule leaves `doan handoff` out, since a terminal agent's `doan handoff` is refused anyway. The command a person approves under that `prompt` runs inside Codex's sandbox, whose Doan permission profile allows the app socket ('Fence' below), so it reaches the app with that one approval (Claude's `CLAUDE_ALLOW_RULES` lack `doan handoff`, so Claude's Ask first asks once too).
- **Profile allowances** (`writeSetting` and `mergeSetting` in `storage.ts`): Claude Code writes the profile's `settings.json` too, so Doan never replaces it. `mergeClaudeAllow` decides; a file it refuses (non-UTF-8, invalid JSON, wrong-shaped `permissions`/`allow`), a link or another non-file stays as it is, with no notice, since no screen shows profile files. A missing file is created exclusively with `link(2)`. An existing one is published through Agent setup's `swapFile` (move aside, check the moved file's `dev:ino` and bytes against what Doan read, link the merge), so a save Claude made after Doan's read is never overwritten: that file goes back and the next launch merges into it. Because `swapFile` moves the file aside and restores it with `link(2)`, a hard-link probe of the staged file runs first; on a filesystem without hard links the launch fails `write_failed` and the original stays where it is (accepted: userData is on APFS). The staged, probe and moved-aside names are in `agent-auth/`, on the profile's volume, not in the profile folder: a fenced Claude turn can write parts of the profile folder, and a file it renamed onto one of those names would be published or restored as `settings.json`. The moved-aside file is read without blocking and with a cap (`regularFile.ts`).
- **Child environment** (`environment.ts`): an allowlist (basic, locale, certificate, proxy) read through `launchEnv`, plus the login shell's PATH, `DOAN_RUNTIME` naming this app's `conversation-runtime.json` (§4 'App-socket credentials'), `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION=1` and the profile variable (Antigravity also gets `GEMINI_HOME=<profile>/.gemini`); an extra naming a `RESERVED` variable refuses the launch. Inherited API keys, other providers' homes and external session IDs never pass; dedicated-HOME providers get back only `SSH_AUTH_SOCK` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=<real HOME>/.gitconfig`, never `gh` or other tokens.
