- **Fence** (`fence.ts`; built in `conversation/prepare.ts` `runFence`, applied in `acpRunner.ts`, `runners.ts` and `service.ts`; `profileFencedCommand` for processes outside a conversation): a Doan-connection conversation run (`use === "run"`), every model and skill list read but Codex's, the Claude usage read, every Claude MCP connector command, a Claude sign-out's MCP logout step and every Copilot and Cursor process carry the fence, which keeps the agent's commands and file tools, and the hooks, MCP servers, plugins and helpers the CLI starts, out of the rest of Doan's userData in every permission mode, Full access included: the other profiles (a staging `codex-<id>` included) with their plaintext working copies, `conversations/`, the vault and metadata, other conversations' pasted images and every other userData file. A blocked access is the tool's own failure; there is no Doan screen or code for it. A Claude process is fenced whenever it loads settings an agent can write, whether or not it runs a model tool call: the CLI starts the hooks and MCP servers those settings name itself, beside the model (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-15, 2026-11-16). An ACP list read opens a session as a run does, and Copilot's and Cursor's start the MCP servers their profile names. Every Copilot and Cursor process (`fencedEverywhere`: sign-in, status, Copilot's usage read, the version check before a run) loads code an agent can write: Copilot imports its own JavaScript from the profile's package cache, the newest version there and with no signature check, and its prompt sessions start the profile's hooks and MCP servers (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-16 'Every Copilot process ran JavaScript…'); Cursor's launcher starts Node with `NODE_COMPILE_CACHE` under the profile, and Node runs a cache entry whose header matches the source's length and checksum whatever code it holds (probe: a forged entry ran in `--version`). Not fenced: Claude's sign-in, status and the provider's own sign-out; Claude's and Codex's version check before a run; Antigravity's sign-in and status; Codex's sign-in, status, sign-out, model and skill lists, usage read, MCP connector commands and identity lookup; legacy external runs; and the processes Doan starts outside any profile: the login-shell probe (`$SHELL -lic`, which runs the person's shell startup files), the located provider binaries' `--version` runs for tool facts (with the person's HOME) and the clone and `--git-init` git commands, which use the person's git config. A send to a live Codex server builds no new fence; the server keeps the one it started with.
  - Reopened paths (`fencePolicy`): the run's own profile (read; writes only as below), `conversation-runtime.json` (read; `runtime.json` stays closed), this turn's pasted-image directories (read), the `BROWSER` hook (read; Claude, Antigravity) and, for Antigravity, its pinned server (read). A Codex run opens the whole `attachments/` directory instead, because one app-server serves many turns and its limits are fixed when it starts.
  - Writes in the agent's own folder (`AGENT_WRITES` in `fence.ts`; the folder is the profile for Claude and Codex, and `.copilot`, `.cursor` or `.gemini` in the dedicated HOME of the others): closed, except what the CLI writes during a run, its sign-in and its status, an allowlist per provider from isolated probes (Claude Code 2.1.327 for conversation state and 2.1.329 for MCP sign-in and account and MCP token refresh, codex-cli 0.169.0, Copilot 1.0.105, Cursor 2026.10.22) and, for Antigravity's server 1.3.0, which runs only signed in, a signed-in profile's entries and their times. A later process reads the rest of the folder as its instructions, settings, rules, skills, subagents, plugins, hooks, MCP servers and permissions, some outside the outer fence (Codex outside Full access, Claude's sign-in and status commands; POSTMORTEM 2026-11-09, 2026-11-19), and a store a new CLI version adds stays closed until a probe opens it. What stays writable: Claude's transcripts, sessions, shell snapshots, history, caches, the `.storage-write.lock` it takes before writing credentials to secure storage, its root-level refresh locks (`.oauth_refresh.lock`, its `.owner` record and `.owner.tmp.` writes for account OAuth, and `mcp-refresh-doan_<slug>_<hash>.lock.lock` for proactive MCP refresh, with hyphens in the connector slug replaced by underscores and a 16-digit lowercase hex hash), and `.claude.json` (the CLI writes it on every run; every Claude process that starts the MCP servers it names, `mcpServers` and `projects[*].mcpServers`, runs inside the fence); Codex's rollouts, databases, caches and `tmp/` (the Codex process writes them, and runs inside this fence in Full access only); Copilot's session state and store, logs, plugin data, `config.json` (its login) and `settings.json` (Doan rewrites it before every process); Cursor's ACP sessions, state, caches and `auth.json` (its login); Antigravity's conversations, brain, token and its per-conversation databases in `.gemini/config/`. Closed among others: Claude's `settings.json`, `CLAUDE.md`, `rules/`, `output-styles/`, `session-env/` (the environment files hooks leave for a session, which its commands source again on a resume; Claude Code 2.1.327 probe), skills, subagents, commands, plugins and the profile's `.claude/` and `.mcp.json` (the project settings of the Claude processes Doan starts with the profile as their folder); Codex's `config.toml`, `hooks.json`, `.env`, `rules/`, `AGENTS.md`, `AGENTS.override.md`, `prompts/`, skills and plugins; Cursor's `cli-config.json` (its permission allow list); Antigravity's `.gemini/config/` memory, skills, workflows and MCP and hook files, and its server's `settings.json` and trusted workspaces. Every Codex process loads `.env` into its own environment (codex-cli 0.169.0, probe: a planted `PATH` made the CLI's own `git` calls run a decoy outside any sandbox); `AuthManager` removes one before any Codex process starts, so a file planted before it was closed loads nowhere. In a dedicated HOME, other agents' folders that these CLIs read as the person's own are closed too (`HOME_STORES`: `.agents`, `.claude`, `.codex`, `.grok`; Copilot 1.0.105 loads personal skills from `~/.agents/skills`, Cursor 2026.10.22 skills from all four and hooks, permissions and commands from `.claude`), each as a whole folder; the rest stays writable, since it is the HOME the agent's own commands use (npm, pip and build caches). Doan writes the doan stub, the Codex rules and Claude's and Copilot's settings from outside the fence; the stub stays readable.
  - Codex permission profiles: the profile is read-only to the agent's commands. Codex writes its rollouts, databases, caches and its runtime helpers (`tmp/`) from its own process, outside its command sandbox (codex-cli 0.169.0 probe: a turn's commands, `apply_patch` and an approved escalation included, ran and the rollout was recorded). The settings and instruction files above are denied, so they are unreadable to sandboxed commands and closed to approved escalated ones.
  - Claude auto memory is off in every fenced run (`autoMemoryEnabled: false` at the flag tier, `claudeRunSettings` in `runners.ts`): its files sit under `projects/`, which the CLI writes its transcripts to, so a write rule could not keep them, and a memory one turn wrote would reach every later turn in the profile (Claude Code 2.1.327 probe: with it off, a memory file in the profile was not loaded).
  - Mechanism: macOS refuses a second Seatbelt policy inside one (exit 71), so Codex outside Full access, which sandboxes its own commands, gets Doan's limits as its own settings instead of the outer fence.
    - Outer fence: `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec -p <policy> -D NAME=<path>… <command> <args>` for the Cursor, Antigravity and Copilot servers in every mode and every list read, every other Copilot and Cursor process, every fenced Claude process and the Codex app-server in Full access. The policy text is in `fence.ts`; paths go only in `-D` values and must be real paths, because an unresolved `/var/...` or relative value matches nothing and blocks nothing. `sandbox-exec` keeps the provider's pid, process group and start time, so Stop and quit cleanup are unchanged.
    - Claude: every Doan-connection turn and list read runs in the outer fence with `sandbox.enabled: false` and auto memory off at the flag tier (`claudeRunSettings` in `runners.ts`), whatever a user, project or local source says, since Claude's own sandbox cannot start inside it; the usage read passes the same setting. Doan does not decide by what the repository or the profile may start: detecting it missed shape after shape (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-16). Hooks, MCP servers, plugins, credential helpers and Git filters the CLI starts all run inside the fence, and so would a skill the agent writes and uses during the turn. Claude's own sandbox does not narrow a Doan turn, so in Ask first a Bash command it would have run unasked asks.
    - Processes outside a conversation (`profileFencedCommand`): the Claude and Copilot usage reads run from the profile, since the fence closes the rest of userData; the MCP connector commands and a sign-out's MCP logout step (`AuthManager` `mcpStep`) run from the profile as before. Through the SDK the usage read started the MCP servers of a `.mcp.json` in a folder above userData (`~/Library/Application Support`, `~/Library`, `~`), which any outer-fenced agent can write, and `claude mcp list`/`get` start those the profile's `.claude.json` names (probe).
    - Codex outside Full access: the app-server starts with `-c` arguments (`codexProfileArgs`) that define Doan's permission profiles `doan` (extends `:read-only`) and `doan-workspace` (extends `:workspace`) and set `default_permissions="doan"`. Both turn network on with only the app socket's directory allowed, because Codex opens no unix socket without network.
    - A Codex send whose mode needs the other mechanism (Full access against the other three) closes the idle server and opens a new one that resumes the thread with `thread/resume` (`codexRestart`, `ConversationService.switchCodexServer`).
  - Deny by name: in Codex's permission profiles a deny beats every allow under it, and Codex applies only a profile's deny entries to an approved escalated command, so a deny on userData would close the profile and `runtime.json` too (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-09). The profiles deny the entries off the way to an allowed path instead, listed when the Codex server starts (`fenceDenied`); a name a rule could read as a pattern refuses the run.
  - Fail closed: a run whose fence cannot be built does not start and fails with an existing code. Before spawning it is `unknown`: a path that does not resolve, is not absolute or carries a control character, a profile not directly under `agent-auth/profiles/`, a listing that cannot be read, a Codex profile server without the app's socket. A `sandbox-exec` that is missing or exits at once (65 for a policy error, 71) is `process_exited`. It is never `cli_missing`. A Claude or Copilot usage read whose fence cannot be built reads as `read_failed`, an MCP connector command as a failed command, a Copilot or Cursor sign-in or check as a failed one, and a Copilot or Cursor version check before a run refuses the run as `unknown`; none starts a process.

**Storage** (`storage.ts`, `metadata.ts`, `cipher.ts`).
- Metadata stores no token, code, PKCE verifier or credential path. Unreadable or unknown-version metadata is never read as empty, since a later save would erase the other connections: channels refuse (`read` answers `null`) and runs refuse with `not_logged_in`.
- Vault: sealed with Electron `safeStorage` (Keychain item `doan Safe Storage`, shared by dev and packaged builds), replaced atomically (exclusive 0600 temp, fsync, rename). Without encryption nothing is persisted in plaintext.
- Commit ordering: the working copy is scrubbed before the commit; vault image and metadata are stored together after the last cancellation point, and a failed metadata write restores the previous image.
- Pending cleanup (`cleanup.json`): a staged profile is recorded before its login starts, a replaced one before the metadata write; damage is never read as empty. A startup sweep logs out recorded profiles in the write slot, skips one a stored connection references, and removes a profile only after its logout exits 0.
- Working copies: written into the profile before a vault provider's process starts; the last process holding the profile writes a changed copy back and removes the plaintext (failure → unverified `storage_failed`). A lone temporary sibling at startup is an interrupted write, not a sign-out. Links out of the profile are refused.

**State, attempts and readiness** (`state.ts`, `manager.ts`; `state.test.ts`, `manager.test.ts`).
- One sign-in or sign-out at a time across providers (`busy`); a cancelled or replaced attempt's late result is `stale`. Only the starting window can cancel, open the link or submit a callback (`not_owner`); closing it cancels. An attempt reports only after its process group is gone.
- Stored metadata loads as unverified and the first run checks it, except Copilot, whose status check costs a model request: its first completed run verifies it (`verifyByRun`).
- Codex and Antigravity have no stable account ID and show only an email from a lookup (`identity.ts`); email is shown, never compared. The lookup runs in sign-in before the commit, and in a check without writing metadata (a check runs outside the write slot); it never changes an outcome. Antigravity sends its lookup only after the vault hold is dropped, because quit force-releases a hold and a late drop could absorb an already removed working copy as empty.
- Readiness is a pure function of state and tool facts (`readiness()`); there is no polling. `account_mismatch` is not retryable. Connection IDs, profiles, account IDs and diagnostics never reach a window.

**Conversations on a Doan connection** (`conversation/prepare.ts`).
- The owner `{kind: "doan", provider, connectionId, accountId, workspaceId, profile}` is stored in the index (since v2). Resume needs a connected, verified, unexpired connection on the same profile with no sign-in or failed sign-out. With a stable account ID the account and workspace must match; without one only the same connection ID resumes. Email never establishes identity.
- Every await before acceptance rechecks the connection generation and ID. A run leases the profile until its group is gone and its write-back landed.
- A Claude run is pinned to its login's organization (`managedSettings.forceLoginOrgUUID`), so a repository setting that supplies its own API key makes Claude refuse (`credential_override`) instead of replacing the Doan login. A Claude connection with no organization reads as `reauth_required` (`unpinned` in `state.ts`), and its runs refuse with `not_logged_in`.
- ACP permission requests forward the provider's own options and are answered as cancelled outside a running turn; `session/load` is the final admission.

**Agent usage** (`usageService.ts`, `usageRead.ts`, `usageParse.ts`, `usageSchedule.ts`, `usageStore.ts`; PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Agent usage'). Account usage for Claude Code, Codex, Cursor and Copilot; Antigravity is never read (its ACP server has no usage read, and `agy` needs its own login).
- **Fresh reads** (start, sign-in, popover open, Refresh usage, window shown again) run in a process opened only for the read, under a lease with use `"usage"`, and resolve only after its group is gone. Each provider's request is in `readUsage` (`usageRead.ts`; Claude through the SDK with no prompt, Copilot through its headless mode, since its ACP mode has no quota read); Cursor's is one GET ('Cursor usage token exception' below). Claude and Copilot run in their own profile inside the outer fence ('Fence' above), never a repo, whose settings (an `apiKeyHelper`) could replace the login; a fresh Claude read is pinned to the login's organization as a run is, so such a device setting is refused rather than read as an API key's "no usage limits".
- **Turn-end reads** reuse the run instead of a process: a Doan-connection Claude turn asks its open `Query` before closing its input, and a Codex turn its conversation's app-server. Legacy external and ACP runs read nothing at turn end. A Doan-connection Cursor run reads once when its `session/load` answers "not found" (`Prepared.limitReached`, set by `withTurnUsage`): through `UsageService.turnEnd` with `limit`, from the run's own profile (`readCursorSummary`), and the open refuses `rate_limited` when a window is at 100%, else `session_unavailable`. A Cursor `rate_limited` send refusal comes only from here, so the renderer words it as a conversation that cannot continue (`dockedRefusal`). Cursor saves no session for a first turn its plan limit stopped and its ACP answer marks no limit, so usage is the only sign. The credential copy goes with the run's lease once the stopped group is gone, so the read takes it synchronously before its first await. The Claude read is capped at `TURN_USAGE_MS` (`conversation/runners.ts`): it keeps the CLI's input open, and the cap plus the CLI's exit after closing must stay inside `EXIT_WAIT_MS` (`conversation/service.ts`), or a CLI that never answers would make the next send refuse as busy. The exit stays inside it because a next send ends the finished turn's process ('Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'Ending a finished turn's process'), not because the CLI always exits on its own. A turn whose prompt the CLI never read reads nothing unless it ended `rate_limited`, whose usage read supplies the reset for the usage-limit schedule ('Agent conversation runtime and IPC' 'Usage-limit schedule').
- **Leases.** A `"usage"` lease never counts as a conversation, so sign-out and replacement never answer `stop_required` for it. `stopRuns` in `main/index.ts` also stops the provider's usage read and waits for its lease; quit stops reads before agent sign-in quits. A read first waits for a running check, and checks an unchecked connection as a run's preparation does (Copilot is admitted by `verifyByRun`); a connection readiness refuses is not read.
- **Timeouts** count from the provider's `initialize` answer (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-01), with a separate cap for an `initialize` that never comes (`USAGE_OPEN_MS`, `USAGE_READ_MS` in `usageRead.ts`).
- **`not_supported`** comes from the answer, never the version (no per-version feature gating): JSON-RPC `-32601`/Method not found, or Claude's unsupported control request subtype (`usageNotSupported`). A response of an unknown shape is `read_failed`, never a guess.
- **Store.** Main memory only, keyed by provider and `connectionId`; a new `connectionId` drops the value, and a read that ends under another `connectionId` is dropped. Schedule: `shouldRead` (`usageSchedule.ts`); main decides visibility, which holds while the main window is shown and the app is not hidden, or while the prototype window is shown (`usageVisible` in `usageWindows.ts`); the prototype window's show, hide and close re-evaluate it. Failures log only Doan's own words, never a reply body, provider message or credential.
- **IPC.** `agent-usage:read` (the main window and the prototype window's main frame, `prototypeComposer` in `senderAllowed.ts`; `usageIpc.ts`) and `agent-usage:changed`, sent to both windows, carry `UsageEntry[]` (`usageTypes.ts`), just what the Settings > Agents row is worded from. No connection ID, profile, account, token or diagnostic crosses.

**Sign-out and replacement.**
- A cancelled or failed replacement leaves the stored connection and its verified state as they were, unless the live login was touched (a keychain provider's login exited 0 in the live profile, or a vault provider's commit failed and the previous vault image could not be restored).
- Codex replacements are staged in `profiles/codex-<id>/` because `codex login` revokes and deletes the profile's existing login before its login server starts; the old profile is logged out only after success.
- With conversation runs active (an idle Codex app-server counts; model list and usage reads and a Claude run left with only a done turn's background work, whose lease is quiet, do not), sign-out and replacement return `stop_required` unless sent with `stop: true`, which stops those runs and waits for their leases.
- Sign-out succeeds only when a fresh status process reports the profile signed out; a failure persists in `signOutFailed` and blocks runs and sign-ins across restarts. Sign-out is local; history stays, and another account never runs the earlier conversations: a send to such a conversation refuses with `workspace_changed` when only the Claude Code organization differs (`workspace_mismatch` with both organization ids known, `organizationsKnown`), with `account_changed` for another account or another workspace otherwise (`account_mismatch`, `workspace_mismatch` with an organization id missing, and GitHub Copilot's `host`), which signing in to that account and workspace again brings back, and with `sign_in_changed` for a sign-in that cannot be proven the same or another Doan profile (`account_unverifiable`, `profile_mismatch`, or a stored session in a profile a staged replacement left; `judgmentCode` and the `configDir` check in `prepare.ts`).

**Browser links.** A link reaches only the owning window; `open-link` opens main's latest link for the attempt, and a window can never name a URL.
- Every Claude and Antigravity process, runs included, gets `BROWSER=<userData>/agent-auth/browser-hook`, which prints the URL instead of opening a browser; without a usable hook the CLI would open the default browser, so nothing starts. The agent's tool shell inherits it, so a command it runs to open a browser opens nothing.
- Claude: main forwards only the hook's authorize URL (`response_type=code`, localhost `/callback`), never the printed manual-code URL, which Doan has no entry for. A Claude `browser` setting overrides `BROWSER` and makes the sign-in fail.
- Antigravity: only a Google auth link returning to `127.0.0.1`; `submit-code` delivers only an address for the same listener and state. Logs keep Doan's own categories only, because the server's messages carry the link, the account and credential fields. Status and logout limits start at the `initialize` answer: a slow server start would otherwise fail sign-ins the user already approved.
- `codex login`, `cursor-agent login` and `copilot login` open the system browser themselves; Doan only offers Open again (accepted; BACKLOG.md).

**IPC.** `agent-auth:*` (types in `agentAuth/ipc.ts`), main window's main frame only (`senderAllowed.ts`); Settings > Agents never touches conversations (§7 'Settings IPC'). `changed` carries the whole snapshot with a revision and `onAgentAuth` drops older revisions, so a racing initial read never replaces newer state. Main-only `AuthIpc.check(provider)` also returns the outcome (`checkVerdict`); the window's reply does not.

**GitHub token exception.** Copilot is the only place Doan holds a GitHub token (exception to §9): the official Copilot login's token, whose broad scopes (`read:user read:org repo gist codespace write:plugin_gateway_connections`) Doan cannot narrow. It stays encrypted in the vault, is materialized only into the Copilot HOME for Copilot processes, and is never used for repository access.

**Antigravity identity exception.** Only to show the email, Doan reads the client ID, secret and refresh token from its own working copy and makes one refresh grant to `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token` and one userinfo `GET`. The file's `token_uri` must equal that address and its `scopes` include `userinfo.email`, or nothing is sent; redirects are refused. The access token stays in memory and never reaches state, metadata, logs or a window; Doan never writes the credential file. Widening this needs its own decision.

**Cursor usage token exception.** Only to read usage, Doan reads the access token from the Cursor lease's working copy (a regular file, no link, size-capped) and sends it as the dashboard session cookie in one `GET https://cursor.com/api/usage-summary` (`usageRead.ts`). The host is fixed, redirects and answers from another origin are refused, and the answer is capped in size and time. The token stays in memory for that call; it is never refreshed (an expired one is `read_failed`) and never reaches state, metadata, logs, IPC, error text or a window. The API is private, so any other shape or failure is `read_failed`. Widening this needs its own decision.

**Known limits.**
- Real token expiry, refresh and concurrent refresh have not been observed for any provider.
- Cursor's login shell puts system paths first on PATH, so its tool shell may not run the bundled `doan` first.
- Accepted risks of the minimum-version policy: changed meaning under unchanged shapes is not caught; text-based classifications can fall back to generic codes; Claude incompatibility is never recorded; an incompatible provider looks ready until its first run. Cursor and Copilot self-update; Copilot ACP is a preview.
- Tool-fact probes run provider CLIs under the real HOME and write under `~/.codex` and `~/.local/share/cursor-agent`.
- Cursor does not ask before editing inside the workspace. Several Codex app-servers on one `CODEX_HOME` (a conversation, the check and the lookup, a usage read) may refresh the same keychain token.
- A SIGKILL during a staged Codex replacement leaves its login process running; the sweep removes only the profile. A successful sign-out leaves a staged profile directory, minus its credential.
- Claude Code's and Codex's keychain items are keyed by the profile path and stay after userData is deleted; signing out first removes them. `doan Safe Storage` (the vault key) and MCP connector tokens are keychain items too; whether they stay was not checked. A sign-out that answered `stop_required` and was never confirmed is cancelled by quit: the connection stays and shows as connected on the next launch, so the person can sign out again.
- Antigravity: in the runs checked (sign-in, status, one turn, sign-out), Doan's server and its language server wrote neither the real `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` nor the `gemini | antigravity` keychain item with the environment above. Writes seen there come from the person's own `agy`, which other apps (Orca) run on a schedule. The language server's uses of `go-keyring` and `os/user.Current` were not ruled out statically.
- Fence ('Fence' above), all providers:
  - Other processes of the same user (a terminal agent, Orca) can read userData: file permissions do not separate one uid.
  - It holds only direct access inside the run. Settings the agent leaves elsewhere (`~/.zshrc`, configuration of another provider, a terminal agent's settings) can run later outside it, in a process Doan does not start or does not fence ('Fence' above lists them).
  - `doan check` inside it cannot add a session association to `<userData>/sessions.jsonl`; its output and exit code are unchanged.
  - `sandbox-exec` is marked deprecated in its man page; if macOS removes it, outer-fenced runs fail to start.
- Fence, Antigravity: its sign-in and status run outside the fence with the profile an agent in it can write. Its status check started none of the hooks and MCP servers planted in the profile (`.gemini/config/hooks.json`, `.gemini/config/mcp_config.json`, `.gemini/settings.json`), and the server loaded no `.pth` or `usercustomize.py` from the profile's user site-packages (`.local/lib/python3.11/site-packages`, `Library/Python/3.11/lib/python/site-packages`; probe); its sign-in was not probed.
- Fence, Claude and Codex: their version check before a run runs outside the fence with the lease's environment (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` / `CODEX_HOME` = the profile). Codex's profile settings are closed to its turns; whether Claude's `--version` loads anything from the profile was not probed.
- Fence, Claude:
  - Doan does not read MDM or server-delivered managed settings, and managed settings beat the flag tier: if they turn the sandbox on, every Bash command of a Doan turn fails with exit 71 inside the outer fence.
  - The outer fence holds files only; it is `(allow default)` outside userData, so a hook, MCP server or helper the repository names still runs with the person's network and files outside userData, as the agent's own commands do in Full access.
  - A pasted image sent with Send now during a fenced turn was not opened for that turn, so the message waits in the queue for the next one.
- Fence, Codex:
  - Sandboxed commands in Ask first, Plan and Accept edits reach the network without approval, and those modes do not start while the app has no runtime (no socket).
  - The denied entries are listed when the server starts, so a userData entry made later (another provider signed in meanwhile, a new staging profile) is open until that server ends. Sandboxed and approved escalated commands can list userData's entry names; a denied entry's content stays closed.
  - An approved escalated command can write userData outside the denied entries (`conversation-runtime.json`, attachments, new entries) and the profile outside its denied files (`skills/` and `plugins/`, doan stub included, and Codex's stores); MCP dependencies and hooks it plants there run outside the sandbox on a later profile server. The approval card shows the command. One probe saw an approved `apply_patch` fail to write a denied entry (`conversations/`); the real app did not check it.
  - Permission profiles and `turn/start` `permissions` are an experimental API. Ask first's sandbox is Doan's read-only profile, so a `sandbox_mode` or `default_permissions` written into Doan's Codex profile `config.toml` is unused.
  - A server that runs only in Full access cannot create or update `skills/.system` (Codex's built-in skills), and a Full access turn was seen without the plugin skills the same conversation listed in Ask first (cause unconfirmed).

### MCP connectors

Entry: `app/src/main/mcpConnectors/` (`service.ts`; pure rules in the other modules, each with its test). Product rules: PRODUCT §3 In scope 9 and 'MCP tools', §5 item 0 'MCP Connectors'. ACP runners open sessions with `mcpServers: []`.

- **Ownership.** Doan keeps the server list. The provider CLI registers a server in the Doan profile, runs its MCP OAuth sign-in with its own client, and keeps the token in the OS keychain, so Doan still ships no OAuth client ('Agent authentication'). Doan never reads, writes or deletes a token or keychain item; tokens go only through the provider's `mcp logout` and `mcp remove`. Commands are `COMMANDS` in `service.ts`, run with the provider's command, base arguments and `providerEnv` like any agent-auth process.
- **Server names.** Doan registers every server as `doan-<slug>` (`CONNECTOR_NAME`, `connectorName` in `names.ts`): fixed slugs for the presets (`presets.ts`), and for a URL server a slug of its host, at most 16 characters, with `-2`, `-3` when taken. Codex keys an MCP token's keychain account as `<name>|<hash of the URL>`, so a Doan server named like the person's terminal server at the same URL would share its token, and Doan's Disconnect, Remove or sign-out would delete the terminal's. Claude keeps MCP tokens inside the profile's keychain item and cannot collide, but follows the same rule. Every cleanup (reconcile, sign-out logouts) touches only `doan-*` names. Tools reach agents as `mcp__doan-<slug>__<tool>` (Claude) and `mcp__doan_<slug>__<tool>` (Codex).
- **URLs.** `parseConnectorUrl` normalizes input once, and the stored form is that URL's `href`. A stored URL is never normalized again (dropping one trailing slash is not idempotent): the same string is registered, recorded in a pending logout and written into a staging `config.toml`, because Codex derives the keychain key from it.
- **Leases and attempts.** Every MCP command holds the profile through `acquire(provider, "mcp", added)` (`agentAuth/manager.ts`); `added` carries the Claude sign-in's link-file environment (`hookLinks`). Like a model list read, an `"mcp"` lease never counts toward `stop_required` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30). Each provider runs one attempt (Connect, Disconnect or Remove) at a time, in a per-provider queue; Remove spans both providers and starts only while neither runs one. A sign-in or sign-out write first stops that provider's MCP work through `stopMcp`, separately from `stopRuns`, because a pending Codex logout runs in a temporary home and holds no lease. Quit stops every attempt, and a stopped attempt writes nothing.
- **Claude sign-in.** `claude mcp login` refuses to run without a terminal, so it runs in a node-pty (`pty.ts`). The pty's child leads its own process group (forkpty's `setsid`, pinned by `pty.test.ts`) and the whole group is stopped with `OwnedGroup`, since node-pty's `kill()` signals one pid ('Quit and process cleanup'). The link comes only from the `BROWSER` hook's link file. `mcpAuthLink` (`authLink.ts`) accepts an `https` PKCE code request returning to the CLI's own loopback `/callback`, on any host, because each server authorizes on its own host. Limits (`LIMITS` in `service.ts`): the link must arrive soon after the login's start (a Claude `browser` setting overrides `BROWSER`, and no link comes), and approval within 5 minutes of the link.
- **Codex sign-in.** `codex mcp add` registers and starts the sign-in, or `codex mcp login` for a server already registered. Codex opens the default browser itself, so Doan opens no link (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30 'Doan opened a second Codex sign-in page'). The approval limit is the same 5 minutes.
- **Connect failure logs.** For both providers, Doan logs the failure code and, when available, the process exit code.
- **Token storage.** The Codex base arguments (`agentAuth/providers.ts`) add `mcp_oauth_credentials_store="keyring"` to every Codex process, conversation servers included, for the reason `cli_auth_credentials_store` is there: `auto` can fall back to a file silently, and a token refresh during a conversation also saves. Codex therefore gets no plaintext check. After every Claude login, however it ended, a `.credentials.json` in the profile is deleted (`plaintextLeftovers`), and it fails an otherwise successful login as `storage_failed`.
- **State.** `connectorState` (`state.ts`) derives an item's state from readiness, the running attempt, the last status read, `everConnected` and a pending-logout block. A successful Connect adds the provider to that server’s `everConnected` entry. A successful Disconnect removes it. On explicit sign-out, or after a replacement commits with two different known account IDs, `logoutForAuth` clears that provider’s cached status reads and removes the provider from every server’s history in one store patch. During replacement, matching IDs or a missing ID preserve history. A pending logout masks a signed-in result because the token may belong to the previous account. For a registered server without usable access, history for that provider selects Reconnect needed (Reconnect). Without that history the row shows Not connected (Connect). Status commands use the network (Claude `mcp get` health-checks the server), so `refresh` runs them only when the page opens, after an attempt ends, on Retry, after a server is added and when the provider's sign-in changes, and never polls; a read that started before a success is discarded.
- **Registration.** A server registers at its first Connect with that provider, and a cancelled or failed first Connect keeps the registration (Codex's `mcp add` leaves one; the next Connect uses `mcp login`). A registration under the server's name at another URL is not that server: Connect logs it out and removes it, then registers the stored URL. Remove logs out, then removes, at every provider with a Doan connection, and only then drops the server from the file: Codex rebuilds the keychain key from the registered URL, so removing first would leave the token. Each time a provider becomes ready (app start, sign-in, replacement commit), it retries its pending logouts and reconciles: `reconcilePlan` logs out and removes the `doan-*` registrations that are neither a preset nor in the file (a Remove while that provider had no connection), including one whose name a later Add on the same host took again but whose registered URL differs from the stored one. URLs are compared as the stored string, which both CLIs list unchanged. An unreadable file reconciles nothing, and a missing registration is never added.
- **Files** (`files.ts`, `store.ts`; `files.test.ts` and `store.test.ts` are their schemas). Both are rewritten whole through an exclusive 0600 temporary file and a rename.
  - `<userData>/mcp-connectors.json` (`v: 1`): the URL servers and `everConnected`. An unreadable file is not read as defaults, because the providers keep Doan's registrations and an empty list would drift from them: it stays in place, refuses every write, and Add and Remove are refused, while presets still connect. A newer `v` is never read or written.
  - `<userData>/mcp-connectors-pending.json` (`v: 1`): the logouts that failed during a sign-out or replacement (provider, name, registered URL). It is separate so that blocking works while the connectors file is unreadable. A record that cannot be written, or whose removal cannot be, blocks that server's Connect in memory until quit. An unreadable file is moved aside to `.broken-<time>`; while the file was unreadable or newer, every `doan-*` server stays blocked for both providers until one logout of it succeeds in this run, since the lost records cannot be known.
- **Sign-out and replacement boundary.** agentAuth calls the injected `mcpLogout(provider, request)` (`McpLogoutRequest` in `agentAuth/manager.ts`) inside a sign-out and after a committed replacement, and `McpService.logoutForAuth` implements it; agentAuth knows no connector file, and the step never fails the sign-out. Targets come from the provider's own list (`claude mcp list`, `codex mcp list --json`) rather than the file, so a broken file stops no logout; a server the list shows signed out is skipped. When the list cannot be read, the targets are the union of the `doan-*` servers in the profile's own config (`profileConfig.ts`, names and URLs only) and every server Doan knows, so a config parser miss never drops a known server. The plan is `mcpLogoutPlan` and `mcpLogoutOutcome` (`plans.ts`):
  - Codex sign-out logs out each server before the provider logout, which does not clear MCP keychain entries; a server whose logout failed is removed from the live profile and recorded.
  - Claude sign-out clears connection history before skipping the per-server list and logout commands. `claude auth logout` clears the profile's MCP tokens, and Doan clears Claude's pending records once the connection is gone.
  - Replacement runs only after the commit (a replacement that does not finish leaves registrations and tokens, POSTMORTEM 2026-10-29), and the provider admits nothing until it ends. A replacement keeping the stored account ID logs nothing out; Codex stores none, so each of its replacements logs out. Claude logs out the live profile's servers. Codex logs out in the previous profile, then appends the logged-out servers (and those listed signed out) to the staging profile's `config.toml` with `codexServerToml`, because `codex mcp add` would start a new OAuth sign-in and open the browser even with a token present.
  - Pending logouts are retried when a provider becomes ready and right before that server's Connect, which proceeds only after success. Codex retries at app start whether or not it is signed in, in a temporary `CODEX_HOME` under the OS temporary directory whose `config.toml` holds only that server: the keychain key depends on the name and URL alone, and the old profile may be gone.
- **Codex turns.** Before every doan-owned turn the runner waits until every `doan-*` server in the profile's `config.toml` (`codexProfileServers`, `conversation/mcpStartup.ts`) reported a status other than `starting` in `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` for its thread, because a turn that starts earlier runs without that server. The notification has a `threadId` and no `turnId`, so the runner takes it by thread instead of the turn's transport. Past 1 s an `activity` (`kind: "other"`) says "Waiting for …", and at 10 s (`startupGate.ts`) a second one says which servers were not ready and the turn starts; no new event type exists, so the history format is unchanged. History is append-only, so the renderer (`threadItems.ts`) folds a Waiting line followed by the same servers' not-ready line into one.
  - Each provider has a connector generation (`McpService.generation`) that a successful Connect, Disconnect or Remove raises; a replacement commit does not, because a Codex replacement opens new runners on its new profile. A runner remembers the generation it read its servers at; before a turn, when the generation changed, it forgets those servers' statuses and sends `config/mcpServer/reload`, without restarting the server. A newly opened runner sends none, a refused reload is sent again before the next turn, and a running turn keeps its tools.
  - Claude starts its SDK on every turn with the `user` setting source, which reads the profile's servers, so it needs neither wait nor reload.
- **Codex MCP approvals** (`codexApproval.ts`, wired in `conversation/transport.ts` `codexPermission`). Before a tool without a read-only hint Codex sends `mcpServer/elicitation/request` with `_meta.codex_approval_kind: "mcp_tool_call"`; it becomes a `permission.request` with tool `mcpToolCall`. Allow, Deny and Allow for this session map to the answers in `codexApproval.ts`; Allow for this session appears only when `_meta.persist` offers `session`. `always` is never offered, because it writes a lasting approval into the provider's config. The tool name is read from `message` (fallback "Run a tool on <server>"), and the server is named from `connectorLabels`, passed through `CodexContext.labels` (`dispatcher.begin`), so a `doan-*` name never shows; other servers keep Codex's name. Any other elicitation, and an approval with no running turn to ask in or arriving after Stop, is declined at once, so the call fails and the turn goes on instead of ending as `session_unavailable` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27 'Codex ingress confused unowned requests and notification errors').
- **Claude MCP permissions.** The SDK's `canUseTool` already covers MCP tools; `claudePermissionSummary` reads `mcp__doan-<slug>__<tool>` as "Run <tool> on <name>", and a Doan connector tool's input never reaches a summary, activity or the history; another server's tool shows its arguments on the card ('Summaries' above).
- **IPC.** `mcp-connectors:*` invoke channels (`mcpConnectors/ipc.ts`), main window's main frame only, listed in `senderAllowed.ts`; main sends `mcp-connectors:changed` with the whole snapshot, a revision and each provider's generation. `open-link` reopens only main's latest link for the attempt. The renderer imports only `presets.ts` by value (types from the others); `presets.ts` and `names.ts`, which renderer tests import, stay free of Node modules (Settings IPC 'Renderer import boundary').

### Design preset reads

The app reads the CLI's design presets (§6, §12) for the composers' preset pickers; the renderer keeps no catalog copy. Entry: `app/src/main/designSkills.ts` (`designSkills.test.ts`).

- **Channels and callers.** `design-skills:list|get` are allowed to the main window and the prototype window's composer (`senderAllowed.ts`); other senders and malformed arguments get `request_refused` and no CLI runs. The app does not judge the id; the CLI's `usage_error` comes back.
- **Execution.** One run of the CLI `cliCommand` selects ('CLI lookup and local package' above), id after `--`, no cwd or repo, a timeout, no cache or retry. A spawn error is `cli_error`/`unavailable`, never a rejected promise. No model, conversation or file write.
- **Interpretation**: app-side failures are `cli_error` reasons; any other `ok: false` passes the CLI's envelope unchanged. `get` requires the requested id and the body's end marker line, so a cut body is never returned. Success keeps only contract fields; a missing `group` or another `result.version` is still success. A failure is never an empty list. App failures have empty `nextSteps`; `message` is display text, not contract.
- **Telling an older CLI.** A `list()` `usage_error` means no `design-skill` command; a `get(id)` one after a successful `list()` is an unknown id. stderr is never read for meaning; its first line is only quoted in the failure when the CLI exits non-zero without JSON on stdout.
- **Examples.** `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/`.
- **Conversations.** `PRESET_GUIDANCE` (`conversation/prompt.ts`) is one fixed paragraph on every turn pointing at `doan skill`; main adds no list or body and no classifier, so the app cannot show which preset a request will map to.
- **Connecting a chosen preset.** A chosen preset is a fixed first line of the user's text (`composeRequest` in `app/src/renderer/homeComposer.ts`) that the guide's named-preset rule reads; there is no rewriting API. The UI cannot know whether a preset needs a selected frame, so the agent confirms the target (BACKLOG.md 'Undecided'). Project skills use the same mechanism: one fixed line per highlighted skill after the preset line. The line formats and their stripping live in `app/src/main/conversation/requestLines.ts`, which has no imports so the renderer and main's conversation title share it; display and titles strip the fixed-line block of every paragraph, because a combined queue joins composed requests.

## 8. CLI stack: Go native binary

- `doan` is a native Go binary for fast startup, shipped inside the app bundle (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'). The cost of two languages is that formats exist on both sides; §11 keeps them in one schema source.
- Dependencies are pinned in `go.mod`. `golang.org/x/net/html` parses canvases with the browser's HTML5 rules; `golang.org/x/sys/unix` provides the no-follow, descriptor-relative opens that session recording, the storage exclude file and export rely on (§4 'Shell check session associations', §12 'Storage'); `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm` matches path spellings by NFC, as macOS does; `golang.org/x/text/cases` upper-cases the export viewer's project initial as JavaScript's `toUpperCase` does; santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema validates shared formats (§11).

## 9. Distribution

- **CI gate**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs only the shared gate (`verify.mjs`, without `--eval`; AGENTS.md 'Commands'), no e2e, packaging or signing. After `npm --prefix app ci` it downloads the Electron binary (`node -e "require('electron')"` in `app/`), because the injected scripts' drift tests run in it (§7 'Home tests without a DOM'). It runs on pull requests to and pushes to `develop` and `main` on the pinned `macos-26` runner, and other workflows can call it with `workflow_call`. It runs as three `shard` matrix jobs (`verify.mjs --shard <i>/3`: Vitest shards of app-test, shard 1 also running every other step), each with a timeout of about twice the measured shard 1 time.
  - The job id `gate` is the required check name for branch protection, so the job has no display name. It needs the shards and runs `if: always()`, failing unless every shard succeeded, because a skipped required check counts as passing.
  - The workflow uses no secrets, reads with `permissions: contents: read` and never uses `pull_request_target`, because the repository is public and pull requests can come from forks. Actions are pinned to commit SHAs.
  - Cancelling an older run applies to pull requests only; on `develop` or `main` a cancelled run would leave a merged commit without a result.
  - Node is pinned in the root `.node-version`, which local runs and CI both read; steps such as `node --test 'app/runtime/*.test.ts'` load TypeScript directly and depend on the version. Go comes from `go.mod`: `go` is the minimum language version and `toolchain` the exact Go release that builds the CLI. `actions/setup-go` (`go-version-file: go.mod`) installs the `toolchain` version when the line is there and otherwise only the `go` line's version, so without it the signed CLI would be built with the `.0` release; a local `go` older than `toolchain` downloads that release under the default `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto`.
- **Local package**: `npm --prefix app run package` builds the unsigned arm64 `Doan.app` (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'). It stays unsigned so eval, runtime probes and Codex sessions need no certificate, and their notification and keychain state does not change identity. It is a `--mac dir` build, so it has no update feed (below). A copy launched directly in an Applications folder runs agent setup against the launching HOME (§7 'Agent setup').
- **Fuses**: `build.electronFuses` in `app/package.json` flips the packaged Electron's fuses before signing, in the local package and in CI alike. `runAsNode`, `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` and `enableNodeCliInspectArguments` are off, so the signed binary cannot run as plain Node or take `NODE_OPTIONS` or inspector flags and act under Doan's signature, Keychain items included; Doan never runs its own binary as Node. `enableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` and `onlyLoadAppFromAsar` are on, so the app runs only from an `app.asar` whose header hash matches the one electron-builder writes into `Info.plist`; `app.asar.unpacked` (node-pty) is outside that check. No fuse covers Chromium's remote-debugging switches, so the packaged app exits at startup when launched with `--remote-debugging-port` or `--remote-debugging-pipe` (`app/src/main/remoteDebugging.ts`); Chromium opens that server only after the main script starts. The unfused `node_modules` Electron of dev runs and Playwright probes keeps the switches.
  - With `enableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` off, Electron also ignores `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS` and removes it from `process.env` (measured on 45.3.4), so a launching environment cannot add a CA to the app. Main reads launchd's value back at startup, trusts it for its own requests and passes it to agents (§7 'Launch environment').
  - `resetAdHocDarwinSignature` is on because the local package is unsigned: flipping rewrites `Electron Framework`, and on arm64 its stale ad-hoc signature makes macOS kill the app at launch (measured). CI signs right after flipping, so the reset changes nothing there.
  - Playwright's `_electron.launch` always passes `--inspect=0`, so it cannot launch the packaged app; probes that need Playwright launch the built `app/` with the `node_modules` Electron.
- **Signing in CI**: Developer ID signing and notarization run only in GitHub Actions, in the `sign` job of `.github/workflows/test-build.yml` (tester builds) and `release.yml` (releases). No machine's keychain is involved, and no encrypted key is ever committed, because the repository is public.
  - Both workflows start from a tag push: `tester-*` for a tester build, `v*` for a release, `release-dryrun-*` for a release build without the version check or the draft. `workflow_dispatch` is not used: it only starts when the workflow file is on the default branch, and `main` changes only through `/release` or `/publish`. A tag push runs the workflow file of the tagged commit.
  - Jobs: `source` (the tag commit must be on `origin/develop`, or `origin/main` for `v*`; releases also run `checkReleaseVersion.mjs --tag`), then the CI gate through `workflow_call`, then `sign`, and for `v*` only `publish`. The tag check is defence in depth: a tag's own workflow file runs, so the real guard is the environment's required reviewer.
  - The `release` environment holds the secrets and opens them only to `sign`: required reviewer = the maintainer; the maintainer approves tester builds and dry runs, while `/release` approves its own release run through the `pending_deployments` API after checking the run's head SHA is the tag commit, because invoking `/release` covers every approval; deployment refs = the tags `v*`, `tester-*` and `release-dryrun-*`. Secrets: `MAC_CERT_P12_BASE64` (the Developer ID Application certificate and key as a base64 `.p12`, passed as `CSC_LINK`), `MAC_CERT_PASSWORD` (`CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`), `APPLE_API_KEY_P8` (the App Store Connect API Team key's `.p8` content), `APPLE_API_KEY_ID`, `APPLE_API_ISSUER`; variable `MAC_IDENTITY_NAME` (`<Name> (<Team ID>)`, passed as `-c.mac.identity`). The first `sign` step checks they are set and that the issuer is a UUID, the key ID ten capitals or digits and the `.p8` a private key, printing only names, lengths and whether a value holds whitespace; a malformed ID otherwise fails only at notarization, after the app is signed. Agents never read, print or set them; the one-time setup and renewal belong to the `/release` skill.
  - electron-builder imports the `.p12` into its own temporary keychain on the runner; no intermediate certificate was added. `APPLE_API_KEY` must be a file path, so `sign` writes the `.p8` to `$RUNNER_TEMP` and removes it in an `if: always()` step. The dmg is notarized with `notarytool submit --key … --key-id … --issuer …`.
  - Signing is passed as command-line overrides (`-c.mac.identity`, `-c.dmg.sign=true`), so `app/package.json` keeps `mac.identity: null`. electron-builder signs every binary under `Contents/`, including `Resources/bin/doan` and node-pty's `pty.node`/`spawn-helper`. CI builds carry Team ID `7H4HNHTB45` and a designated requirement on it (`certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "7H4HNHTB45"`, not a cdhash), the same as the earlier locally signed tester builds, so installed copies keep their notification and keychain permissions while the Team ID stays the same.
  - Entitlements: `app/resources/entitlements.mac.plist` holds only `allow-jit` and `allow-unsigned-executable-memory`, and `mac.entitlements` and `mac.entitlementsInherit` both point to it. Without `entitlementsInherit`, electron-builder signs helpers, the CLI and `spawn-helper` with its template, which adds `disable-library-validation`; Doan keeps library validation on because it holds agent credentials. Under these entitlements the window, node-pty and the `osascript` administrator path of agent setup were checked to work; Doan only spawns `/usr/bin/osascript`, so it needs no `automation.apple-events`.
  - Without notarization credentials electron-builder only warns, so `sign` requires "notarization successful" in its log. The dmg wait is capped at 30 minutes; a timeout prints the submission id, which `notarytool wait <id>` can follow.
  - `app/scripts/verifySignedPackage.mjs <dmg> [<zip>]` checks the result the way a tester's Mac will: `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, a Developer ID authority, the runtime flag and a secure timestamp on every Mach-O, entitlements against the committed plist, the fuse wire in `Electron Framework` against `build.electronFuses` (`compareFuses` in `releaseChecks.mjs`), the designated requirement, `stapler validate`, `spctl` for the app and for a quarantined dmg copy (electron-builder silently leaves the dmg unsigned when it cannot find the identity, and only this catches it), and with a zip the app inside it and the zip's `latest-mac.yml` entry. CI runs it before uploading; `/tester-build` and `/release` run it again on the downloaded files.
- **Tester builds**: `/tester-build` pushes `tester-<date>-<sha>` at `origin/develop`, and `test-build.yml` leaves `Doan-tester-<date>-<sha>.dmg` as an Actions artifact for 7 days. Tester builds have no update feed.
- **Release builds**: `/release` pushes `v<version>` at the merge commit on `main` (develop → main, or `hotfix/<slug>` → main for a hotfix). `release.yml` builds `--mac dmg zip`, then `publish` (no environment, no secrets, `contents: write`) checks the version again with a token that can see drafts and runs `gh release create v<version> --draft --verify-tag` (`--prerelease` for `-rc.N`) with no notes; `/release` checks the draft and, when the checks pass, adds the notes and publishes it. electron-builder never creates the tag (a draft published without one would get a tag on the default branch's HEAD), so the tag comes first.
  - **Repository settings.** Immutable releases are on, so a published Release's assets and tag cannot change (drafts still can, which the resume path relies on), and the ruleset "Release tags are permanent" refuses deleting or moving any `refs/tags/v*`; tester and dry-run tags are outside it and are still deleted after use. Secret scanning with push protection and Dependabot alerts are on; Dependabot opens no pull requests.
  - Assets: `Doan-macos-arm64.dmg` (no version, so `releases/latest/download/Doan-macos-arm64.dmg` stays stable), `Doan-<version>-arm64-mac.zip`, its `.blockmap` and `latest-mac.yml`. The dmg is uploaded once, after stapling, because stapling changes its bytes; the dmg entry in `latest-mac.yml` keeps the pre-staple hash, which the macOS updater does not use (it downloads the zip, whose app is stapled).
  - `app/scripts/checkReleaseVersion.mjs <version>` (or `--tag v<version>`, which also requires `app/package.json` to match) accepts only `x.y.z` and `x.y.z-rc.N`, newer than the latest published stable release and than earlier tags of the same base, and fails with `bad_semver`, `tag_mismatch`, `published_exists`, `draft_exists` or `not_newer`. A tag alone, without a release, passes, so a failed run can be rerun; an existing draft must be deleted first.
- **Update feed**: only release builds contain `Contents/Resources/app-update.yml`. electron-builder writes it for every dmg or zip target, even with `--publish never`, and without a `publish` setting it derives a GitHub feed from the repository; only an explicit `publish: null` stops it, and the CLI cannot pass that (`-c.publish=null` arrives as the string `"null"`; only `mac.identity` is coerced). So `test-build.yml` builds with a generated config that copies `build` with `publish: null`, and the local `--mac dir` package never gets one. rc releases also write `latest-mac.yml`. The app treats `app-update.yml` as the feed switch (`hasFeedConfig`, §7 'App updates'): it reads `latest-mac.yml` from the release that `app-update.yml` names and downloads the zip, never the dmg. With `allowPrerelease`, electron-updater's GitHub provider (6.8.11, and unchanged through 7.0.0-alpha.13) takes a stable tag only for an `alpha` or `beta` copy, so on its own a `-rc.N` copy picks only the newest `-rc` tag; the app therefore also reads the stable release for a prerelease copy and takes the newer of the two (§7 'App updates'), so `x.y.z-rc.N` moves to a later rc or to a stable release. A stable copy takes only the release GitHub's `/releases/latest` names. Updates replace the bundle in place, so `~/.local/share/doan/current` and the links through it keep pointing at the new CLI (§7 'Agent setup').
- **Notices**: the package carries the root `LICENSE` as `Contents/Resources/LICENSE` and `cli/internal/designskill/notices/` as `Contents/Resources/notices/` (`extraResources`).
- **App icon**: `mac.icon` is the Icon Composer bundle `app/resources/icon.icon` (white fill, one `doany.png` layer: the V2 artwork scaled to 72% of the 1024 canvas on its longer side, centred; shadow and translucency off). electron-builder compiles it with `actool` into `Contents/Resources/Assets.car` (`CFBundleIconName` `Icon`) and `icon.icns`, which also becomes the dmg volume icon. macOS 26 applies the icon shape and margins itself; a full-bleed PNG or icns is drawn shrunk on a backing plate once the app is registered in `/Applications`, so the icon stays a `.icon` bundle. `actool` comes with Xcode 26 or later (not the command line tools alone), so packaging needs it.
- **Installer disk**: `build.dmg` in `app/package.json` lays out the dmg: a white background (`app/resources/installer-background.png` 640 × 536, `@2x`) with the buddies' group photo at the top, an arrow between Doan (200, 306) and the Applications link (440, 306), and “A place for your next idea.” below them. The PNGs hold only the draft's background part; Finder draws the icons, labels, title and bars. electron-builder sizes the outer window from the background and ignores `dmg.window` when a background is set, and Finder's path and status bars cover the bottom of the content, so the artwork's text ends 105 px above the bottom edge (checked on one macOS version only, BACKLOG.md 'Not verified in the real app'). In Dark appearance Finder may draw the icon labels white on the white background; that is accepted, because the icons and the arrow carry the action. Draft: `.doan/canvases/buddy-installer.html` (`installer-row-tagline`).
- **Doan is licensed under Apache-2.0** (root `LICENSE`).
- Design preset notices (`cli/internal/designskill/notices/`: Impeccable's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE.md` byte for byte, the ehmo/platform-design-skills MIT license, Doan's `MODIFICATIONS.md`) are embedded in the CLI and shipped as files in the package ('Notices' above). `SOURCES.md` keeps per-file provenance in the repo only.
- **Doan (app and CLI) does not handle GitHub tokens.** It neither reads nor stores them, except the Copilot connection's token held encrypted only for Copilot agent authentication (§7 'Agent authentication'). The agent does git and PRs with the user's credentials.

## 10. Moving between machines

- Doan never commits or pushes. With storage `repo`, canvases and `.doan/config.json` (versioned by `v`) move with `git pull`; with storage `local` they stay in the checkout that has them and move only as exported files (PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files'); the app picks pulled canvases up through file watching. Canvas read state stays on each device (§7 'Seen canvases').
- Everything in userData, plus Doan's agent credentials, is machine-local and is not carried over (§11 'Device-local files'): a new machine redoes first run (agent sign-in, project choice; a pulled repo needs no setup), and opening Doan from Applications sets up the CLI and skills again (§7 'Agent setup').

## 11. Shared formats

- JSON Schema files in root `schema/` are the source of truth for every format both languages touch: `.doan/config.json`, the CLI envelope, `runtime.json`, `app-socket.v1`/`v2`/`v3`/`v4`/`v5`/`v6`/`v7` and `sessions.v1`. TypeScript (ajv, `app/src/formats/validate.ts`) and Go (santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema v6, `cli/internal/formats/validate.go`) validate against the same files, draft 2020-12. `schema/embed.go` embeds them into Go; the Go module sits at the repo root because `go:embed` cannot reach parent directories.
  - Exception: each command's `result` has no schema; §12 states it and Go produces it, so two implementations cannot drift. `onboarding` passes on the app's v2 `result` and `active` the app's v1 selection without `root`, each validated against its socket schema first. The envelope schema keeps `result` as any value, and CLI integration tests validate the envelope, then deep-compare `result`.
- Shared fixtures in `schema/fixtures/` are read by both languages' tests.
- **Cross-file schemas**: each schema has an absolute `https://doan.design/schema/<name>.v<N>.schema.json` `$id`. Both languages register every schema before compiling; Go's external loader refuses network and disk fallback.
  - `app-socket.v1`'s root constrains nothing on purpose; callers must use the `$defs/request`, `$defs/response` or `$defs/selection` validator.
  - Socket objects reject unknown properties; numeric version normalization belongs to the readers, not the schema.
  - `runtime.json` is machine-local but shared-schema validated because TypeScript writes and Go reads it; `sessions.v1` likewise because Go writes and TypeScript reads. Filesystem facts (existence, canonical paths, profile equality, cwd containment) are checked outside the schema, and session IDs are opaque.
- **Onboarding, work, open, handoff, prototype and remove sockets**: readers dispatch on numeric `v` before validating. Their fixtures live in `schema/fixtures/app-socket-v2/` through `schema/fixtures/app-socket-v7/`, not `app-socket/`, because v1-only readers glob that directory. v3 `session` follows the opaque session-ID contract of `sessions.v1`, and `session` and `provider` come together or not at all. v3 `update` carries `frame` (v4's `$defs/frameId`, referenced by its absolute `$id`), `note` (`$defs/note`, shared with the reply) and `conversation` (the same opaque-ID contract, never with `session` or `provider`), each only on `update`; `update` stayed in v3 without a new `v` (§4 'Work signals over app-socket v3'). The v3 `ok: true` reply may carry `others` (1–8 elements of `kind`, `provider` from the five provider ids, `frame`, `note`; `additionalProperties: false`, with no id or handle field ever), also without a new `v`: requests are unchanged and only the reply grows (§4 'Best effort'). v4's `frame` request field must be non-empty, contain no C0 control or DEL, and fit in 200 code points. A deep link shares the content checks and limits the entire URL to 8,192 UTF-16 code units (§7 'Parsing'). The v4 reply's `frame` is `found`, `missing` or `none`, and `not_ready` is one of its error codes (§4). v5's `text` limit is in code points and sized for six-byte JSON escapes (Go `handoffTextLimit` and TypeScript `HANDOFF_TEXT_LIMIT` are pinned to it by tests that read the schema), and its `handoff` key pattern matches the CLI's own check (§4 'Handoff over app-socket v5'). v6's `frame` is v4's `$defs/frameId` by its absolute `$id` (code points, while the CLI and app also cap UTF-16 units; the fixtures hold an astral ID inside one bound and outside the other), its `mark` is convention 14's literal, and its reply codes carry no frame judgment (§4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6'). The `work`, `open`, `handoff` and `export` results have no schema (§12). The onboarding envelope mapping is data in `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/onboarding/mapping.json`, read by Go, TypeScript and the CLI translator test; envelope tests glob only `valid/` and `invalid/` there, so other subdirectories are not read as envelopes.
- **Product-name fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/brand/cases.json` classifies each `doan`/`Doan` occurrence; app and CLI string tests fail on a lowercase `doan` in prose. Test data, not a format.
- **Project thumbnail fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/project-thumb/cases.json` holds project names with the hue and initial the app's sidebar thumbnail and the export viewer's project crumb must both draw (non-ASCII, emoji, multi-code-point first graphemes, whitespace JavaScript and Go trim differently). Go and TypeScript tests both read it. Test data, not a format.
- **Open-link fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/open-link/cases.json` holds links Go must build (root, path, expected URL) and URLs TypeScript must refuse (§7 'Deep links'); Go builds and TypeScript reads, so both tests read it. Test data, not a format.
- **Prototype-mark fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/prototype-mark/cases.json` holds raw mark values with their validity and size, and body fragments with the marks a parser must report outside `<template>` and the `doan check` codes they produce (convention 14). Go's parser and check, the TypeScript mark parser and the injected prototype script all read it. Test data, not a format: no schema and no `v`.
- **Prototype-link fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/prototype-links/cases.json` holds body fragments with the frames and links (value and enclosing frames) a parser must report outside `<template>`, the `doan check` codes they produce and their link groups, plus frames-and-links-only group cases (convention 15). A link group is the playable frames that links join, direction ignored: a link joins each playable enclosing frame to a playable target, frames keep document order and groups are ordered by their first frame. Go's parser, check and `canvas.LinkGroups` and TypeScript `app/src/main/prototype/links.ts` read it; the TypeScript side takes the parsed frames and links, not the HTML, and its frames may be pre-filtered to the playable ones with the same result. Ids and values stay raw: a caller that caps them leaves an over-long one out instead of cutting it, since a cut id can match another. `clicks.json` beside it holds the prototype window's click decisions (convention 15, §3 'Prototype window' 'Links'): each case is a canvas body, the frame that plays, the element clicked with `element.click()` (inside a shadow root or an iframe when named) and the `{goto}` posted, or none. The injected prototype script's test and the exported prototype's `isolate.js` test run it in a real DOM, and the board script's and the injected prototype script's tests run `cases.json`'s bodies there too (§7 'Home tests without a DOM'). Test data, not a format: no schema and no `v`.
- **Never-served fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/serve-withheld/cases.json` holds repo-relative paths and whether §3 'Never served' holds them back. Go `repo.Withheld` and the app server's `withheld` (`app/src/main/serve/path.ts`) both read it, so the server and `doan check`/export cannot drift apart. Test data, not a format.
- **Design preset fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/` holds envelopes both languages read, not a schema, because the result shape is not a schema (§12).
- **Device-local files without shared schemas**: everything else in userData (`folders.json`, `auto-folders.json`, `onboarding.json`, `preferences.json`, `agent-setup.json`, `ui-state.json`, `canvas-seen.json`, `mcp-connectors.json`, `mcp-connectors-pending.json`, `star-prompt.json`, `thumbnails/`, `conversations/`, `agent-auth/`, `agent-runtimes/`, `terminal/`, `attachments/`; owners in §7) is app-owned, validated internally by TypeScript, never shared with the CLI and never committed.
- **Canvas row menu and prototype window Export** (Move to Trash, Export): add no userData file, schema, `v` or fixture. The Trash belongs to the OS, the app remembers no export folder, the `canvas:*` and `prototype:*` replies are app-internal, and `doan export`'s `result` has no schema (§12).

## 12. CLI output contract

The public contract for external agents. Envelope: `schema/cli-envelope.v1.schema.json`; usage: `doan help`; what an agent does on each code: the guide `cli/internal/cli/skill.md` (§6).

- Every command has `--json`. Success `{v: 1, ok: true, result}`; failure `{v: 1, ok: false, error: {code, message, nextSteps}}`, where `nextSteps` tells the agent what to do next.
- `error.code` is a snake_case pattern, not an enum, so new codes need no new `v`.
- `error.config` (optional): when a repository command fails on a config file — `repo.Find`'s config stage could not stat it (other than missing or `ENOTDIR`), it is not a regular file, or it could not be read or parsed, or its `v` is unsupported — the failure names that file's absolute path, the parent's when discovery stopped at a broken parent config. An explicit exception, like `root`: a failed command has no root to be relative to. Other failures (canvas enumeration, serving scope, the start path, `repo_not_found`, `doan config`'s value checks, `doan init`'s own checks) carry none, and human output never shows it. Being optional, it kept `v` at 1.
- `error.storageReason` (optional, only `"not_git"`): `ls` without a config (`repo_not_found`) in an existing directory that is in no Git repository (a `.git` at or above HOME does not count, §13), so the app can plan the repository Keep out of Git would create before Set up. Other commands and other `repo_not_found` causes carry none. Being optional, it kept `v` at 1; an older strict reader rejects it, which only a development app pointed at a newer CLI meets.
- Exit codes tell failure kinds apart; `error.code` and `nextSteps` pick the action (exit 2 covers both `usage_error` and `repo_not_found`, which need different actions). The full code → exit table is `ExitCode` in `cli/internal/cli/envelope.go`, with onboarding pinned by `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/onboarding/mapping.json`.

| Exit | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | Success |
| 1 | `internal_error`: a bug, and any code `ExitCode` does not map, so mistakes are not hidden |
| 2 | Caller-fixable input: arguments, repository and config, the canvas or frame named, export refusals, the asking conversation or root of `doan handoff`, `doan onboarding …` or `doan remove` run from an app conversation (`conversation_refused`), a failed `git init` ('Storage'), and onboarding request refusals (`destination_exists` too, which `doan export` reuses) |
| 3 | App unavailable: `app_not_running`, `app_protocol_mismatch`, `project_not_open` (also `app_launch_failed`, onboarding `session_stale`) |
| 4 | `doan check` ran and found error-level findings; envelope stays `ok: true` because the findings are in `result` |
| 5 | Refusals and failed operations the user or the app must resolve: onboarding's, the storage failures other than a failed `git init` (also from `init` and `config`, 'Storage'), `busy` (the app cannot act now), the `doan handoff` refusals the conversation's state decides, and `doan remove`'s `conversation_running` |

- Exception: `doan work` turns every app failure into `ok: true`, exit 0, with `delivered: false` (§4 'Work signals over app-socket v3'); only its argument, repository and relative `DOAN_RUNTIME` errors fail (exit 2; an I/O error while finding the repository is `internal_error`, exit 1). `ExitCode` is unchanged.
- `app_not_running` `nextSteps` include the sandbox recovery order (open Doan; run `doan` outside the sandbox or ask approval; then Settings… > About). `project_not_open` covers a folder not saved in Doan, including another worktree of a saved repository, except that a terminal `doan work start` or `update` registers such a worktree (§4 'Auto-registration') and so reports `delivered: true` when it finishes in time.

### Commands and `result` shapes

Optional keys are omitted, never `null`; empty lists are `[]`; paths are repo-relative with `/` unless stated. These shapes are the contract (§11); the app's TypeScript readers depend on them. Edge cases live in `cli/internal/cli/` and its tests.

- **`doan init [--token-css <path>] [--storage local|repo [--git-init]]`** → `{initialized, repo, storage, tokenCss?, created, nextSteps}`. App–CLI contract for project setup (PRODUCT §5 item 8 and app item 0.2).
  - Targets `--repo` or cwd with no upward search; a repo nested in another is allowed. A valid config makes it a no-op (`initialized: false`); a broken one fails and is never overwritten.
  - Token CSS must be a regular file inside the repo, stored as real repo-relative on-disk spelling. Without `--token-css` it prompts only on a terminal without `--json`.
  - Storage ('Storage' below): `--storage local` writes the exclude pair and no `.gitattributes`, and `created` lists the label `.git/info/exclude`; a plain `init` follows a root that is already `local`. `--storage local` on a set-up checkout and explicit `--storage repo` on a `local` root are `usage_error` pointing to `doan config --storage`; a plain `init` with storage `unknown` fails `local_exclude_failed`.
  - The config is published last, after the `.gitattributes` lines or the exclude pair, because a discoverable config marks initialization complete: an exclude write that fails leaves nothing, and a retry never sets up a repo-mode checkout by accident (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25 'Init published success before merge setup could fail'). No serialization between initializers and no fsync guarantee (`init.go`).
  - `nextSteps` always points to the `context` design preset; `init` runs no model or preset.
- **`doan config [--token-css <path> | --clear-token-css] [--add-serve <path>]... [--remove-serve <path>]... | --storage local|repo [--git-init]`** → `{repo, tokenCss?, serve, changed, storage, storageReason?}`, plus `written` (labels of the files changed: `.gitattributes`, `.git/info/exclude`) and `guidance` (the switch's consequences for the person, PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files') with `--storage`. No write flags is read-only; unchanged operations keep the file and its mtime (product rules: PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Project Settings').
  - Paths are repo-relative or absolute inside the repo and stored in canonical repo-relative spelling; token CSS must be a regular file; serve additions must exist and may not be the repo root, `.doan` metadata (except `canvases/`, `ds/`, `assets/`), or a `.git` directory or `.env*` file anywhere in the repo or anything inside one (§3 'Never served'); removal accepts targets that no longer exist.
  - `--storage` cannot be combined with another write flag, so a failed call never applies half a change. A request for the current mode is `changed: false` and writes nothing, even when the exclude file cannot be written.
  - Writes go only to the in-repo config, and with `--storage` also to `.gitattributes` and the exclude file ('Storage'); a linked `.doan` never redirects them. App calls serialize per root; separate CLI writers have no lock (`config.go`, `config_ops.go`).
- **`doan ls`** → `{root, scope, storage, storageReason?, canvases: [{path, id?, title?, width?, frames: [{id, title?, implements?}], error?: {code, message}}], git?}`.
  - `canvases` merges `.doan/canvases/` and `.doan/ds/` (lower-case `.html` only, §1 convention 1) in path byte order with no type field; the path identifies the type (§1, §13). `root` is the real absolute root; `scope` is the serving scope from the same `repo.Scope` that backs `css_not_served`.
  - A read error is not an empty list: an unreadable canvas sets the row's `error` (`unreadable_canvas`) (PRODUCT §5 item 9). A folder under `.doan/canvases/` or `.doan/ds/` whose entries cannot be read, the directory itself included, is a row of its own in the same path order, `{path, frames: [], error}` with `unreadable_folder`, and the rest is listed; it is not a canvas, and the app drops it from its canvas rows (`canvasRows` in `app/src/main/view.ts`). `message` never contains an absolute path. An unparsable but readable canvas has only `path` and `frames: []`, with no `error`. Other whole-set failures (a canvas directory that cannot be inspected, the serving scope) fail the command; `check` and `export` still fail on an unreadable folder, because they need the whole set (`repo.Canvases`).
  - `storage` and `storageReason` as in 'Storage' below.
  - Optional `git`: `{common, main?, branch?, detached?, worktrees}`, absolute canonical paths (an explicit exception, like `root`); `branch` and `detached` are exclusive; missing or broken metadata omits it without failing (§13 'Read-only Git metadata').
- **`doan active`** → `{repo, selection?}`, `selection` `{canvas: {path, id?}, frame?: {id, title?}, element?: {doanId?, selector, html, truncated?, text?}}`, omitted when nothing is selected or another project owns it (§4).
- **`doan check`** → `{findings, summary: {canvases, errors, warnings}}` (§1). `canvases` counts every inspected file, unparsable ones included; zero adds the `no_canvases` warning without failing. A canvas or canvas folder it cannot read fails the whole command with `internal_error` (exit 1), unlike `ls` (PRODUCT §5 item 9).
