- **`doan work start <canvas path>`** and **`doan work update <canvas path> [--frame <id>] [--note <text>]`** → `{delivered, others?}`, `others` only when the app's schema-valid `ok` reply listed other sessions on the same file (§4 'Other sessions'), in its order and shape; text mode prints the same result JSON; **`doan work done [<canvas path>...]`** → `{delivered, links, skipped}`. `delivered` is true only when the app accepted the v3 request (§4). `links` are `canvasLink`s for the valid paths in argument order, returned whatever `delivered` says; `skipped` lists invalid `done` arguments verbatim, and the done signal is still sent. No paths gives empty lists. A bad `start` or `update` path, and an `update` argument outside §4 'Update arguments', is `usage_error`.
- **`doan onboarding <start | status | check | sign-in | cancel | project inspect | project select | project clone | complete>`** → the v2 reply's `result` (§4). `project inspect`, `select` and `clone` take `--storage local|repo` and send `storage` only when it is given (§4 'Storage in onboarding'). No `--repo` or upward search; paths are absolute; only `start` may launch the app among the onboarding commands (`open` may too).
- **`doan open <canvas path> [--frame <id>]`** → `{opened, frame}` with `opened: true` and `frame` one of `found` (highlighted), `missing` (a frame was asked for but not found, only the canvas opened) or `none` (no frame asked). `found` and `missing` come from the canvas's static frame list, an approximation (§7 'Deep links'). Failures: `usage_error`, `canvas_not_found` for a missing file or a canvas the app does not list, `busy` with the reason in `message`, and the app-unavailable codes, `app_launch_failed` included. The app being unreachable, not ready or busy is never a success. Rules and the bootstrap: §4.
  - **`--frame <id> --prototype`** → `{opened: true, frame: "found", prototype: true}`: the app started playing that frame in the prototype window and the main window did not move. `--prototype` without `--frame` is `usage_error`. Before the app: `canvas_unreadable` (over 50 MiB, unreadable or unparsable; `nextSteps`: run `doan check`), `frame_not_found` (no such frame in the file, its ID shared, or over 200 UTF-16 units) and `not_prototype` (no valid mark; `nextSteps`: tell the person, mark it only if asked), all exit 2. From the app: `canvas_not_found` for a canvas it does not list or whose canvas ID two rows share (2, `nextSteps` adds `doan check`), `busy` only for first run and the buddy screen (5), and the app-unavailable codes as above. Rules: §4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6'.
- **`doan handoff <canvas path> --message <text|->`** → `{requested, canvas}` with `requested: true` and `canvas` the repo-relative path asked for; success means the app took the request, not that a conversation started (that is decided when the turn ends). Failures: `usage_error` (arguments, text, path, an over-long line), `not_app_conversation` (no valid `DOAN_HANDOFF`, or a key the app does not know), `canvas_not_found` for a missing file and `root_mismatch` (2); `app_not_running`, `app_protocol_mismatch` and `project_not_open` (3); `not_running`, `not_canvas_conversation`, `plan_mode`, `handoff_turn`, `already_requested` and `same_canvas` (5); a v5 `bad_request` is `internal_error` (1). It never launches the app, and `doan help` marks it as for Doan app conversations only. Rules: §4.
- **`doan remove [--repo <path>]`** → `{removed, project, roots}` with `removed: true`, `project` the group's name as the sidebar shows it and `roots` every root that left the list, as absolute paths (an exception to the repo-relative rule: the roots are other worktrees, outside this one). Failures: `usage_error` (arguments, a relative `DOAN_RUNTIME`), and `repo_not_found`, `invalid_config` and `unsupported_version`, whose `nextSteps` point to Remove from Doan in the app rather than `doan init` (2); `app_not_running`, `app_protocol_mismatch` and `project_not_open` (3); `busy` (the reason in `message`) and `conversation_running` (5); a v7 `bad_request` or a success that does not name the requested root is `internal_error` (1). It never launches the app, and `doan help` says to run it only when the user asks. Rules: §4.
- **`doan export <canvas path> [--frame <id> --prototype] -o <file> [--force]`** → `{path, output, bytes, frames, external, links}`, plus `prototype: true` and `linkedFrames` with `--frame <id> --prototype` (both keys are absent otherwise): `path` is the canvas as `ls` lists it, `output` the absolute file written (an explicit exception, like `root`), `bytes` its size, `frames` the canvas's static frame count, `external` the internet URLs the file still loads, as written (a `//host/x` URL is listed so but loads as `https:` in the file), and `links` the local `<a href>`, `<form action>` and other non-asset references left as written, both sorted and without duplicates; `linkedFrames` is how many frames of the start frame's link group the file holds besides the start frame (0 for a frame with no links). Shape pinned by `cli/internal/cli/export_test.go`; the pipeline is §3 'Exported canvas and viewer'.
  - The canvas argument must be a listed canvas (repo-relative, or absolute inside the repo; case and Unicode spelling map to the listed one), else `canvas_not_found`; a relative `-o` is read from the current directory. An output in any directory named `.doan` (any repo, any depth, also through a link) is a `usage_error`, because a file there is listed as a canvas.
  - `--frame` and `--prototype` come together: either alone is `usage_error`, because a frame alone means nothing to a whole-canvas export and refusing it keeps an agent from believing it exported one frame. The file is the prototype page (§3 'Exported canvas and viewer' 'Prototype page'); the text output is `Exported <path> frame <id> to <output> (<n> bytes)`.
  - Refusals come before any write, in this order (`Export` in `cli/internal/cli/export.go`), except that a canvas file over 50 MiB is `export_too_large` before it is parsed, and inlining stops at that limit, so only references before the stop are reported: a canvas that does not parse or lacks exactly one positive-integer `doan:width` is `export_refused` with no reference lines; with `--prototype`, the frame judgment of §4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6', before inlining so a wrong ID costs no inline; references Doan can't include (§3 'Exported canvas and viewer') are `export_refused`; a result over 50 MiB is `export_too_large`; an existing output is `destination_exists` (`--force` replaces it, a link at the output is replaced, not followed). `nextSteps` stay inside the guide's approval rules: they never tell an agent to move files or change config on its own.
  - Messages hold no absolute path. `export_refused` and `export_too_large` list one item per line as `- <item>: <detail>` (order: the `Refused` and `Largest` comments on `export.Result` in `cli/internal/export/inline.go`; line format: `export.go`), and the app parses those lines (§7 'Canvas row menu IPC'): an item may contain `": "`, a detail may not.
  - The file is written through a same-directory temporary file (`writeExportWith`). Without `--force` it is published with `link(2)`, which fails on any existing name, so an output that appeared while the page was built is `destination_exists` too, after the write; a volume without hard links (exFAT, some network shares) falls back to checking the output and renaming, which leaves that window open (accepted). With `--force` it is renamed over the output. A failure removes the temporary file, and so does a SIGINT or SIGTERM ('Parsing, output and structure'). A SIGKILL (the app's export timeout in `app/src/main/cli.ts` `runExport` included) can leave it. Only this process's own file is removed, never another export's temporary file of the same name pattern. It reads only repo files, so it needs no app.
- **`doan skill`** → `{version, body}`. `doan help` → `{usage}`.
- **`doan design-skill [<id>]`** reads presets embedded in the CLI; needs no repo, config or app and writes nothing. Without an id → `{version, presets: [{id, title, description, scope, group}]}` in catalog order, never bodies. With an id → `{version, preset, body, sources: [{repository, commit, path}]}`, `body` ending with `[doan:design-skill] end of preset <id> v<version>`. `group` values are in `cli/internal/designskill/catalog.json`; group order is first appearance. Lookup is by catalog key only, never a path; empty, invalid or unknown ids are `usage_error`. Cases: `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/`.

### Storage

Where a Doan root's files are kept (PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files'). Only Go reads and writes it (`cli/internal/repo/storage.go`, `storage_write.go`, `cli/internal/cli/storage.go`); the app reads `storage` from `ls` and `config`.

- **The mode is the exclude pair alone.** `<common dir>/info/exclude` (shared by every worktree of the repository; a submodule's is under the superproject's `.git/modules/<name>/`) holds a marker comment line `# doan: local` directly followed by the root's pattern `/<rel>/.doan/` (`/.doan/` for a root at the Git top level), `rel` relative to the Git top level, escaped byte by byte. gitignore has no end-of-line comments, so the marker cannot share the pattern's line. `.doan/config.json` records no mode, so the two cannot disagree. Lines a person wrote (`.doan`, `.gitignore` entries, `!.doan/`) are neither touched nor read as the mode; a differently cased spelling of the root makes a different pattern (accepted).
- **Values.** `storage` is `local` when the pair is present, `repo` when the exclude was read without it (or is missing, or the folder is not a Git repository), and `unknown` when `.git` or the exclude cannot be read. `storageReason`, omitted when there is none, is `not_git`, `git_unreadable` or `exclude_unwritable` (also a final-file link, a non-regular file, or a file that a pair would push over the 1 MiB cap). Git location comes from `gitLocation`, shared with `ReadGit`, so a `.git` at or above HOME is ignored the same way (§13).
- **Writes.** Adding opens the exclude `O_WRONLY|O_APPEND|O_NOFOLLOW`, creates a missing one exclusively (and `info/` at 0755), adds a newline first when the file does not end with one and keeps its CRLF style, so other writers' lines are never lost. Removing writes a filtered copy to a unique same-directory temporary file with the original's permissions, rechecks the original's identity and bytes right before the rename and refuses on a change, and removes the temporary on failure; every pair for the root is removed. The recheck is not a compare-and-swap against a writer arriving after it (accepted). Reading follows a final-file link as Git does; writing never does. Ancestor directory links are followed (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-24).
- **Order.** `init --storage local` writes the pair before the config; `config --storage repo` adds a missing `.gitattributes` line before removing the pair, so a failure leaves the root `local`; `config --storage local` leaves `.gitattributes` alone (its lines are harmless and the file is the team's).
- **`.gitattributes`.** `.doan/** linguist-documentation` is the only `.gitattributes` line Doan writes, required whenever storage is `repo` (`init` and `config --storage repo` add it when missing; `missingAttributes` in `cli/internal/cli/storage.go`). A `merge=union` line for `.doan/comments/*.jsonl` that earlier builds wrote is left alone, like any other line in the team's file. `init` leaves a set-up repo unchanged (`cli/internal/cli/run_test.go`).
- **Creating a repository** (`--git-init`, `init` and `config` only, with `--storage local`; `cli/internal/repo/gitinit.go`). The decision (`gitInitDecision`) refuses a folder at or above HOME, or with HOME unknown, with a HOME-specific `local_not_git` whose next steps never mention `--git-init`, with or without the flag; `--git-init` without `--storage local` is `usage_error`; a folder already in a repository ignores it. Otherwise, before any other write: git is located as clone does (first `git` on the injected `PATH`; `/usr/bin/git` only when the developer directory from `DEVELOPER_DIR`, `/var/db/xcode_select_link` or the CLT path has `usr/bin/git`; never `xcode-select`), else `git_missing` with nothing written. `<root>/.git` is claimed with an exclusive `mkdir` and its dev/ino recorded; an existing one is `git_init_failed`, left alone. `git init --quiet --template=` then runs in the root with an allow-listed environment (`HOME`, the injected `PATH`, `DEVELOPER_DIR`, `LANG`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`; every other `GIT_*` dropped), stdin closed and stderr discarded, so no hook samples are copied and the person's default branch and object format apply (sha256 repositories read like sha1). A failure is `git_init_failed`. After init `ReadStorage` must see the repository; the exclude pair then goes through the ordinary write, which creates the missing `info/`. If any later step fails, the `.git` is removed only when its dev/ino still match the claim; a removal that fails is `cleanup_failed`, whose message and next steps name the leftover `.git` path. Only the `.git` is rolled back; the other writes keep their usual failure behavior. `created` and `written` list `.git/`. A kill (the app's `CLI_TIMEOUT_MS`, quit, cancel, Ctrl-C) after init leaves the `.git` and pair without a config; the next setup reads a repository and proceeds (accepted). `PATH` and `DEVELOPER_DIR` reach the command through `Context.Git`, filled from the environment for `init` and `config` only.
- **Errors** (exit 5, the same codes as onboarding): `local_not_git` for local storage in a folder that is not a Git repository (its next steps say to ask the person and pass `--git-init` only when they agree), `local_exclude_failed` for Git metadata or exclude failures when a write is needed, and `git_missing` and `cleanup_failed` from repository creation; `git_init_failed` is exit 2. Messages hold no path or raw I/O error, except `cleanup_failed`'s `.git` path.
- **Labels.** `created`, `written` and onboarding `changes` name the exclude `.git/info/exclude`, a fixed label rather than a path: in a linked worktree or submodule `.git` is a file.
- No Git command runs except that `git init`, and `.git/index` is never read.

### Parsing, output and structure

- **Parsing**: `--json` is detected before parsing, so even a parse failure produces an envelope. Unknown flags are `usage_error`; `--` ends flags. The command is the first argument: a flag or `--` before it is `usage_error` naming that rule (a help flag, `--help` or `-h`, still prints usage and runs nothing), because Codex rules compare tokens with argv in order (§7 'Agent setup' 'Codex rule'). Details in `args.go` and its tests.
- **Output**: with `--json`, stdout is one envelope line and stderr is empty. Without it, failures go to stderr with the same exit code. The exception is the SIGINT/SIGTERM cleanup of a staged write: export's (§12 'Commands and `result` shapes'), and `repo.WriteStaged` for `.doan/config.json` (`init`, `config`), the `.gitattributes` lines (`init`, `config --storage repo`) and the exclude file's removal of the storage pair. It removes only this process's own temporary file, exits with 128 + the signal number and no envelope, and may print a cleanup failure to stderr. A SIGKILL (the app's CLI timeout, `app/src/main/cli.ts`) cannot be caught and can leave that file (`.doan/.config-*`, `.gitattributes-*`, `.exclude-*` beside the exclude file), as it can leave export's (accepted). Human output escapes C0/C1 control characters except LF and HT, because repository text from git, such as canvas titles, may carry terminal control sequences. Non-UTF-8 file bytes become U+FFFD so the envelope still validates. The stdout envelope does not escape `<`, `>` and `&` (`SetEscapeHTML(false)` in `cli/internal/cli/run.go`): the values are the same, and the six-byte escapes would push `doan skill --json` over the output budget (§6). App-socket requests (`json.Marshal`) and the export page escape as before.
- **Structure**: `main` only calls `cli.Run(args, stdin, stdout, stderr) int`. Command bodies never touch `os.Exit` (except a staged write's signal cleanup, above), writers, cwd, clock or environment, so tests and callers reuse them (`run.go`).
- (References: Orca's `{ok, result | error: {code, message}}` envelope, verified in its local bundle; open-claude-design's meaningful exit codes, github.com/maxritter/open-claude-design.)

## 13. Multiple repos and worktrees

- The app keeps several repo and worktree roots registered and watched, with one loaded workspace.
- **Discovery** (`cli/internal/repo/find.go`): `--repo` is checked alone and never walks up; otherwise the first directory upward with `.doan/config.json` wins, so the inner repo wins when nested. A broken inner config stops the search instead of falling through to an outer repo. `.git` is never consulted. The app never reads config itself; it uses `doan ls` and `doan config` (Show in Finder only checks that the config path the CLI named for a Config error exists, §7 'Recovery').
- **Paths inside the repo**: every repo path (`tokenCss`, `serve`, `--token-css`, `data-doan-implements`, local CSS links and `@import`s) resolves the same way: lexical check first (a missing `../x` is outside, not missing), then symlinks, then the real path compared with the real root by path segment, never string prefix. Errors that canvas or config content alone can cause are "missing" findings; other I/O errors are `internal_error`. `doan export` and `doan check` resolve through one `repo.Resolver` for the whole command (`cli/internal/repo/resolve.go`): it finds on-disk spelling from an index of each real directory, read once per command and keyed by the real path, never the lexical one, so a file created after that read is missing for the rest of the command (export still opens each file with `O_NOFOLLOW` when it reads it). Read errors are returned and not cached. One-off resolutions (`repo.Resolve`, `repo.Scope`, `tokenCss`) build their own.
- **Listing** (`files.go`): symlinks, and a symlinked `.doan` or canvas directory, are never followed, so nothing is read or written outside the repo. The one exception is the storage exclude file in the Git common directory (§12 'Storage'). The app's one path classifier is `app/src/formats/canvasPath.ts`; filesystem discovery stays in Go.
- The skill tells agents to ask when the instruction's root differs from their working directory (§1).

### The app's folders

Discovery stays in Go only; the app never re-implements listing, discovery or config reading. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 app items 0–1. Main: `app/src/main/shell.ts`.

- **Opening** runs `doan ls --json` from the picked folder so Go owns subfolder-to-root discovery, and stores `result.root`. **Reading** is always `doan ls --json --repo <stored path>` through the CLI `cliCommand` selects (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'); each folder fails alone.
- **Re-root**: on `repo_not_found` the app reruns `ls` from a hand-added folder and stores a different `root` it finds (an auto-registered root is never re-rooted upward, §4 'Auto-registration'); Set up project repeats this right before `doan init`, so the app never creates a nested repo. Success re-reads the stored path and never adopts `result.repo` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-21).
- **Watching** (`watch.ts`, `designChange.ts`): symlinked targets are not watched, matching the CLI's discovery. Served `scope` entries are watched through their parent directory, so atomic-rename saves and wiped build outputs are seen (limit: a nested served directory missing during a read loses its parent watcher until the next read). A canvas HTML name moves only its row's `rev`; any other or unknown name, or a served change, bumps `servedRev`. Atomic-write temporaries beside a changed canvas are ignored because agent editors create and rename them within one debounce. Settings and other metadata under `.doan/` never count as served changes. The root's gitdir (§7 'Seen canvases' 'Git signal') has its own non-recursive watcher, resolved again on each re-arm; only a `HEAD`, `FETCH_HEAD` or `ORIG_HEAD` change counts, and it only re-reads the git signal: no `doan ls`, served bump or canvas reload.
- **Canvas view updates** (`app/src/renderer/canvasView.ts`): the open canvas reloads only on `servedRev` or its own row's `rev` (§3 'Moved canvas', §3 'Accepted reload risk').
- **Row errors**: an `ls` row with `error` stays in the list, so consumers check the error first.
- **Home metadata**: `git` on list entries is the CLI's optional `ls` `git` result; `mtimeMs` and `changedAt` are app display fields, not CLI output. When the main window gains focus, the app rereads the roots to refresh Home metadata. The gitdir watcher uses changes to `HEAD`, `FETCH_HEAD` and `ORIG_HEAD` for canvas read state. Removing a folder never touches files on disk.

### Read-only Git metadata

Go interprets Git metadata for the optional `ls` `git` result and storage (§12); TypeScript tests `.git` presence during Add and reads remotes from `.git/config` for onboarding's project inspection (§7 'First-run onboarding'). The Git commands Doan runs are onboarding's clone (§7) and Keep out of Git's `git init` (§12 'Storage'); the Git metadata it writes is the storage exclude pair and the files `git init` makes. Project setup takes `not_git` from `ls`. Separately, for canvas read state and its watcher, TypeScript looks for the nearest `.git` at or above the root, stopping before the home directory (`app/src/main/gitSignal.ts`). The CLI's Git metadata algorithm is in `cli/internal/repo/git.go`.

- A `.git` marker at or above the canonical home directory is ignored, so a dotfiles repository cannot group unrelated projects. An invalid nearest marker never falls through to an outer repository.
- Metadata files must be regular and small and registrations are capped; broken metadata omits `git` without failing `ls`. The cap is not a total I/O or latency bound, and path text with a symlink inside a `..` span can parse differently from Git.

## 14. Agent evaluation

Development tooling for [USABILITY-EVAL.md](./USABILITY-EVAL.md), run by `/agent-eval`; not shipped. It measures how real Claude Code and Codex use Doan through the shell CLI. Runner `eval/run.ts` and `eval/lib/`, with no package manifest of its own (socket validation loads the app's installed Ajv); scenarios `eval/scenarios/<id>/` (format and validation in `eval/lib/scenario.ts`; each scenario's intent in its `rubric.md` and `checks.ts`). Results go to `.scratch/agent-eval/<round>/`; what matters is copied into the month's `docs/usability-eval/rounds-<YYYY-MM>.md` and the issue list in USABILITY-EVAL.md.

- **Isolation** (`eval/lib/workspace.ts`): each run gets a temp directory under `/tmp` (outside any checkout, short for the socket-path limit) with the seed repo, its own `doan` first on PATH and its own runtime. The real stub bytes and the eval's Codex rule join the seed in one baseline commit, so none counts as agent output. The eval's rule (`evalCodexRuleText` in `eval/lib/codex-rule.ts`) is the app's `codexRuleText()` (imported, never copied) plus an `allow` rule for each command it leaves to the person's Codex settings (`init`, `config`, `export`, `handoff`, `remove`, `onboarding`): Codex runs with `approval_policy="never"`, which refuses a command that must leave the sandbox, and the eval measures how agents use the guide, not the person's approvals, so every `doan` command runs outside the sandbox there. Nothing is installed in the real home; real user state (`app/runtime/real-state.ts`) is compared before and after every round. Fake apps (`eval/lib/socket.ts`) answer the socket with an explicit `DOAN_RUNTIME`, so nothing launches; every eval test that runs the CLI also sets an absolute temporary `DOAN_RUNTIME` (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-02). Agents and judges get no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `CODEX_SESSION_ID` or `DOAN_HANDOFF` from the runner (`eval/lib/agent.ts`), so each agent's work signals carry its own session and a runner started inside a Doan conversation passes no key; the fake-agent fixture runner injects a fixed one. They also get none of the user's own instruction files (`isolateUserInstructions` in `eval/lib/agent.ts`): Codex gets a `CODEX_HOME` of its own that links only the user's `auth.json`, `auth.lock` and `auth.json.lock`, because Codex has no setting that skips `$CODEX_HOME/AGENTS.md` and it writes refreshed tokens in place through the link and coordinates through the locks; Codex creates the locks only at a sign-in or refresh, so they are linked whenever `auth.json` is, even dangling. Its `config.toml` is a copy of the user's plus a `[projects."<temp repo>"]` table with `trust_level = "trusted"`: Codex 0.169 loads a project's `.codex/` layer, and so the eval's rule, only for a project trusted there (a `-c` override does not count), and a link would carry that trust into the user's config. The runner adds trust only for the temp repo and preserves existing trust entries in the copied config; that also covers a worktree scenario's second worktree. That home is under `/var/tmp`, and so is a Codex judge's (`outsideCodexSandbox` in `eval/lib/workspace.ts`; the two agents' lanes run side by side, so a Claude run's judge lives while Codex agents run), because Codex's `workspace-write` sandbox lets the agent's shell write its folder, `/tmp` and `$TMPDIR`, and Codex itself reads that home and writes through its links outside the sandbox; the run's rollout lives there and is copied into the run directory before the home is removed with the workspace. Claude gets `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_MDS=1` (read from the Claude Code 2.1.325 bundle, not a documented flag), which keeps user settings and the model, so no scenario seed may carry a project memory file. User-level Claude plugins, skills and hooks still load. The Claude allow rules go into the run's project `.claude/settings.json` from the app's `CLAUDE_ALLOW_RULES` (imported); under `-p` with Bash in `--allowedTools` they cannot show whether a permission prompt is avoided. `shellCli: false` is PATH setup, not an access-control boundary. A `git: false` scenario (`scenario.json`, O5) runs the agent in a folder outside any Git repository: the baseline commit lives in a bare `seed.git` beside it that the runner's Git reads name explicitly (`--git-dir`), and Codex gets `--skip-git-repo-check` because `exec` refuses an untrusted non-Git folder; its sandbox and approval settings are unchanged.
- **CLI**: real rounds require the packaged CLI so agents see what users get; the self-test builds its own.
- **Agent commands** (`eval/lib/agent.ts`, pinned): Claude `stream-json` keeps tool calls in the transcript. Codex runs sandboxed with approvals off, relying on the Codex rule for the app socket, with shell snapshots off and in a non-login shell (`allow_login_shell=false`) so neither a cached environment nor the user's profile can put an installed `doan` ahead of the run-local PATH. Claude ignores every MCP server; Codex can only disable a leftover `doan` server by name, and the runner adds that override only when `codex mcp list --json` reports one, because Codex 0.166 refuses to start on an override for a server it does not define (an unreadable list stops the run). Runs of one agent are sequential.
- **Agent CLI provenance**: each prompt asks the agent to report `command -v doan`; the automatic `agent_cli_provenance` check fails a run whose answer is missing or differs, even when scenario checks pass. For a scenario with a shell CLI, the setup command first opens the call log for append. The expected executable is the run-local wrapper. The report verifies the initial lookup only. (Reference: OpenAI security cookbook on shell snapshots, https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/enterprise/security-cookbook.)
- **CLI call evidence**: the run-local `bin/doan` records argv before launching the packaged CLI and records its exit status on return. Each invocation has its own ID in `doan-calls.jsonl` beside the seed repo. Failure to append blocks execution, and an unreadable, malformed or unfinished log fails the run. The runner retains the log with the round. Checks and the judge read it for agent invocations, including those made from child processes. Seed setup and checker calls go directly to the CLI. The transcript still supplies other shell commands and tool events, and the scenario without a shell CLI checks it for attempts to use another executable.
- **Check contract** (`eval/lib/types.ts`): `checks.ts` default-exports `(ctx) => Check[]`; a throw or an empty or malformed list is a failed `checks_error`. Checks read and run only through `ctx`, and paths leaving the repo throw. `ctx.work()` returns the fake app's v3 exchanges (`work.jsonl`, token-free) with the SHA-256 of each target canvas when the request arrived (null when missing), so `eval/lib/work.ts` judges start-before-edit (an `update` counts as `start`) and done-at-end by content, not by prose; an accepted `update` also carries the target canvas's text when it arrived. `ctx.open()`, `ctx.prototype()` and `ctx.handoff()` do the same for the v4, v6 and v5 exchanges (token-free, with the target canvas's hash when the request arrived; `eval/lib/open.ts`, `prototype.ts` and `handoff.ts`). `ctx.canvasNames()` lists the names the OS reported under `.doan/canvases/` and `.doan/ds/` of `repo` from after the baseline commit until the agent or fixture finished (`eval/lib/canvas-watch.ts`; reports arrive late, macOS's even for writes made just before the watch started, so only those between the reports of a marker file written at each end count), so a file written and removed before the checks still counts. What each scenario requires of them is its `eval/scenarios/<id>/checks.ts`; the fake app (`eval/lib/socket.ts`) answers in the app's check order as far as a fake can reach it; it has no loading project, first run or buddy screen, so v4 and v6 never answer `not_ready` or `busy`, and it renders no window. Agent behavior is judged from invocation evidence (`ctx.calls`, `eval/lib/transcript.ts`), never from guide or tool-result prose, and invocation alone does not prove success. Checker subprocesses get narrow protections (`python -I`, Git hooks and filters off; `eval/lib/context.ts`), not a sandbox.
- **Handoff scenarios** (`scenario.json` `handoff: {canvas}`, `eval/lib/scenario.ts`): the run stands in for a Doan canvas conversation on that canvas. The agent's environment gets a fresh `DOAN_HANDOFF`, and the fake app (`eval/lib/socket.ts`) answers v5 for that key; without the setting it answers v5 as an older app does, with v1 `bad_request`. The stand-in sets only `DOAN_HANDOFF`, not `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION` or `DOAN_CONVERSATION_ID`, so `doan work start` and `done` are delivered there, unlike in a real app conversation (§4 'Suppression'): these scenarios measure the handoff request, not the rest of the conversation environment. The workspace carries only the eval's Codex rule (`evalCodexRuleText()`, 'Isolation' above), not the profile's `doan-handoff.rules`, and Codex runs with approvals off, so the permission path of a real Doan Codex conversation (§7 'Agent authentication' 'Profiles') is checked in the real app, not here.
- **Other-session scenarios** (`scenario.json` `workOthers`, `eval/lib/scenario.ts`): the `others` array the fake app puts on every accepted v3 `start` and `update` answer (never on `done` or errors), checked against the v3 reply schema at load and when the fake app starts; it also starts the fake app. What W9 (no mention of other sessions in the prompt) and W10 (the prompt says to go ahead regardless) require is in their `checks.ts` and `rubric.md`. W5 runs with a fake app that answers without `others` (no information). Re-asking when the list grows and the app-conversation environment are not reproduced.
- **Judge** (`eval/lib/judge.ts`): the other model family scores `rubric.md` (standing exception, AGENTS.md 'Runtime differences'); `--no-judge` skips it. Output `{ verdict: pass|partial|fail, reasons, stalls, layer: guide|output|check|product|none }`; an unparseable answer is `judge_error` and the round continues.
- **Result**: any failed check → `fail`; otherwise the judge's verdict. The judge never turns a failed check into a pass. Known conditions (`eval/lib/known-conditions.ts`) and the summary's classification only label runs in `summary.md` (`eval/lib/record.ts`); they never change results.
- **Self-test**: `node eval/run.ts --self-test` runs every scenario's fixtures with a fake agent and judge; the pass fixture must pass every check and the fail fixture must fail at least one of the scenario's own checks. `<kind>.before.commands.json` runs before the fixture's files are overlaid and `<kind>.commands.json` after, so commands can bracket the edit; a pass fixture's failing command is reported in `problems`.
- **Scenarios are fixed after their first recorded round** so rounds stay comparable; a change to task, seed or intent gets a new id (`W1b`). A checker fix that only corrects false results, or a rubric clarification of existing intent, may land in place with a note on the affected round. A superseded scenario is removed from `eval/scenarios/`; its rows stay in the rounds files under `docs/usability-eval/`. Prompts that mirror app text are compared with the app function in tests, not copied: R8d, R9b, R10 and R11 with `turnPrompt` (`eval/lib/turn-prompt.ts`), as a canvas conversation's turn that may hand off. W11 judges the edited canvas's link groups with the app's `linkGroups` (`eval/lib/prototype-links.ts` imports `app/src/main/prototype/links.ts`, never a copy). W13 judges those names with the app's `classifyDesignChange` (`eval/lib/design-change.ts` imports `app/src/main/designChange.ts`). `eval/lib/answer.ts` holds `finalAnswer` (the final answer from a Claude or Codex transcript) and `withoutCode` (fences and inline code removed); H2b, H5 and R9b use `finalAnswer`, and H2b and R9b also `withoutCode`. R9b reads reply links with the app's `parseReplyAnchor`.
